 (0,5,3,4),(1,3,3,5),(2,4,3,3),(5,0,7,0),(0,6,0,6).             \tag{W4N1}
\]
Its binary-order worth table is
\[
 (0,0,0,4,0,0,6,6,0,4,0,5,6,6,7,12),                          \tag{W4N2}
\]
so all role singleton worths vanish, and attainment by the displayed
common-total generators proves exactness.  Direct staged elimination gives
\[
 q^{\min}=(5,1,5,1),\qquad q^{\max}=(3,3,3,3),\qquad O/T=2/3.   \tag{W4N3}
\]
For Lmin, complementing $w(123)=7$ gives $q_0\le5$, so the first floor is at
most $5/m$, attained by the displayed candidate.  On the face $q_0=5$,
efficiency and $q_1+q_2,q_2+q_3\ge6$ give $q_3,q_1\le1$; the next floor
therefore pins $(q_1,q_2,q_3)=(1,5,1)$.  At $m=5$ the equal levels merge.
For Lmax, $q_1+q_2\ge6$ forces ceiling at least three.  At ceiling three it
pins $q_1=q_2=3$; then $q_2+q_3\ge6$ pins $q_3=3$, and efficiency gives
$q_0=3$.
An independently encoded exact staged LP audits every pinning step.
Canonical expansion gives singleton-normalized exact examples for every
$n=m+3\ge8$.  They remain above the conjectured atomic target, but prove that
four-role contraction cannot be closed by an atomic-size-independent
$3/4$ estimate.  A valid quotient theorem must retain atomic weight,
largest-block weight, or labelled carrier information.  The $m=8$ verifier
also constructs explicit attainment witnesses for all $2^{11}$ clone
coalitions.

The common crossing face of this family has a complete atomic-weight-aware
explanation.  More generally, suppose both weighted selectors lie on
\[
 q_0+q_1=W,\qquad q_0+q_3=W,\qquad T=2W.                       \tag{BWS1}
\]
The face is the interval
\[
 q(t)=(t,W-t,t,W-t),\qquad \ell\le t\le u.                    \tag{BWS2}
\]
Put $P=\max(h_0,h_2)$, $p=\min(h_0,h_2)$ and similarly
$Q=\max(h_1,h_3)$, $q=\min(h_1,h_3)$.  Weighted water filling from below
and above gives, before common-interval clipping,
\[
 t_{\min}=\frac{WP}{P+Q},\qquad t_{\max}=\frac{Wp}{p+q}.       \tag{BWS3}
\]
Clipping cannot increase their distance, and
$O/T=1-|t_{\min}-t_{\max}|/W$.  Orienting the difference gives
\[
 \frac OT\ge\frac1{P+Q}+\frac1{p+q}
 =\frac{n}{(P+Q)(p+q)}
 \ge\frac{4n}{n^2-(n\bmod2)}.                                 \tag{BWS4}
\]
The last step is the sharp integer product bound for two positive summands
of total $n$.  Thus the full conjectured quotient inequality holds on every
balanced common seesaw face.  Equality in the harmonic/product proof occurs
only at atomic weights four and five, so this branch has strict target slack
for $n\ge6$.  An exact audit covers all 91,390 positive weight vectors through
$n=40$ and 4,112,550 rational clipping intervals; the displayed argument is
the proof.

Each flat in (BWS1) is a tight pair-coalition face.  It is a complementary
exact-partition equality only in the special case where the opposite slab
endpoints coincide.  Thus this bound is selection-order arithmetic on the
intersection of two core faces, not a new full exact-game-cone facet.  Its
strict margin for $n\ge6$ also excludes this branch from the six- and
seven-player target-equality orientations, which must remain in the labelled
rooted or mixed facet arms.

The same common-two-flat geometry admits a useful endowed reduction even
without the balance assumption.  Parameterize its core interval by
\[
 q(t)=(e_0+t,e_1+s-t,e_2+t,e_3+s-t),\qquad 0\le t\le s,
 \quad T=E+2s,\quad E=\sum_i e_i.                         \tag{EWS1}
\]
The theorem below assumes $e_i\ge0$ and the stronger face-normalization
condition $e_i=0$ whenever the role multiplicity $h_i$ is one.  Zero
singleton worth of a unit role does not by itself imply this condition: its
minimum on this particular line may exceed its minimum on the full core.
For $r\in\{0,2\}$ and
$f\in\{1,3\}$ put
\[
 \kappa_{rf}=\frac{h_r(e_f+s)-h_f e_r}{h_r+h_f}.          \tag{EWS2}
\]
An envelope comparison of the two rising and two falling per-capita lines
gives
\[
 t_{\min}=\operatorname{clip}_{[0,s]}\max_r\min_f\kappa_{rf},
 \qquad
 t_{\max}=\operatorname{clip}_{[0,s]}\max_f\min_r\kappa_{rf}. \tag{EWS3}
\]
The crossing geometry supplies an order restriction not valid for a generic
$2\times2$ matrix.  A positive gap cannot separate the two diagonals: at a
parameter between them the four
per-capita lines would form a strict cycle
$R_0>F_1>R_2>F_3>R_0$.  Hence the low entries share a row or column.  After
this exclusion, the two max--min values in (EWS3) are exactly the two middle
clipped crossings.  Writing them as
$\kappa_2\le\kappa_3$ gives
\[
 \{t_{\min},t_{\max}\}=\{\kappa_2,\kappa_3\},\qquad
 \frac OT=1-\frac{2(\kappa_3-\kappa_2)}{T}.                \tag{EWS4}
\]
The remaining adjacent middle-gap inequality admits a closed atomic proof.
After
row/column relabelling and, for a column, reversing the parameter, let $p$ be
the low-row weight, $r$ the other row weight, and $q\le b$ the column
weights.

\begin{theorem}[face-normalized endowed common-two-flat bound]\label{thm:endowed-seesaw}
Normalize a positive adjacent middle gap to one.  The minimum possible total,
over all nonnegative endowments with $e_i=0$ on every unit-weight role, is
\[
 S(p,r;q,b)=
 \begin{cases}
  2+p/q+(p+q+b)/r,&p>1,\\
  +\infty,&p=r=1,\\
  2(2r+b+1)/(r-1),&p=1<r,\ q=1,\\
  (q+1)(2r+q+b)/[q(r-1)],&p=1<r,\ q>1.
 \end{cases}                                             \tag{EWS5}
\]
Consequently the atomic bound below holds when the face is normalized in this
way and its total atomic weight is $n\ge6$:
\[
 \frac OT\ge \frac{4n}{n^2-(n\bmod2)}.                   \tag{EWS6}
\]
Equality is possible only at $n=6$ and
$(p,r,q,b)=(2,2,1,1)$, up to the displayed symmetries.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let $\ell,\ell+1$ be the scaled middle crossings and set
$z_f=e_f+s$ for $f\in\{q,b\}$.  Clipping gives $\ell\ge0$ and
$s\ge\ell+1$.  The crossing inequalities and total are
\[
 pz_f-fe_p\le(p+f)\ell,\qquad
 rz_f-fe_r\ge(r+f)(\ell+1),\qquad
 T=e_p+e_r+z_q+z_b.                                    \tag{EWS7}
\]
If $p>1$, add both high-row inequalities and substitute the low-row
$q$-inequality.  This gives exactly
\[
 T\ge2+\frac pq+\frac{p+q+b}{r}
        +\frac{n}{r}(\ell+e_r).                         \tag{EWS8}
\]
Equality is attained at $\ell=e_r=0$ by
$z_f=(r+f)/r$, $e_p=p(r+q)/(qr)$, $s=z_q$; the remaining
endowment is $(b-q)/r$ on role $b$, which is nonunit whenever positive.

If $p=1$, then $e_p=0$.  For $r=1$ the two rows coincide.  Otherwise
(EWS7) implies the sharper
$\ell\ge[r+q+qe_r]/[q(r-1)]$.  Summing the high inequalities and substituting
this bound gives the last line of (EWS5), plus a nonnegative multiple of
$e_r$, when $q>1$.
When $q=1$, also $e_q=0$ and $z_q=s$; the low and high $q$ inequalities give
$\ell\ge(r+1+e_r)/(r-1)$.  Adding the two high lower bounds to the total and
substituting this inequality yields the third line plus a nonnegative
multiple of $e_r$.  Equality in both cases occurs at $e_r=0$; the remaining
explicit choices are recorded in the exact verifier.

Put $F=\lfloor n^2/4\rfloor$ and
$H_n=2/(1-n/F)=2+2n/(F-n)$.  Since (EWS4) gives
$O/T=1-2d/T$, it remains to prove $S\ge H_n$.  For $p>1$, set
$k=p+q+b=n-r$.  Since $q\le b$ and $p\ge2$, Engel's inequality gives
\[
 S-2\ge\frac4{k-2}+\frac{k}{n-k}
 \ge\frac{(2+\sqrt{k})^2}{n-2}\ge\frac{16}{n-2}
 \ge\frac{2n}{F-n}.                                    \tag{EWS9}
\]
The last comparison is $16/(n-2)\ge8/(n-4)$ for even $n$; for odd
$n\ge7$ it is equivalent to $n^2-6n-2\ge0$.  The $p=q=1$ line gives
$S-2=2n/(r-1)\ge2n/(n-4)$.  Finally, if $p=1,q>1$, put
$d=n-2q-2\ge r-1$.  Dropping positive terms and applying Engel again gives
\[
 S-2\ge\frac2q+\frac{2(q+1)}d
 \ge\frac2q+\frac6d
 \ge\frac{(\sqrt2+\sqrt6)^2}{q+d}
 >\frac{14}{n-4}>\frac{2n}{F-n}.                       \tag{EWS10}
\]
Equality in the chain is therefore possible only in the first case at
$n=6,(p,r,q,b)=(2,2,1,1)$.
\end{proof}

The equality role interval has endpoints
$(3,3/2,0,3/2)$ and $(9/2,0,3/2,0)$.  Its lower-envelope role game is exact;
the complementary values
$w(01)=w(03)=9/2$, $w(23)=w(12)=3/2$, and $w(R)=6$ force its
core onto the displayed line, and the zero singleton bounds cut out exactly
that interval.  Canonical expansion at weights $(2,1,2,1)$ is
singleton-normalized and has
\[
 \Lmin=(2,2,1/2,1/2,1/2,1/2),\qquad
 \Lmax=(3/2,3/2,3/2,0,0,3/2).                           \tag{EWS11}
\]
Thus it is precisely six-player equality survivor 1408, the $(4,2)$ versus
$(2,4)$ profile.  At $n=5$, the earlier
$((1,1,1,2),(0,0,0,2),1)$ row gives $3/4$, so the dimensional threshold is
sharp.  At $n=7$ the sharp bound within this face-normalized branch is
$5/8>7/12$, so neither seven-player equality orientation lies here.  A
common pair-slab endpoint is a tight-coalition face, not automatically a
complementary exact-partition equality; the latter requires opposite slab
endpoints to coincide.  The theorem is therefore selection arithmetic on
its stated face and is not a new full exact-cone facet.  The verifier checks
$1{,}950{,}536$ oriented formula rows through $n=60$ and independently solves
the original six-variable unit-gap LP on all $4{,}844$ positive weights
through $n=20$, recovering every formula optimum or infeasible equal-unit-row
case.  It also checks all $64$ exactness minima and both staged selectors of
(EWS11), eight independently formulated role controls, and the previous
$2{,}484{,}224$ bounded endowment rows.  The displayed inequalities, not
those finite checks, are the proof.  A constant
half-overlap shortcut is still false at the abstract seesaw level.

The face-normalization hypothesis cannot be inferred from zero role
singletons.  With weights $(3,1,1,1)$, the lower envelope of
\[
 (0,5,3,4),(0,3,5,4),(2,4,3,3),(5,0,7,0),(0,5,0,7)
\]
has zero singleton worths and weighted selections
$(9/2,3/2,7/2,5/2)$ and $(4,2,3,3)$.  They share the upper endpoints of
pair partitions $01|23$ and $03|12$, but the maximal nonnegative extension
of that line has direction $(1,-1,1,-1)$ and endowment $(1,0,0,1)$.
Thus unit-weight role $3$ has positive effective face endowment.  Canonical
expansion and an independent six-player replay give overlap $11/12>2/3$.
This is a scope control, not a target counterexample: the unrestricted
common-two-flat proof must use the off-face exactness witness attaining the
unit singleton to pay for its positive face endowment.

There is already a sharp payment when the positive unit role is the common
hub.  Write the two tight pair coalitions defining the line as
$C=\{h,a\}$ and $D=\{h,b\}$, and let $o$ be the fourth, opposite role.
For any $q$ on their common face, take an exactness witness $y$ for the
singleton $\{h\}$.  Since $q(C)=w(C)$, $q(D)=w(D)$,
$y_h=w(h)$, and $y_o\geq w(o)$,
\[
 T-y_o+w(h)=y(C)+y(D)\geq q(C)+q(D)=T-q_o+q_h,
\]
and hence
\begin{equation}
 q_h-w(h)\leq q_o-y_o\leq q_o-w(o).                    \tag{EWS20}
\end{equation}
The hub and opposite coordinates move in the same direction on the line.
Thus, if $h$ has unit weight and zero singleton worth, evaluating at their
common minimizing endpoint gives $e_h\leq e_o$.  This common-face hub
payment is a structural exactness lemma.  It does not charge a unit arm:
an arm occurs in only one of the two tight coalitions.  The two arm
orientations therefore remain open in the unrestricted common-two-flat
branch.

The remaining common-face problem is finite in atomic weight.  If all
effective-endowment restrictions are dropped, the same projection as above
has the exact unit-gap cost
\begin{equation}
 S_0(p,r;q,b)=2+\frac pq+\frac{p+q+b}{r},\qquad q\leq b. \tag{EWS21}
\end{equation}
The lower bound is the calculation leading to (EWS8), and its displayed
equality point remains nonnegative without the unit-endowment restrictions.
For $p\geq2$ the preceding integer argument already proves the atomic
target.  If $p=1$, then
\[
 S_0-2=\frac1q+\frac{n-r}{r}.
\]
For $q=1$ this is at least $n/(n-3)$.  For $q\geq2$, $b\geq q$ and Engel's
inequality give
\[
 S_0-2\geq\frac1q+\frac{2q+1}{r}
 \geq\frac{(\sqrt2+\sqrt{2q+1})^2}{2q+r}
 >\frac{13}{n-1}.
\]
Direct comparison with $2n/(\lfloor n^2/4\rfloor-n)$ proves the target for
every $n\geq10$.  Thus off-face exactness is needed on common-two-flat faces
only for $n=6,7,8,9$; the complete subtarget list, up to ordering the side
weights, is
\[
\begin{array}{c|l}
6&(1,2;1,2),(1,3;1,1)\\
7&(1,3;1,2),(1,4;1,1)\\
8&(1,5;1,1)\\
9&(1,6;1,1).
\end{array}                                               \tag{EWS22}
\]
These are relaxed weight rows, not claimed exact-game counterexamples.

One orientation of every displayed row is already excluded structurally.
All have $p=q=1$.  If the unit low role $p$ is the common hub, the hub payment
gives $d=e_r-e_p\geq0$.  Comparing the low and high inequalities in the unit
side column yields
\[
 e_p+\ell\geq\frac{r+1+d}{r-1}.
\]
Adding the two high crossing bounds to the total, and writing
$A=2r+b+1$, then gives
\begin{equation}
 T\geq\frac Ar(e_p+\ell+1)+\frac{r+b+1}{r}d
 \geq\frac{2(2r+b+1)}{r-1}.                            \tag{EWS23}
\end{equation}
This is the safe face-normalized cost.  Hence only the orientations in which
the unit low role is not the common hub remain in the finite common-face
residue; it can be an arm or the role opposite the hub.

For the finite audit we use the standard fairness characterization without
guessing selector strata.  A point $x$ of a compact convex weighted role
core is weighted Lmin exactly when, for every $y$ in the core,
\begin{equation}
 \frac{y_i}{h_i}>\frac{x_i}{h_i}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \exists j:\ \frac{x_j}{h_j}\leq\frac{x_i}{h_i},\quad
 \frac{y_j}{h_j}<\frac{x_j}{h_j}.                       \tag{EWS24}
\end{equation}
Weighted Lmax satisfies the reversed min--max implication.  A violation
gives a strict repeated-payoff lexicographic improvement on a short segment;
the converse follows by taking the direction to the lexicographic optimizer
and its first improved level.  Thus (EWS24), the interval--pair-slab core
description, and the unit-singleton attainment witnesses form a quantified
linear-real encoding with no assumed payoff stratum or active coordinate
face.  A separately formulated rational regression computes $236$ staged
selectors on $118$ exact lower-envelope games and excludes a violating
no-repair direction in all $944$ role tests.  This audits the formulation;
the preceding convex argument is the proof of the characterization.

The finite audit is conclusive.  For every row of (EWS22), it splits the
$12$ possible choices of two common pair-slab endpoint labels and the $16$
coordinatewise overlap sign cells.  All $6\cdot12\cdot16=1{,}152$
quantified linear-real sentences are unsatisfiable, with no unknown result.
The encoding retains a core point attaining every zero unit-role singleton
but relaxes attainment of all other coalition bounds, so its feasible set
contains every exact game under consideration.

\begin{theorem}[atomic common-two-flat quotient bound]\label{thm:common-two-atomic}
Let an exact nonnegative weighted four-role game have positive integer role
weights of total $n\geq6$, with zero singleton worth on every unit-weight
role.  If weighted Lmin and weighted Lmax share endpoints of two independent
complementary pair-sum slabs, then
\[
 \frac OT\geq\frac{4n}{n^2-(n\bmod2)}.
\]
\end{theorem}

For $n\geq10$ this is the human unrestricted cost argument.  At
$n=6,7,8,9$, the same formula closes every weight row outside (EWS22), and
the exact finite audit excludes strict violation on the six remaining rows.
That is exactly the asserted weak inequality.  Together with
Theorem~\ref{thm:common-face-rigidity}, it gives the following stronger
structural branch closure.
\begin{theorem}[atomic weighted four-role quotient bound]
\label{thm:atomic-four-role}
Let an exact nonnegative weighted four-role game have positive integer role
weights of total $n\ge6$, with zero singleton worth on every unit-weight
role.  Then
\[
 \frac OT\ge\frac{4n}{n^2-(n\bmod2)}.
\]
\end{theorem}

If the selectors coincide the claim is immediate.  Otherwise
Theorem~\ref{thm:four-role-confinement} supplies two common pair endpoints and
Theorem~\ref{thm:common-two-atomic} applies.  This completes the weighted
four-role quotient objective.  The finite component inherited from the
common-two theorem is computer-assisted and has not received external
specialist review; equality classification within its six finite rows is
not supplied by the UNSAT audit alone.

On the 228 archived six-player rows where all maximum-overlap roots are
unaffordable on their full menus, every row enters the nongainer-cycle arm.
Of 420 cycles, 169 are separated by the Lmax face and 251 are common-face
modules.  At the row level, 163 expose at least one inductively contractible
common module.  The other 65 are role-irreducible with respect to these
cycles, and every one has an Lmax-tight separator.  This is the exact finite
shadow of Proposition~\ref{prop:common-module-induction}, not evidence that
the separator payments already close the target.  Of the 65 role-irreducible
rows, the cycle itself gives a tight target-reaching direct block on 62.
In each of the remaining three, the unique splitting-row complement contains
every gainer and is therefore outside the proper-packet hypothesis of the
direct identity; replacing at least one gainer by one additional upper
nongainer gives an Lmin-tight block with cardinality base $4/5$.  Every row
has such a target-reaching tight direct block containing a cycle, but over
all 228 rows the cycle itself works on only 216; the other 12 need one
additional nongainer, and full carrier closure still does not select it.
These exact finite counts falsify two tempting cycle-selection rules; they
are not evidence for an unbounded-dimension inequality.
Eighty-five of those rows have an Lmax-tight coalition that splits a carrier
cycle, but complementing one such separator supplies a target-valid direct
identity with nonnegative order residual on only one row.  Thus strictness in
(SCP) is qualitative currency: a general proof must aggregate separator
payments, repair their order residuals, or use weighted contraction.
Of the six fixed seven-player hostile and sharp controls, only the
top-and-bottom-fixed branch has every full-menu maximum-overlap root
unaffordable.  Its roots form a nongainer common-face module and a separate
direct packet reaches $7/12$.  Both sharp equality orientations instead
remain on the affordable rooted-facet arm.

The full Lmax staged packet aggregates the separator currency.  Let $H$ be a
nongainer carrier cycle with $x_i=a$, $z_i=b$, and $L=a-b\ge0$ on $H$, and
let $Q$ be the cumulative Lmax upper set through its stratum.  For each
$i\in H$, sign-coherent pinning gives
\[
 \sum_S\lambda^i_S\mathbf1_S-\sum_{j\in Q}c^i_je_j
   =k_i\mathbf1_N,\qquad \lambda^i,c^i\ge0,\quad c^i_i>0, \tag{ASB1}
\]
with $z$-tight support.  Evaluating at $d=x-z$ and summing gives
\[
 \sum_S\Lambda_S[x(S)-z(S)]
 =LC_H+\sum_{j\in Q\setminus H:d_j\ge0}C_jd_j
   -\sum_{j\in Q:d_j<0}C_j(z_j-x_j),                 \tag{ASB2}
\]
where $C_H=\sum_{j\in H}C_j>0$.  Every bracket on the left is nonnegative,
and every row splitting $H$ is strictly positive by (SCP).

The negative terms have exactly the missing labels.  If $j\in Q$ is a
gainer, then $z_j\ge b$, while maximality of the overlap on $H$ forces
$x_j\le b\le a$.  When $L>0$, each debt edge from $j$ to $H$ is strict in
the Lmin order and is available to the sign-coherent Lmin transport.  It is
not a high--low overlap rectangle, because
\[
 \max(x_j,z_j)=z_j\ge b=\min(x_i,z_i),\qquad i\in H.
\]
Thus the canonical label-currency packing has no such interval.  The split
\[
 z_j-x_j=(z_j-b)+(b-x_j).                            \tag{ASB3}
\]
does not make either term independently countable overlap currency.  Direct
comparison--residual cancellation below avoids matching these pieces
separately: it turns the whole direct arm into one normalized scalar budget
$U_s-L_H$.  The aggregate separator packet may still be needed to prove that
budget for a selected nongainer block or to supply a marked-facet alternative.
If $L=0$, strict Lmin availability may also disappear.  Circuit amplification
makes unnormalized coefficient bounds meaningless.
If the chosen Lmax coalitions never split $H$, their coalition-incidence part
factors through the weighted contraction and canonical block expansion above.
Proposition~\ref{prop:common-module-induction} discharges that branch.  Thus,
in a role-minimal counterexample, the aggregate packet may be restricted to
cycles with at least one splitting row.  The player-unit normalization still
requires weighted pinning coordinates in the current-role selection step;
no general weighted affordability theorem is assumed.

The proper-packet condition itself is automatic away from the tied block.
Suppose $L=a-b>0$ and let $j$ be any gainer.  Maximality of the overlap on
$H$ gives $x_j\le b<a=x_i$ for $i\in H$.  If every $x$-tight coalition
containing $i$ also contained $j$, carrier monotonicity would give
$x_j\ge x_i$, a contradiction.  Hence there is an $x$-tight coalition $C_j$
containing $i$ and omitting $j$; the module property forces $H\subset C_j$.
Thus:

\begin{lemma}[strict-loss proper-packet support]
For a nongainer carrier cycle with $a-b>0$, every gainer is omitted by some
Lmin-tight coalition containing the whole cycle.  In particular the gainer
part of that coalition is proper and its comparison payment is nonnegative.
\end{lemma}

The coalition may depend on the omitted gainer.  The lemma does not prove
the order-residual condition, and when $L=0$ the strict Lmin-order
contradiction disappears.  The cardinality condition, however, is automatic.

\begin{lemma}[carrier-cycle cardinality]
Let $p,g,h$ be the atomic multiplicities of the three sign blocks in the
direct identity, with $n=p+g+h\ge6$, $p,g\ge1$, and let $h\ge2$ because the
nongainer block contains a carrier cycle.  For every proper gainer packet of
atomic multiplicity $0\le s<g$,
\[
 1-\frac{gp}{(g+h)(p+g-s)}
 \ge \frac{4n}{n^2-(n\bmod2)}.                       \tag{CCC}
\]
Equality is possible only at $(n,p,g,h,s)=(6,2,2,2,1)$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The left side decreases with $s$, so take $s=g-1$.  At fixed $n,g$, the
subtracted product
\[
 \frac{g}{g+h}\frac{p}{p+1},\qquad p=n-g-h,
\]
decreases with $h$, so take $h=2$.  With $g=n-p-2$, the remaining base is
$(n+p)/[(n-p)(p+1)]$.  For even $n$, cross multiplication against $4/n$
leaves $n$ times
\[
 4p^2+(4-3n)p+n^2-4n,
\]
whose discriminant is $-7n^2+40n+16<0$ for $n\ge8$; at $n=6$ direct
substitution of $1\le p\le3$ gives the unique zero $p=2$.  For odd $n$, the
cross-product quadratic has leading coefficient $4n$ and discriminant
\[
 -(n+1)(7n^3-47n^2+9n-1)<0\qquad(n\ge7),
\]
because the cubic is positive at seven and increasing thereafter.  This
proves (CCC) and its equality statement.
\end{proof}

Consequently the strict-loss carrier-cycle branch has only one remaining
direct condition, and even that condition has an exact scalar reduction.
Recall from the direct master identity that, for $d=p+g-s$ and
$B=g/d$,
\[
 d(R_x+R_z)=g[U_s-\Delta(J)],\qquad
 z(H\cup J)-x(H\cup J)=\Delta(J)-L_H,                \tag{CB1}
\]
where $L_H=x(H)-z(H)$ and $\Delta(J)=z(J)-x(J)$.
Therefore
\[
 B[z(H\cup J)-x(H\cup J)]+R_x+R_z=B(U_s-L_H).       \tag{CB2}
\]
The gainer labels have disappeared.  Direct substitution in $U_s$ also gives
\[
 U_{s+1}-U_s=\frac{z(G)-x(G)}g\ge0.                 \tag{CB3}
\]
Hence the best proper packet always has $s=g-1$, and any such packet gives
the same combined margin.

\begin{lemma}[comparison--residual cancellation]
Once a sign-compatible nongainer block $H$ and nonempty complement $P$ are
fixed, the direct identity with any proper gainer packet reaches its best
non-low-coordinate margin at $s=g-1$.  It closes that part of the target
exactly when $U_{g-1}\ge L_H$; no separate comparison or residual-sign
condition and no choice among equally sized gainer labels remains.
\end{lemma}

Thus neither contraction, packet properness, target cardinality, nor labelled
residual matching remains open on this arm.  The remaining choice is the
nongainer block $H$ containing a carrier cycle.  The full nongainer
maximum-overlap root block satisfies the scalar budget on all 65
role-irreducible six-player archive rows; the cycle alone does so on 63.
The following aggregate formula remains useful, but a new seven-player
control shows that its proposed universal sign is false.

That candidate statement has a particularly simple aggregate form.  Let $M$ be
the maximum overlap coordinate, let $H$ be all nongainer roots, let $P$ be
the other nongainers, and put
\[
 U=\sum_{i\in P}(M-z_i),\qquad
 R=\sum_{j\in G}(M-x_j),\qquad
 D=z(G)-x(G).
\]
Thus $D$ is also the total nongainer loss.  Substitution of
$x(P)=pM-U+[x(P)-z(P)]$, $x(G)=gM-R$, and
$z(H)=hM$ into $U_{g-1}-L_H$ gives the exact equivalence
\[
 U_{g-1}-L_H=
 \frac{g(g+h)U-gpR-[g+h(p+1)]D}{g(g+h)}.            \tag{RBB}
\]
Consider the lower envelope of the three singleton-normalized allocations
\[
 (6,7,1,0,2,0,1),\quad (7,0,0,0,0,10,0),\quad
 (0,0,0,5,4,6,2).
\]
Its exact selections are
\[
 x=(3,7/2,1,3,2,7/2,1),\qquad
 z=(14/5,16/5,2/5,13/5,16/5,16/5,8/5).
\]
The maximum-overlap roots are $H=\{1,5\}$ at $M=16/5$.  They are
mutually locked, both full rooted menus are unaffordable, and Lmax-tight
coalitions $33$ and $101$ split the carrier cycle.  Thus the block is
role-irreducible, but with $P=\{0,2,3\}$ and $G=\{4,6\}$ one has
\[
 U=19/5,\quad R=17/5,\quad D=9/5,\qquad
 g(g+h)U-gpR-[g+h(p+1)]D=-8.                       \tag{RBB-CE}
\]
Hence the full-root-block RBB conjecture is false.  This does not threaten
the overlap target: the actual ratio is $76/85>7/12$.  The unique
target-reaching tight direct row is coalition $59$; its nongainer part
$\{0,1,3,5\}$ adjoins two labelled nongainers to the cycle, leaves
$P=\{2\}$, and gives base $5/6$ and scalar margin $17/30$.
It is selected by the primitive first-stage Lmin circuit
\[
 \mathbf 1_{59}+e_2+e_6=\mathbf 1_N,                \tag{RBB-C}
\]
where $2,6$ are the first-floor players and $6$ is the omitted gainer.
The unit columns in (RBB-C) are stage-floor columns, not tight singleton game
inequalities.  Hence this is a relative selection-cell circuit, not evidence
for a new full exact-game-cone facet.
The archived exact verifier reconstructs all coalition minima, checks staged
dual pinning relations, and independently rules out every staged improving
direction in rational Z3 arithmetic.  The two optimality encodings share the
reconstructed core and tight menus, but not their multiplier/direction
formulations.

There is nevertheless a dimension-free localization of the missing charge.
Put $d=x-z$.  For a gainer $j$, every positive-loss source $i$ in a
sign-coherent Lmin transport satisfies $x_i\le x_j<M$.  A nongainer root has
$x_i\ge M$, so every such source lies among the nonroot nongainers $P$.
Moreover, with $u_i=M-z_i$ and $r_j=M-x_j$,
\[
 u_i=(M-x_i)+(x_i-z_i)\ge r_j+(x_i-z_i).             \tag{RET}
\]

\begin{lemma}[root-exclusion transport]
Every gainer's sign-coherent Lmin transport draws its positive loss currency
entirely from nonroot nongainers.  Each eligible source pays both its own
loss and the gainer's root-distance reserve as in \textup{(RET)}.
\end{lemma}

The lemma identifies the correct source block, but the known transport
theorem does not bound its joint column congestion.  That congestion, or a
mixed marked-facet alternative, is the quantitative gap.

The scalar objective on candidate supports has an additional exact
simplification.  Let $L=N\setminus G$ be the full nongainer set, choose
$H\subset L$, put $P=L\setminus H$, and retain
$p=|P|$, $g=|G|$, $h=|H|$.  Substitution in the maximal-proper budget gives
\[
 U_{g-1}-L_H=\frac{n}{g+h}z(H)-x(L)+\frac{p-g+1}{g}x(G)
 +\frac{(g-1)(g+h)-hp}{g(g+h)}z(G).                \tag{CRS}
\]

\begin{lemma}[cardinality-ranked support]
Within any family of admissible nongainer blocks of one fixed cardinality
$h$, a block maximizes the direct scalar budget if and only if it maximizes
$z(H)$.
\end{lemma}

Every term in (CRS) except $nz(H)/(g+h)$ is fixed at cardinality $h$, and
that coefficient is positive.  Thus the staged Lmin packet decides which
labelled supports are admissible, while Lmax ranks equal-size candidates by
one ordinary coalition sum.  What remains is an existence/threshold theorem
across cardinalities.  One cannot simply take the globally largest Lmax
coordinates, since their block need not occur as the nongainer trace of a
cycle-containing staged support.

There is a stronger cross-cardinality form.  Put $T=x(N)=z(N)$,
$D=z(G)-x(G)$, and
$Q(P)=\sum_{i\in P}(T/n-z_i)=pT/n-z(P)$.  Total balance reduces (CRS) to
\[
 U_{g-1}-L_H=\frac{pT-nz(P)}{n-p}-\frac{p+1}{g}D
 =\frac{n}{n-p}Q(P)-\frac{p+1}{g}D.                 \tag{EDQ}
\]

\begin{lemma}[excluded-block deficit quota]
A staged-admissible nongainer support $H=L\setminus P$ closes the
maximal-proper scalar budget if and only if
\[
 Q(P)\ge \frac{(n-p)(p+1)}{ng}D.                    \tag{EDQ2}
\]
\end{lemma}

On the carrier-cycle arm, (CCC) says that the cardinality base already
reaches the target, so strict scalar closure is stronger than the target
requires.  Let
$\tau_n=n/\lfloor n^2/4\rfloor$ and retain the surplus of the carrier
cardinality base.  Since the maximal-proper weight is $g/(p+1)$, (EDQ)
gives the exact nonlow target reserve
\begin{equation}
 \tag{EDQ5}
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathcal R_{\tau_n}(P)
 &=\frac{g}{p+1}(U_{g-1}-L_H)
 +\left(1-\frac{gp}{(n-p)(p+1)}-\tau_n\right)T\\
 &=(1-\tau_n)T-D-\frac{gn}{(n-p)(p+1)}z(P).
 \end{aligned}
\end{equation}

\begin{lemma}[target-reserve quota]
If $\mathcal R_{\tau_n}(P)\ge0$, then the maximal-proper direct identity
reaches the target without requiring the strict scalar quota (EDQ2).
\end{lemma}

Indeed, the first line is the normalized comparison--residual contribution
plus cardinality slack; substitute $Q(P)=pT/n-z(P)$ to obtain the second.
The omitted low-Lmax payment is nonnegative.  Whenever the cardinality base
is at least the target---in particular, under the carrier-cycle hypotheses of
(CCC)---this criterion is no stronger than (EDQ2), and is strictly weaker
when the base surplus is positive.  Without that base hypothesis the two
tests need not be ordered.  The lemma still requires selection of an
admissible support and the proved comparison premise.

This subsumes cardinality ranking and removes the opaque comparison across
block sizes: the excluded nongainers must carry a specified Lmax deficit
below the common mean.  On a fixed labelled selection cell one can decide
the direct carrier arm by scanning the complete staged Lmin support rows
containing the cycle and testing (EDQ2) on their excluded blocks.  This is a
relative support-menu algorithm, not a full exact-game-cone facet or a
polynomial full-cone enumeration theorem.

There is also an exact marginal law.  If
$P_{p+1}=P_p\mathbin{\dot\cup}\{i\}$ and $E_p$ is the quota surplus in
(EDQ2), then
\[
 E_{p+1}-E_p=T/n-z_i-\frac{n-2p-2}{ng}D.             \tag{EDQ4}
\]
The required marginal deficit decreases linearly with $p$ and becomes
nonpositive after the midpoint.  This explains why some six-player cells
require an excluded pair even though neither available singleton closes,
whereas adding above-mean Lmax mass destroys the larger excluded blocks in
the seven-player control.  The admissible support family need not itself be
nested.

The rooted and direct currencies admit an exact common interface.  Fix a
root $r$ and a root-containing fractional packing $u$ supported on
$x$-tight coalitions.  Put
\[
 \delta=\sum_Su_S-1,\qquad
 q_i=1-\sum_{S\ni i}u_S\quad(i\ne r),               \tag{RCA1}
\]
and assume the offroot loads are at most one.  For any
$A\subseteq N\setminus\{r\}$ set $P_S=A\setminus S$.  Coordinate counting
and core feasibility give
\begin{align}
 \sum_Su_Sz(P_S)&=\delta z(A)+\sum_{i\in A}q_i z_i,\notag\\
 \sum_Su_S|P_S|&=\delta |A|+\sum_{i\in A}q_i,       \tag{RCA2}\\
 \sum_{i\ne r}q_i(x_i-z_i)&\ge
             \delta(x_r-z_r).                       \tag{RCA3}
\end{align}
Indeed, player $i\in A$ is absent with packing mass
$(1+\delta)-(1-q_i)=\delta+q_i$.  Also
$z(S)-x(S)\ge0$ on every supported $x$-tight coalition; summing and using
$x(N)=z(N)$ proves (RCA3).  Thus these identities require no facet
classification.

There is a fixed-cardinality reserve consequence.  Suppose all supported
rows contain the selected nongainer carrier, are proper on the gainer side,
and have $|P_S|=p$.  Proper $x$-tightness supplies the comparison premise,
and with $c_p=gn/[(n-p)(p+1)]$,
\[
 \sum_Su_S\mathcal R_{\tau_n}(P_S)
 =(1+\delta)((1-\tau_n)T-D)
  -c_p\left(\delta z(A)+\sum_{i\in A}q_i z_i\right). \tag{RCA4}
\]
Nonnegativity of the right side therefore selects a target-reaching row.
This is a literal coupling of rooted deficit mass to excluded-block reserve,
but it is not yet the universal handover: a marked price optimizer need not
be supported on proper rows of one complement cardinality, and a negative
average does not imply an affordable facet.

The cost of repairing an improper packing row can nevertheless be separated
exactly into its two currencies.  To avoid confusing tight-pair labels with
payoff gainers, put $\Gamma_r=G$ and $B_r=B$ in the notation of (LPC), and write
$\pi_r(S)=w(S\setminus\{r\})$ for the labelled-price coefficient of a
rooted row.  For any two rooted rows $S,C$,
\[
 \pi_r(S)-\pi_r(C)
 =K\bigl(|(C\setminus\{r\})\cap\Gamma_r|-|(S\setminus\{r\})\cap\Gamma_r|\bigr)
  +|(S\setminus\{r\})\cap B_r|-|(C\setminus\{r\})\cap B_r|. \tag{PXC1}
\]
This is the exact labelled-price charge; it depends on tight rooted-pair
labels, not merely on payoff strata.  On the reserve side, let
$P^+=P\mathbin{\dot\cup}\{i\}$, $p=|P|\le n-2$, and keep the selected gainer and
nongainer strata fixed.  Direct subtraction in (EDQ5) gives
\[
 \mathcal R_{\tau_n}(P^+)-\mathcal R_{\tau_n}(P)
 =\frac{gn\{(n-2p-2)z(P)-(n-p)(p+1)z_i\}}
 {(n-p)(p+1)(n-p-1)(p+2)}.                         \tag{PXC2}
\]
Deleting payoff gainers from a coalition does not change its excluded
nongainer block and hence costs no reserve; deleting an additional
nongainer changes reserve by exactly (PXC2).  Thus (PXC1)--(PXC2) reduce
properization to an explicit price-versus-reserve charge, but do not prove
that a charged replacement row is present in the tight support.

In particular, on the pair-free branch $\Gamma_r=\varnothing$ and
$\pi_r(S)=K+|S\setminus\{r\}|$.  Hence every deletion has labelled-price
charge exactly equal to its incidence size.  Suppose a co-singleton tight
row $S=N\setminus\{j\}$ omits a nongainer $j$, has
$\mathcal R_{\tau_n}(\{j\})\ge0$, and admits a carrier-preserving proper
tight subset $C$.  If $S\setminus C$ contains only payoff gainers, then $C$
has the same reserve and closes the direct target.  If it additionally
contains exactly one nongainer $i$, it closes if and only if the right side
of (PXC2), added to $\mathcal R_{\tau_n}(\{j\})$, is nonnegative.  At
$n=6,p=1$ the increment is
\[
 \frac{g}{10}\bigl(z_j-5z_i\bigr).                 \tag{PXC3}
\]
This conditional properization lemma is dimension-free before the final
specialization.  Its hypotheses make explicit the two facts still missing
from a universal theorem: selection of the tight subset and payment of any
additional omitted nongainer.

Exactness removes the apparent crossing obstruction completely.  More
generally, let $S,C$ be tight at one core point $x$ and suppose $S\cup C=N$.
Put $I=S\cap C$.  Core feasibility gives
$v(S)+v(C)=v(N)+x(I)\ge v(N)+v(I)$.  By exactness, however, there is a core
point $y$ with $y(I)=v(I)$, and then
$v(S)+v(C)\le y(S)+y(C)=v(N)+v(I)$.  Hence equality holds throughout:
\[
 v(S)+v(C)=v(N)+v(I),\qquad x(I)=v(I),\qquad
 z(I)-x(I)=[z(S)-x(S)]+[z(C)-x(C)]\ge0.             \tag{PXC4}
\]
Thus tight sets covering the grand coalition intersect tightly in an exact
game.  In particular, intersecting any proper carrier row with a tight
co-singleton preserves tightness, the carrier, and properness, and
automatically supplies the comparison premise.  This elementary exact-game
uncrossing fact is stated within the standard exact-cone framework
\citep{CsokaHeringsKoczy2011,StudenyKratochvil2022}; no novelty claim is made
for it.  After this reduction the only remaining charge is the telescoped
target-reserve change in (PXC2), not a comparison-slack defect.

That reserve has a useful ordered-cut lower certificate.  Let $B\subseteq H$
be a retained block of $b$ nongainers with $z_i=M$ for $i\in B$, put
$K=H\setminus B$, suppose $z_i\le M^-\le M$ for every $i\in P$, and put
$p=|P|$, $g=|G|$, and
\[
 c_p=\frac{gn}{(n-p)(p+1)}.
\]
Since $D=T-z(H)-z(P)-x(G)$, the target reserve admits the exact rewrite
\[
 \mathcal R_\tau(P)
 =-\tau T+x(G)+z(H)+(1-c_p)z(P).                    \tag{PXC5}
\]
The retained block gives $z(H)=bM+z(K)$.  If $c_p\le1$, the final term is
nonnegative; if $c_p>1$, then $z(P)\le pM^-$.  Hence
\[
 \mathcal R_\tau(P)
 \ge bM+z(K)+x(G)-\tau T-p(c_p-1)_+M^-.            \tag{PXC6}
\]
If $M^-$ bounds every nongainer outside $B$, dropping $z(K)\ge0$ gives the
root-block-only corollary
\[
 \mathcal R_\tau(P)
 \ge bM+x(G)-\tau T-p(c_p-1)_+M^-.                 \tag{PXC7}
\]
Relative to the still coarser substitution $M^-=M$, the next Lmax stratum
supplies the exact additional credit
\[
 p(c_p-1)_+(M-M^-).                                \tag{PXC8}
\]
This lemma is dimension-free algebra, but nonnegativity of its right-hand
side is a selection obligation, not a theorem for every carrier row.  The
importance of (PXC6)--(PXC8) is that they retain both sides of the ordered
cut: the additional high-$z$ nongainers kept in $K$ and the low-$z$ price of
the excluded block $P$.  This is the same colour of information exposed by
the sparse Lmax pinning packet.

The complementary Lmin-side certificate comes from the comparison payment
itself.  Let $I$ be the selected tight row, let
$A=G\setminus I$ be its omitted gainers, put
$\ell(P)=x(P)-z(P)$ and $\Delta(A)=z(A)-x(A)$.  Total balance gives
\[
 \sigma_I:=z(I)-x(I)=\ell(P)-\Delta(A)\ge0.        \tag{PXC9}
\]
Substituting $z(P)=x(P)-\ell(P)$ in the target reserve therefore yields
\[
 \mathcal R_\tau(P)
 =(1-\tau)T-D-c_p x(P)+c_p\Delta(A)+c_p\sigma_I,  \tag{PXC10}
\]
and in particular
\[
 \mathcal R_\tau(P)
 \ge (1-\tau)T-D-c_p x(P)+c_p\Delta(A).            \tag{PXC11}
\]
Thus the same row has two independently useful lower certificates: (PXC6)
prices its Lmax ordered cut, while (PXC11) prices the Lmin mass of its
excluded block against the gain deliberately omitted by properness.  More
importantly, (PXC10) says that the entire difference between the latter
certificate and the true reserve is the normalized Lmax comparison payment;
there is no third residual currency.  These
are not independent proof systems---both are algebraic consequences of the
same selected tight row---but they expose the two currencies that a genuine
Lmin/Lmax gluing theorem must couple.

Their exact coupling is a minimum-residual identity.  Take
$M^-=\max_{i\in P}z_i$ in (PXC6), and define
\[
 E_I^x=c_p\sigma_I,\qquad
 E_I^z=
 \begin{cases}
 (1-c_p)z(P),&c_p\le1,\\
 (c_p-1)[pM^--z(P)],&c_p\ge1.
 \end{cases}
\]
Then (PXC5), (PXC6), and (PXC10) give, exactly,
\[
 \max\{\mathrm{PXC6}(I),\mathrm{PXC11}(I)\}
 =\mathcal R_\tau(P)-\min\{E_I^z,E_I^x\}.          \tag{PXC12}
\]
Both errors are nonnegative.  Consequently the selected-row max-certificate
is equivalent to the single hybrid inequality
$\mathcal R_\tau(P)\ge\min\{E_I^z,E_I^x\}$.  This is stronger than mere
nonnegativity of the reserve and is the precise load-bearing statement for a
minimum-deletion exchange proof.  It also prevents a proof from silently
combining two error terms on a fictitious row; the outer existence
quantifier is handled separately below.

For the corrected disjunction, however, the two optimizations themselves do
decouple.  Put $K_\tau=(1-\tau)T-D=O-\tau T$ and define the two row prices
\[
 Z_I=z(P)+(c_p-1)_+pM^-,\qquad
 X_I=c_p[z(P)+\sigma_I]=c_p[x(P)-\Delta(A)].
\]
Direct substitution gives
\[
 \mathrm{PXC6}(I)=K_\tau-Z_I,\qquad
 \mathrm{PXC11}(I)=K_\tau-X_I.                    \tag{PXC13}
\]
Consequently, for any nonempty admissible row family $\mathcal F$, if
$Z_*:=\min_{I\in\mathcal F}Z_I$ and
$X_*:=\min_{I\in\mathcal F}X_I$, then
\[
 \max_{I\in\mathcal F}\max\{\mathrm{PXC6}(I),\mathrm{PXC11}(I)\}
 =K_\tau-\min\{Z_*,X_*\}.                          \tag{PXC14}
\]
Thus a row closes precisely when the cheaper of the independently optimized
Lmax cut price and Lmin omission price is at most the global overlap surplus.
One does not add certificates from different rows: a minimizer of the cheaper
price supplies the actual selected row.  This turns the sufficient
max-certificate selection step into the scalar hybrid-price inequality
$\min\{Z_*,X_*\}\le K_\tau$ and gives a direct interface to the two LP
duals.

At fixed deletion cardinality these two prices are ordinary maximum-weight
row problems.  Let $L=N\setminus G$, let $\mathcal F_p$ be the
carrier-containing proper $x$-tight rows with $|L\setminus I|=p$, and put
$m_I=\max_{i\in L\setminus I}z_i$.  Define
\[
 S_z(I)=z(I\cap L)-(c_p-1)_+pm_I,\qquad
 S_x(I)=x(I\cap L)-\Delta(I\cap G).
\]
Then direct complementation gives
\[
 Z_*=z(L)-\max_{I\in\mathcal F_p}S_z(I),\qquad
 X_*=c_p\left[x(L)-D-max_{I\in\mathcal F_p}S_x(I)\right]. \tag{PXC15}
\]
For $c_p\le1$ the Lmax score is simply retained nongainer $z$-mass.  For
$c_p>1$, stratifying by the highest excluded Lmax level makes the additional
term constant on each stratum, so (PXC15) is still a finite sequence of
linear maximum-weight row problems.  This proves an exact algorithm on an
explicit tight menu and exposes two support-function duals.  Their extreme
inequalities are relative selection-cell facets; promotion to facets of the
full exact-game cone still requires the semi-balanced indecomposability test.
Scanning every nonempty cardinality class gives
\[
 Z^{\rm all}_*=\min_p Z_{*,p},\qquad
 X^{\rm all}_*=\min_p X_{*,p}.                     \tag{PXC16}
\]
By (PXC14), some admissible carrier row has a nonnegative two-colour
max-certificate if and only if
$\min\{Z^{\rm all}_*,X^{\rm all}_*\}\le K_\tau$.  Minimum deletion is a
particularly rigid finite selector, but it is only a sufficient subfamily;
the dimension-free proof should use the full scan and may close at a larger
deletion size.

There is a dimension-free equality consequence.  If $K_\tau=0$, then every
$Z_I,X_I$ is nonnegative, and
\[
 \max\{\mathrm{PXC6}(I),\mathrm{PXC11}(I)\}\ge0
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad z(P)=0.             \tag{PXC17}
\]
Indeed either closing price must vanish, hence $z(P)=0$.  Conversely,
nonnegativity of $z$ makes $z(P)=0$ imply $M^-=0$, so $Z_I=0$ and PXC6 is
exactly zero.  Thus equality selection on the carrier-direct arm reduces to
excluding only zero-Lmax nongainers; no gainer-label enumeration remains.
Combined with (CCC), this recovers the existing unique six-player
carrier-direct equality parameters, while leaving the rooted seven-player
equality arm separate.

There is an exact exchange law along every co-singleton-generated deletion
chain.  Let $I$ be an admissible row with excluded nongainer block $P$ of
size $p$, let $j\in (I\cap L)$ lie outside the retained carrier, and suppose
$N\setminus\{j\}$ is $x$-tight.  Since
$I\cup(N\setminus\{j\})=N$, (PXC4) makes
$J=I\setminus\{j\}$ tight; it remains admissible, has excluded block
$P\cup\{j\}$, and has the same omitted-gainer block.  Put
\[
 m=\max_{i\in P}z_i,\qquad m'=\max\{m,z_j\},\qquad
 a_p=p(c_p-1)_+.
\]
Then direct subtraction in the two price formulas gives
\begin{align}
 X_J-X_I
  &=(c_{p+1}-c_p)[z(P)+\sigma_I]+c_{p+1}x_j,\notag\\
 Z_J-Z_I
  &=z_j+a_{p+1}m'-a_pm,                             \tag{PXC18}\\
 \frac{c_{p+1}}{c_p}-1
  &=\frac{2p+2-n}{(p+2)(n-p-1)}.                   \tag{PXC19}
\end{align}
Thus an Lmin-colour deletion cannot lower price once $2p+2\ge n$.
An Lmax-colour deletion lowers price exactly when
$z_j<a_pm-a_{p+1}m'$.  Before the midpoint, these are explicit low-payoff
thresholds; after the coefficient turns upward, nonnegativity prevents the
corresponding Lmin improvement.  The lemma localizes every improving step on
an available tight-co-singleton chain.  It does not assert that every two
admissible rows lie on one such chain, or that the required co-singleton is
tight.

The contrapositive gives the exact local obstruction needed by the separator
arm.  Choose a global $X$-price minimizer $I_X$ and a global $Z$-price
minimizer $I_Z$ (they need not coincide).  For every co-singleton deletion
available from the relevant row, (PXC18) and optimality give the following,
with $p,P,m$ interpreted for the minimizer in the corresponding line:
\begin{align}
 x_j&\ge
 \frac{n-2p-2}{(n-p)(p+1)}[z(P)+\sigma_{I_X}]
 &&(2p+2<n),\notag\\
 z_j+a_{p+1}\max\{m,z_j\}&\ge a_pm.                \tag{PXC20}
\end{align}
In particular, when $z_j\le m$ the second inequality is
$z_j\ge(a_p-a_{p+1})m$.  Repeated strict improvement terminates because each
deletion increases $p$, so each colour has a finite co-singleton descent to a
row satisfying (PXC20).  Therefore simultaneous failure of the two global
price tests produces two explicit exchange-stable rows and these positive
player-price inequalities.  What remains is to aggregate those payments by
the staged Lmax separator, or to show that a shortage of available tight
co-singletons exposes one of the rooted facet packets.  This is a reduction
of the missing theorem, not its proof.

The $X$-stability payment already lies in an elementary balanced class.
Let $R_X$ be the retained noncarrier nongainers whose co-singletons
$C_j=N\setminus\{j\}$ are $x$-tight at an $X$-price minimizer, and assume
$2p+2<n$.  Total balance and $x$-tightness give
$z(C_j)-x(C_j)=x_j-z_j$.  Summing the first line of (PXC20) therefore yields
\[
 z(R_X)+\sum_{j\in R_X}[z(C_j)-x(C_j)]
 \ge |R_X|\frac{n-2p-2}{(n-p)(p+1)}
       [z(P)+\sigma_{I_X}].                         \tag{PXC21}
\]
The underlying incidence packet is simply
\[
 \sum_{j\in R_X}\one_{C_j}+\sum_{j\in R_X}e_j
   =|R_X|\one_N.
\]
Thus each required exchange payment splits exactly into retained Lmax mass
and comparison slack.  The aggregate is the decomposable sum of the balanced
partition identities
$\one_{N\setminus\{j\}}+e_j=\one_N$; it is not the distinct indecomposable
complement-star facet family proved earlier, and it cannot itself be a new
indecomposable exact-cone facet.
Consequently any genuinely new facet needed here must enter through the
remaining supply-or-sufficiency obstruction, not through (PXC21) itself.
Inequality (PXC21) does not prove either that enough co-singletons are tight
or that the available atoms have sufficient total weight to close the target.

For the direct-versus-separator theorem, the exact reserve price is weaker
than either certificate price and should be optimized first.  Define
\[
 Y_I=c_pz(P).
\]
Then (EDQ5) and (PXC13) give
\[
 \mathcal R_\tau(P)=K_\tau-Y_I,\qquad
 Y_I\le Z_I,\qquad Y_I\le X_I.                   \tag{PXC22}
\]
The second inequality uses $\sigma_I\ge0$.  For the first, if $c_p\le1$
then $Z_I=z(P)\ge c_pz(P)$; if $c_p\ge1$, then
$pm_I\ge z(P)$ gives
$Z_I=z(P)+(c_p-1)pm_I\ge c_pz(P)$.
At fixed cardinality and over the full family, therefore,
\[
 \begin{split}
 Y_{*,p}&=c_p\left[z(L)-
       \max_{I\in\mathcal F_p}z(I\cap L)\right],\\
 Y_*^{\rm all}&=\min_pY_{*,p}
       \le\min\{Z_*^{\rm all},X_*^{\rm all}\},\\
 \text{a direct row closes}&\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
       Y_*^{\rm all}\le K_\tau.
 \end{split}                                       \tag{PXC23}
\]
Thus failure of the direct price automatically implies failure of both
certificate colours, but the converse need not hold.  The actual hard branch
for the direct-or-separator theorem is $Y_*^{\rm all}>K_\tau$; (PXC16) is a
stronger sufficient certificate conjecture, not the necessary direct target.
Formula (PXC23) is a pure Lmax support function evaluated on the complete
Lmin-tight menu, giving the cleanest interface to the sparse Lmax dual.

The co-singleton exchange becomes correspondingly simpler:
\[
 Y_J-Y_I=(c_{p+1}-c_p)z(P)+c_{p+1}z_j.             \tag{PXC24}
\]
If $I_Y$ is a global direct-price minimizer with $2p+2<n$, every available
deletion therefore satisfies, with
$R_Y$ denoting all such retained nongainers,
\[
 z_j\ge\frac{n-2p-2}{(n-p)(p+1)}z(P),\qquad
 z(R_Y)\ge |R_Y|\frac{n-2p-2}{(n-p)(p+1)}z(P).     \tag{PXC25}
\]
Unlike (PXC21), this payment is entirely retained Lmax mass and hence enters
overlap directly.  What remains is still a supply-or-sufficiency theorem:
neither (PXC23) nor (PXC25) proves that enough co-singleton deletions exist or
that their aggregate mass forces $Y_*^{\rm all}\le K_\tau$.

When the co-singletons do exist, their deletions are jointly compatible, not
merely pairwise available.  Let $R$ be any set of retained noncarrier
nongainers of an admissible row $I$ such that every
$N\setminus\{j\}$, $j\in R$, is $x$-tight.  Repeatedly applying (PXC4) gives
\[
 I\setminus Q\text{ is $x$-tight and admissible for every }Q\subseteq R.
                                                               \tag{PXC26}
\]
Indeed, after any earlier deletions the current row still contains the next
$j$, so its union with $N\setminus\{j\}$ is again $N$.  Thus the available
co-singletons generate a full Boolean deletion cube.  If
$z_{(1)}\le\cdots\le z_{(|R|)}$ are their Lmax payoffs, then for every
$0\le q\le|R|$,
\[
 \min_{\substack{Q\subseteq R\\|Q|=q}}Y_{I\setminus Q}
  =c_{p+q}\left[z(P)+\sum_{t=1}^qz_{(t)}\right],
 \qquad
 \min_{Q\subseteq R}Y_{I\setminus Q}
  =\min_{0\le q\le|R|}
    c_{p+q}\left[z(P)+\sum_{t=1}^qz_{(t)}\right].   \tag{PXC27}
\]
For fixed $q$ the coefficient is constant, so the exchange argument is just
the order-statistic choice of the $q$ cheapest players.  This proves an exact
one-dimensional direct-price algorithm on every co-singleton cube.  It does
not show that an arbitrary admissible row has a cube large enough to reach
the coefficient midpoint.  The six-player full-menu replay checks all $157$
available cubes and $314$ vertices/order-statistic layers; every archived
cube has dimension one, so higher-dimensional coverage rests on the human
uncrossing proof rather than that finite control.

The shortage side also has an exact geometric meaning.  For each player put
\[
 u_j:=T-v(N\setminus\{j\}).
\]
Every core allocation $y$ satisfies $y_j\le u_j$.  Exactness at
$N\setminus\{j\}$ supplies a core allocation $w^j$ with
$w^j(N\setminus\{j\})=v(N\setminus\{j\})$, and therefore
\[
 u_j=\max_{y\in\core(v)}y_j=w^j_j,
 \qquad
 N\setminus\{j\}\text{ is $x$-tight}
 \Longleftrightarrow x_j=u_j.                       \tag{PXC28}
\]
Consequently every unavailable co-singleton direction has the positive upper
slack
\[
 \kappa_j:=u_j-x_j
   =x(N\setminus\{j\})-v(N\setminus\{j\})>0
\]
and comes with an exact endpoint witness $w^j$.  Thus cube shortage is not an
unstructured support failure: it is a collection of coordinate-exposing
exactness witnesses.  Formula (PXC28) does not show that the separately
chosen witnesses $w^j$ can be aggregated with common normalized weights, nor
that their supports form an indecomposable semi-balanced packet.  That
aggregation-or-separator step remains the missing theorem.

On the six-player full tight menus the exact replay checks (PXC28) in $497$
retained noncarrier nongainer occurrences: $157$ are upper-pinned and $340$
have positive upper slack.  The extracted staged supports give $7$ pinned and
$338$ slack occurrences among $345$ checks.  These multiplicity counts are
finite diagnostics; exactness proves (PXC28) in every dimension.  Every one
of the $340$ full-menu shortage sets (and all $338$ extracted-support sets)
is a singleton, so exactness gives a common endpoint witness in every
archived instance and no positive (PXC30) separator gap appears.  Of the
$340$ full-menu slack occurrences, $300$ lie in the Lmax top stratum and
$40$ lie below it.  Thus this archive checks the one-direction endpoint arm
but supplies no nontrivial finite test of multi-endpoint compatibility.

The endpoint witnesses admit a genuine aggregate alternative.  For a
nonempty set $R$ of missing directions define the co-singleton slack
$s_j(y):=y(N\setminus\{j\})-v(N\setminus\{j\})=u_j-y_j$ and
\[
 \epsilon_R:=\min_{y\in\core(v)}\max_{j\in R}s_j(y).
\]
Compact minimax duality gives
\[
 \epsilon_R
  =\max_{\beta\in\Delta(R)}\min_{y\in\core(v)}
       \sum_{j\in R}\beta_js_j(y).                   \tag{PXC29}
\]
In particular, $\epsilon_R=0$ if and only if the endpoint faces
$\{y:y_j=u_j\}$, $j\in R$, have a common core point.  If their intersection
is empty, then $\epsilon_R>0$ and an optimizing $\beta$, together with core
LP duality, supplies nonnegative coalition weights $\lambda_S$ and a free
scalar $\gamma$ such that
\[
 \sum_S\lambda_S\one_S+\gamma\one_N
   =\sum_{j\in R}\beta_j\one_{N\setminus\{j\}},
 \qquad
 \sum_S\lambda_Sv(S)+\gamma T
  -\sum_{j\in R}\beta_jv(N\setminus\{j\})
   =\epsilon_R>0.                                    \tag{PXC30}
\]
For fixed $\beta$ an extreme dual needs at most $n$ positive coalition
weights.  Complementary slackness makes those coalitions tight at a core
point minimizing the weighted endpoint slack.  Thus missing cube directions
either aggregate into one common endpoint witness or expose a sparse exact
core separator with a positive normalized payment; separate exactness
witnesses are no longer the endpoint of the argument.

This is not yet the full sparse-Lmax arm of the carrier theorem.  The
coalitions in (PXC30) are tight at the minimizing core point and need not be
tight at $z$ or belong to the chosen first-stage Lmax over-cover.  The
remaining gluing obligation is to transfer the positive payment to that
over-cover, or to show that failure of transfer yields one of the already
classified rooted or mixed-witness packets.  Consequently (PXC29)--(PXC30)
prove an endpoint-face supply-or-separator theorem, not the target overlap
inequality.

On the common-witness arm there is an exact projection theorem parallel to
the rooted mixed-witness projection.  Suppose a nonempty proper $R$ has a common endpoint
witness, put $U_R=\sum_{j\in R}u_j$, and let ${\cal D}$ be any family of
selection-tagged requirements $a_d\le w(S_d)$.  For $\rho_d\ge0$ write
$p_i(\rho)=\sum_{d:i\in S_d}\rho_d$.  A nonnegative total-$T$ vector $w$
with $w_j=u_j$ for every $j\in R$ and satisfying all the requirements exists
if and only if
\[
 \sum_d\rho_da_d
 \le \sum_{j\in R}p_j(\rho)u_j
       +(T-U_R)\max_{i\notin R}p_i(\rho)              \tag{PXC31}
\]
for every $\rho\ge0$.  Necessity is weighted summation.  Sufficiency follows
by separating from the down-set generated by the endpoint-fixed simplex;
the right side is exactly its support function.

After normalizing $p_i\le1$ for $i\notin R$, extreme packings use at most
$n-|R|$ positive requirement columns, apart from rays supported wholly on
$R$.  With $q_i=1-p_i$ off $R$ and $\delta_j=p_j-1$ on $R$, their incidence
completion is
\[
 \sum_d\rho_d\one_{S_d}+\sum_{i\notin R}q_ie_i
       -\sum_{j\in R}\delta_je_j=\one_N.             \tag{PXC32}
\]
For purely $x$-tagged requirements, (PXC31) is equivalently
\[
 \sum_{j\in R}\delta_j(x_j-u_j)
       \le\sum_{i\notin R}q_ix_i.                    \tag{PXC33}
\]
This exposes the exact sign of the shortage currency: positive root excess
$\delta_j$ is helped by $x_j-u_j=-\kappa_j$, whereas negative root excess
must be paid.  Equations (PXC31)--(PXC33) give a finite, linear-support
algorithm for the common-endpoint mixed-packet projection.  They do not
prove that its branch optimum reaches the overlap target.  Moreover the
multi-exception incidence system (PXC32) must pass the full
Studen\'y--Kratochv\'il indecomposability test before any factor is called an
exact-cone facet.

The endpoint alternative couples directly to the sparse Lmax over-cover.
Let $(\lambda,\eta,\alpha)$ satisfy (OC), so $\alpha$ is a probability on
the Lmax top block and $\alpha\cdot y\ge M$ for every core point $y$.  If a
nonempty proper $R$ has a common endpoint witness, set
$a_R:=\max_{i\notin R}\alpha_i$.  Evaluating the support function of its
endpoint-fixed simplex gives
\[
 M\le \sum_{j\in R}\alpha_ju_j+(T-U_R)a_R
   =a_RT+\sum_{j\in R}(\alpha_j-a_R)u_j.             \tag{PXC34}
\]
In particular, if $\theta=\max_i\alpha_i$, then
\[
 M\le \theta T-\sum_{j\in R}(\theta-\alpha_j)u_j.   \tag{PXC35}
\]
Thus fixing an endpoint outside the maximally loaded over-cover coordinates
strictly reduces the crude dispersion ceiling $M\le\theta T$.

The positive-separator arm has a priced version of the same inequality.  Let
$\beta$ attain (PXC29), put $d_j:=\alpha_j-a_R$, and suppose
$d_j\ge0$ on $R$.  With
\[
 q_R:=\min_{\substack{j\in R\\\beta_j>0}}
              \frac{d_j}{\beta_j},
\]
choose a core point $y^*$ minimizing the $\beta$-weighted endpoint slack.
Writing $s_j=u_j-y^*_j$ and placing the remaining mass on a largest
off-$R$ over-cover coordinate yields
\[
 M\le\alpha\cdot y^*
 \le a_RT+\sum_{j\in R}d_ju_j-q_R\epsilon_R.         \tag{PXC36}
\]
Indeed, the same mass calculation before the final inequality gives
$a_RT+\sum_jd_ju_j-\sum_jd_js_j$, and
$\sum_jd_js_j\ge q_R\sum_j\beta_js_j=q_R\epsilon_R$.
This is a dimension-free coupling between the sparse Lmax dual and the
endpoint-face Farkas separator.  Its alignment hypothesis is essential: if
some $d_j<0$, endpoint slack at that coordinate can increase rather than pay
the ceiling bound.  The remaining carrier theorem must select an
$\alpha$-dominant shortage block, close directly, or turn this precise
alignment failure into a priced rooted/mixed packet.

The six-player full-menu replay verifies (PXC34)--(PXC35) on all $340$
common-endpoint shortage rows.  The shortage coordinate is
$\alpha$-dominant on $276$ rows; on the other $64$, (PXC35) is a strict
improvement over $M\le\theta T$.  The extracted-support counts are $276$ and
$62$ among $338$ rows.  Only two of the $340$ full-menu coupling bounds are
tight; $338$ have positive upper-bound slack, and (PXC34) coincides with the
weaker (PXC35) on every archived row.  This exact finite handover checks both
sides of the alignment split, but also shows that the new ceiling bound alone
is usually not decisive for the overlap target.

The positive-gap arm has a sharp exact seven-player sign control.  Take the
lower envelope of $(1,0,0,0,0,0,0)$ and
$(0,1,0,0,0,0,0)$ and let $R=\{0,1\}$.  Its core is the two-player simplex
with five null coordinates, the two endpoint faces are disjoint, and
$\epsilon_R=1/2$.  Here
$\alpha=\beta=(1/2,1/2,0,0,0,0,0)$ on their respective supports,
$a_R=0$, and $q_R=1$, so (PXC36) is the equality $M=1/2$.  An explicit
(PXC30) dual has $\gamma=1/2$ and weight $1/2$ on each of the five dummy
singletons.  This is a decomposable dummy-extension control for the signs
and separator price, not a carrier hard case or a new facet.

A tempting quantitative corollary is false even on the six-player archive.
Combining the upper ceiling (PXC35) with the ceiling-root interpolation bound
(CRI), and minimizing (CRI) over the entire permitted interval
$T/n\le M\le M_{\rm PXC35}$, reaches the target on only $28$ of the $340$
full-menu shortage rows.  Using the actual ceiling reaches it on $165$.
Thus neither (PXC35) nor its strictness can replace the labelled direct-price
or mixed-packet argument; further optimization of this scalar ceiling is a
local maximum, not the remaining proof route.

There is nevertheless a canonical way to choose the endpoint shortage at the
actual direct-price optimum.  Let $I$ globally minimize $Y_I$, let $H$ be its
retained carrier cycle, let $C=(I\cap L)\setminus H$, and assume
$2p+2\le n$.  Put
\[
 \rho_p:=\frac{n-2p-2}{(n-p)(p+1)},\qquad
 R^-:=\{j\in C:z_j<\rho_pz(P)\},\qquad C^+:=C\setminus R^-.
\]
Then (PXC24) and global optimality imply
\[
 j\in R^-\Longrightarrow N\setminus\{j\}
     \text{ is not $x$-tight}.                       \tag{PXC37}
\]
Moreover, by the definition of $C^+$,
\[
 z(C^+)\ge |C^+|\rho_pz(P).                         \tag{PXC38}
\]
Thus $R^-$ feeds exactly into (PXC28)--(PXC36); if it is empty, every
retained noncarrier already pays the direct exchange price.  Writing
$h=|H|$, using $z_i=M$ on $H$, and expanding the actual reserve gives
\[
 \mathcal R_\tau(P)
 \ge hM+x(G)-\tau T+z(R^-)
   +[1-c_p+|C^+|\rho_p]z(P).                        \tag{PXC39}
\]
This is the promised direct-price handover: a negative right side is the
exact residual amount that the endpoint separator or a labelled mixed packet
must pay.  It does not disappear into a scalar ceiling.

The discarded amount is itself an exact nonnegative labelled currency:
\[
 \Lambda_I:=z(C^+)-|C^+|\rho_pz(P)\ge0,
 \qquad
 \mathcal R_\tau(P)=B_I+\Lambda_I,                 \tag{PXC40}
\]
where $B_I$ is the right side of (PXC39).  Hence the remaining problem is not
to invent another residual, but to certify this retained Lmax support-function
excess by the sparse staged Lmax packet or a marked facet.

On the six-player archive every one of the $920$ full-menu direct-optimal
rows through the midpoint has $R^-=\varnothing$.  The high-mass bound
(PXC39) closes $682$ rows and at least one optimum on $296$ of the $415$
cycles.  The remaining $238$ rows, spanning $119$ cycles, require additional
labelled currency.  Thus the canonical endpoint arm is absent at the
archived direct optima even though arbitrary nonoptimal rows have endpoint
shortage.  This prevents the endpoint theory from being mistaken for a
complete selection proof and localizes the remaining finite obstruction to
the labelled packet term.

All $238$ negative-$B_I$ rows have the single profile
$(g,h,p,|C^+|)=(2,2,1,1)$, and their ordered-cut error is zero, so (PXC40)
is exactly the established PXC6/Lmax colour there.  In $236$ rows the retained
currency player belongs to the first Lmax top stratum.  The only two off-top
rows are the same exact record and have $z(P)=0$, so the direct price already
vanishes.  Thus every archived positive-price residual is visible to the
first-stage Lmax top support; a dimension-free proof of that visibility is
still missing.

The relevant carrier closure is a preorder, not an arbitrarily chosen cycle.
For positive $x$ define
\[
 r\preceq_x c
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 c\text{ belongs to every $x$-tight coalition containing }r. \tag{PXC41}
\]
This relation is reflexive and transitive.  Every strong equivalence class
$E$ is therefore a tight-face module at $x$: if a tight coalition contains
one member, mutual reachability forces it to contain all of $E$.  Carrier
monotonicity in both directions also makes $x$ constant on $E$.  If $z$ is
constant on $E$---in particular, when $E$ consists of Lmax-top nongainer
roots---the established carrier-module theorem gives the exact alternative:
an Lmax-tight coalition splits $E$ and supplies strict comparison payment,
or $E$ contracts to one weighted role while preserving exactness, both
selectors, total, and overlap.

Thus a top-stratum player contributing to $\Lambda_I$ is automatically a
maximum-overlap root, because it is a nongainer with $z_j=M$ and hence
$x_j\ge M$.  If it lies in the carrier component of $H$, (PXC41) enlarges the
module before applying separator-or-contraction.  This is dimension-free;
whether the resulting separator is sufficiently priced remains the
quantitative obligation.

The one-way case also has a canonical quotient normal form.  Let $E$ be a
carrier strong component and let a root $j\notin E$ satisfy
$j\preceq_x e$ for every $e\in E$, but not conversely.  If no Lmax-tight
coalition splits $E$, contract it to a role $*$ of weight $|E|$.  The quotient
retains
\[
 z_j=z_*=M,\qquad x_*/|E|\ge x_j\ge M,\qquad
 j\preceq_{\bar x} *.                                  \tag{PXC42}
\]
Here the last relation says that every quotient Lmin-tight coalition
containing $j$ contains $*$.  Exactness, the weighted selectors, total, and
overlap are preserved.  Thus a one-way top-currency satellite either has an
Lmax splitter, or reduces to a weighted locked-satellite quotient.  This
does not put that quotient under the four-role theorem unless at most three
other roles remain.

This quotient has an immediate atomic-size cutoff.  The role $*$ and its
satellite contribute $W=|E|+1$ atomic coordinates with overlap $M$, while
nonnegativity and efficiency give $M\ge T/n$.  Hence
\[
 \frac OT\ge\frac Wn\ge
 \frac{4n}{n^2-(n\bmod2)}                            \tag{PXC43}
\]
whenever $W\ge4$ for even $n$, or $W\ge5$ for odd $n$.  Indeed the even
target is $4/n$, while for odd $n$ the required integer satisfies
$W\ge4n^2/(n^2-1)>4$.  Thus a surviving locked satellite has
$|E|\le2$ in even dimension and $|E|\le3$ in odd dimension.  The reduction
does not bound the number of roles outside this small locked block.

Of the $236$ archived top-currency occurrences, $222$ join the carrier
component.  Ninety enlarged modules are also Lmax modules; their contraction
leaves four weighted roles and is therefore closed by the weighted
four-role theorem.  The other $132$ have an Lmax-tight splitter.  In every
one, some strict separator payment $s$ satisfies
$-B_I\le s\le\Lambda_I$, so $B_I+s\ge0$ is an exact finite certificate.
The remaining $14$ occurrences, in seven records, are one-way carrier
satellites: the currency root carries the two-cycle, but not conversely.
These counts audit the handover and leave the one-way satellite as the next
structural case; they do not prove a dimension-free lower bound on strict
separator payment.

For the $14$ archived satellites, four cycle modules have an Lmax splitter
with payment in $[-B_I,\Lambda_I]$ and close directly.  The other ten
contract to the five-role weight profile $(2,1,1,1,1)$ satisfying (PXC42).
This is the exact residual normal form, not a claim that weighted five-role
locked satellites are already covered by Objective A.

The apparent five-role residue has a further dimension-free marked-row
dichotomy.  In the quotient let $*$ denote the contracted carrier component.
Because $j\preceq_{\bar x}*$ is not reversible, some Lmin-tight coalition
$A$ contains $*$ and omits $j$.  For every such row, and for every Lmax-tight
row $D$ which splits the locked block $\{*,j\}$, core feasibility gives
\[
 \delta_A:=z(A)-x(A)\ge0,
 \qquad
 \sigma_D:=x(D)-z(D)\ge0.                         \tag{PXC44}
\]
The first inequality is strict unless $A$ is a common hinge.  The second is
strict unless $D$ is a common hinge; moreover it is necessarily strict when
$D$ contains $j$ but omits $*$, since such a row cannot be Lmin-tight under
$j\preceq_{\bar x}*$.  Thus every one-way satellite exposes either positive
comparison payment in one of the two colours or a labelled common hinge.
This is an existence and sign theorem, not a lower bound on the payment and
not by itself the target inequality.

There is, however, an immediate certificate on a labelled subarm.  If a
zero-payment marked row $A$ consists only of Lmax-top players, then
\[
 \lambda_A=\frac1{|A|},\qquad \eta=0,\qquad
 \alpha_i=\frac1{|A|}\mathbf1_{\{i\in A\}}              \tag{PXC45}
\]
is a normalized Lmax top over-cover supported on a common hinge, with
$\theta=1/|A|$ and zero cross-surplus.  This follows directly from
$\lambda_A\mathbf1_A=\alpha$ and $x(A)=z(A)=|A|M$.  The resulting spectral
bound is exact certificate evidence; whether it reaches the target must still
be checked unless a separate common-hinge theorem applies.

If the common-support arm fails, its deficiency dual makes the obstruction
quantitative.  Let $H=\arg\max z$, let $(y,\epsilon_*)$ be an optimum of
(CSD-D), and let $A$ be any common hinge.  Dual feasibility and zero slack
on $A$ give $y(A)\le0$, while $y_i\ge\epsilon_*$ for $i\in H$.  Therefore
\[
 -y(A\setminus H)\ge |A\cap H|\epsilon_* ,
 \qquad
 y(H\setminus A)\ge |H\setminus A|\epsilon_* .       \tag{PXC46}
\]
Thus a common marked row which both meets and omits the top block has an
exact alternative: either $\epsilon_*=0$ and a common top over-cover exists,
or its off-top labels absorb a negative dual price proportional to the number
of retained top labels while every omitted top label carries positive price.
This is a signed labelled separation witness at the common-support/
mixed-support interface.  It is not yet a feasible polar of the full
mixed-witness projection, a nonnegative mixed packet, a target-price bound,
or an indecomposable exact-cone facet; converting its polar prices into one
of those objects is the remaining gluing step.

The exact six-player replay checks (PXC46) on all $2{,}065$ common rows in
$374$ records.  Seventy-three records have positive common-support
deficiency, and $21$ common rows both meet and omit the top block there.  A
sharp price-transfer control is record $71$ of the first archive: coalition
$23$ contains three of four top labels, $\epsilon_*=1/6$, its off-top price is
$-1/2=-3\epsilon_*$, and the omitted top price is $1/6$.
Only $11$ of the $21$ positive-transfer rows have any optimal over-cover row
with the same top trace as the hinge.  Record $17$ of the second archive is a
four-row control with support $(21,26,54,60)$ and weights
$(2/5,1/5,1/5,1/5)$, none having the hinge's top trace.  Hence a one-row
nested shear is false even finitely; the facet conversion must retain the
aggregate sparse over-cover.

The aggregate conversion is nevertheless canonical when it has positive
root excess.  Adjoin the tagged common hinge to the support of an optimal
deficiency over-cover, choose a top root $r$, and maximize the root load
$p_r$ subject to all off-root loads $p_i\le1$.  If
$\delta=p_r-1>0$, put $q_i=1-p_i$.  Colour erasure followed by the proved
rooted-pair shear gives
\[
 \sum_C\bar\rho_C\one_C+sum_{i\ne r}q_i\one_{\{r,i\}}
 -\left(\delta+\sum_{i\ne r}q_i\right)e_r=\one_N .  \tag{PXC47}
\]
This is a singleton-exceptional min-semi-balanced system supported on the
full aggregate packet.  Support minimality and the Studen\'y--Kratochv\'il
indecomposability test determine whether it is a full exact-cone facet; no
new criterion is being asserted.

Exactly $11$ of the $21$ positive-deficiency transfer rows have
$\delta>0$; the other ten are tied at zero for every top root.  All eleven
positive systems are support-minimal and indecomposable, independently
replayed in exact Z3 linear arithmetic.  They form three rooted support
orbits with multiplicities $4,3,4$.  Two orbits already occur in the
five-orbit six-player residual atlas.  The third has canonical support
$(1,3,13,49)$ and identity
$\one_3+\one_{13}+\one_{49}-2e_0=\one_N$: it is the saturated rooted star
whose three off-root blocks have sizes $(1,2,2)$.  Hence the positive
aggregate arm lies entirely in the already proved saturated/clone-expanded
rooted-star class.  With $K=4$, all eleven displayed markings also have
positive margin $K\delta-Q$: their effective prices $Q/\delta$ are only
$1$ or $2$.  This settles finite scalar affordability, but not the
payoff-stratum alignment required by the rooted price theorem.  The tied
ten, not facet novelty, are the remaining
alignment cell.  Every tied row is already closed by an earlier arm in the
archive: eight have a direct target packet, three satisfy ceiling
interpolation, and five have an affordable labelled root, with overlaps
among those counts.  Hence none is a new finite hard case.  What remains
universal is to prove that tied aggregate geometry forces an earlier arm, and
to prove sufficient hybrid-overlap price for the positive aggregate facet;
facet existence and scalar affordability alone do not supply that alignment.

The tied geometry has its own exact cover dual.  For a top root $r$, let
${\cal D}$ be the aggregate tagged support and define
\[
 L_r=\max\left\{
 \sum_{C\ni r}\rho_C:\rho\ge0,
 \,\sum_{C\ni i}\rho_C\le1\ (i\ne r)
 \right\}.
\]
Ordinary rational LP duality gives
\[
 L_r=\min\left\{
 \sum_{i\ne r}\beta_i:\beta\ge0,
 \,\sum_{i\in C\setminus\{r\}}\beta_i\ge1
 \quad(C\in{\cal D},\,r\in C)
 \right\}.                                           \tag{PXC48}
\]
Thus positive rooted excess is exactly $L_r>1$; the tied arm has a unit-cost
fractional carrier cover for every top root occurring in the aggregate
support.  In fact the cover is automatically integral in the only sense
needed here.  If its total mass is one, every constraint has left side at
most one and hence equality; every label with positive $\beta_i$ therefore
belongs to every rooted trace.  Conversely, one common label gives the unit
cover.  Thus
\[
 L_r=1
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \bigcap_{C\in{\cal D}:r\in C}(C\setminus\{r\})\ne\varnothing .
                                                               \tag{PXC48$'$}
\]
This is a dimension-free aggregate-carrier theorem.  All $31$ nonzero tied
archive roots exhibit it.  The qualification is load-bearing: the carrier
belongs to every row of the selected aggregate support, not necessarily to
every tight row of either full selection face, so common-module contraction
does not yet follow.  The next bridge is support reselection: either promote
an aggregate carrier cycle to a full tight-face module, or use an escaping
tight row as the positive rooted/separator packet.

Passing from one optimal deficiency support to the full Lmax-tight menu makes
that reselection alternative exact.  Fix a common hinge $A$ and apply
(PXC48) to the tagged family consisting of $A$ and every proper $z$-tight
coalition.  Then at least one of the following holds:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item some $r\in A\cap H$ has $L_r>1$, and an extreme optimizer gives a
      positive-root mixed packet with at most $n-1$ tagged rows;
\item a nontrivial $z$-carrier cycle $E\subseteq A\cap H$ is split by an
      $x$-tight row $D$, in which case
      $z(D)-x(D)>0$;
\item $E$ is a tight-face module at both selections and contracts exactly to
      one weighted role.
\end{enumerate}
We call this alternative (PXC49).
Indeed, if every root in $A\cap H$ is tied, (PXC48$'$) supplies a carrier in
every full $z$-tight row containing it and in $A$.  Such a carrier is itself
in $H$: otherwise the feasible tangent moving mass from the top root to the
lower carrier lexicographically improves Lmax.  Choosing one carrier per
root gives a finite directed graph on $A\cap H$ and hence a cycle $E$.
Every $z$-tight row meeting $E$ contains all of it.  If an $x$-tight row
splits $E$, equality at $z$ would contradict that module property, so its
reverse comparison is strict.  If no such row exists, $E$ is also an
$x$-module; Lmin optimality equalizes $x$ on it, and the proved common-module
contraction applies.

This is a dimension-free carrier/separator/contraction theorem at the marked
hinge interface.  It supplies structure and strict sign, not the missing
lower bound on payment.  In the ten formerly tied archive rows, full-menu
reselection gives three positive packets, two strict Lmin splitters (the same
record in two marked rows), and five common modules.  Thus the structural
trichotomy is exact on the finite residue; quantitative price/alignment is
the remaining universal step.

The positive arm nevertheless has an exact quantitative conservation law.
Delete every root-omitting row from a maximum-root-load optimizer (such rows
do not increase the objective and only consume off-root capacity), and write
$p_i$ for its incidence loads,
$q_i=1-p_i$ for $i\ne r$, and $\delta=p_r-1>0$.  If
$\sigma_C=x(C)-z(C)\ge0$ is the comparison payment of a supported
$z$-tight row and
$\Gamma=\sum_C\rho_C\sigma_C$, then the singleton completion evaluated at
the two selections gives
\[
 \Gamma+\sum_{i\ne r}q_i(x_i-z_i)
   =\delta(x_r-z_r).                                \tag{PXC50}
\]
This is just subtraction in
$\sum_C\rho_C\one_C+\sum_{i\ne r}q_i e_i-\delta e_r=\one_N$;
no facet or optimality assertion beyond the chosen packing is used.

For a maximum-overlap root put
$G=\{i:z_i>x_i\}$,
$D=\sum_{i\in G}(z_i-x_i)$,
$\ell_i=x_i-z_i$ on the nongainers, and define the exact alignment defect
\[
 E=\sum_{i\in G}(1-q_i)(z_i-x_i)
   +\sum_{\substack{i\notin G\\i\ne r}}q_i\ell_i .
\]
Then
\[
 \Gamma=D+\delta\ell_r-E,
 \qquad
 \max_{C:\rho_C>0}\sigma_C\ge \frac{\Gamma}{1+\delta}. \tag{PXC51}
\]
The second inequality uses that every retained row contains $r$, so its
total weight is $1+\delta$.  Thus $E\le\delta\ell_r$ is an exact sufficient
condition for the aggregate comparison charge to cover the total payoff
gain.  Perfect gainer coverage with no nongainer deficit gives $E=0$.

This condition is not automatic, even after optimizing the alignment cost
over the entire maximum-root-load face and then across all eligible roots.
Among the $21$ positive-deficiency marked rows in the exact six-player
replay, six contain no eligible hinge-top maximum-overlap root, seven have
tied full-menu root load, and eight have positive eligible root excess.  At
their exact lexicographic optima, only three of those eight have $E=0$, and
only four satisfy $\Gamma\ge D$.  Hence positive root excess and optimal
support reselection alone do not solve payoff-stratum alignment.  The three positive-packet rows in
the formerly tied (PXC49) residue are exactly the zero-defect controls:
$\delta=1$, $D=5/4$, $\ell_r=1/4$, $\Gamma=3/2$, and the normalized
payment floor is $3/4$.  The remaining universal obligation is now the
scalar one: absorb $E-\delta\ell_r$ by an earlier direct/separator arm.
Choosing a better packet on the same maximum-load face is an exactly refuted
universal route.

Nor can the shortfall always be handed to the Lmin-only rooted projection.
In the exact seven-player neutral control, the common hinges $10$ and $26$
both give an eligible full-menu packet with $\delta=1$, but every positive
row is common, so $\Gamma=0$ and
$E-\delta\ell_r=D=711/19046$.  The exact complete-Lmin one-witness
relaxation at that root has bound
\[
 \frac{8}{15}<\frac{7}{12},
\]
whereas adjoining the full coloured Lmax-tight menu raises the exact bound
to $5/6$.  This is a hostile necessity control: positive aggregate root
excess does not imply either comparison-charge closure or an Lmin-only root
certificate.  The missing universal statement must genuinely use the
coloured mixed-witness projection (or a separate direct separator), not just
the uncoloured incidence support.

There is a useful exact correction to the comparison-charge viewpoint.
For an arbitrary two-colour packet in (MCP4), put

\[
 M=\sum_{(\sigma,C)}\rho_{\sigma,C}\sigma(C),\qquad
 S=T-M\geq0,
\]

and let

\[
 A=\sum_{(x,C)}\rho_{x,C}[z(C)-x(C)],\qquad
 B=\sum_{(z,C)}\rho_{z,C}[x(C)-z(C)].
\]

Tightness and core feasibility give $A,B\geq0$.  Evaluating the incidence
completion at $z$ and $x$, respectively, gives the two exact identities

\[
 \boxed{\quad
 A=S+\delta z_r-\sum_{i\ne r}q_i z_i,
 \qquad
 B=S+\delta x_r-\sum_{i\ne r}q_i x_i .\quad}        \tag{PXC52}
\]

Thus both colours share one and the same unused core-budget currency.  The
inequalities obtained by deleting $S$ are valid but can lose the entire
mechanism.  In particular, on the neutral seven-player control the maximum
root-load face has $\delta=1$, $A=B=0$, and
$S=125/19046$; maximizing comparison charge subject only to rooted
feasibility still gives $A+B=0$, now with $\delta=0$ and $S=53/107$.
Nevertheless the full mixed one-witness relaxation reaches the target, as
above.  Hence no universal theorem depending only on actual-selection
comparison charge can prove the coloured handover.  The packet inequality
$M\leq T$ must instead be evaluated at a hypothetical bad branch point,
where it may be violated even though it has positive slack at the actual
