game.  This is exactly the separation currency in the mixed-witness
projection theorem.

The same exact optimization on ten maximum-overlap roots from six selected
hard archive rows exposes a second tradeoff: every comparison-charge
maximizer has zero root excess, although the corresponding maximum root loads
range from $3/2$ to $2$.  Maximizing excess first and maximizing charge first
are therefore distinct faces.  These controls do not disprove the
one-witness root-selection conjecture; they refute the proposed reduction of
that conjecture to a scalar charge at the archived selections.  The live
dimension-free statement is now a bad-point separation theorem: some
distinguished root's mixed packet cuts, together with the direct/stratum
rows, must exclude every branch point below the hybrid target.

One target-reaching bad-point atom can now be proved in every dimension.
Normalize only for display, so $x(N)=z(N)=T$.  Suppose the gainer set is
exactly $\{a,b\}$ and there is a distinct root $r$ such that

\[
 x_r\ge x_a\ge x_b\ge x_j\quad(j\notin\{r,a,b\}),
 \qquad z_r=z_a\ge z_b.                              \tag{PXC53a}
\]

Assume the two spokes $\{r,a\},\{r,b\}$ are $x$-tight and the aligned
spoke $\{r,a\}$ is also $z$-tight.  Then

\[
 \boxed{\qquad O(x,z)\ge \frac{n}{2(n-2)}T
       \ge \frac{n}{\lfloor n^2/4\rfloor}T
       \quad(n\ge6).\qquad}                          \tag{PXC53}
\]

Indeed, let $w^r$ be a root-zero exactness witness.  The two $x$-tight
spokes give

\[
 2x_r+x_a+x_b\le w^r_a+w^r_b\le T.                 \tag{PXC53b}
\]

The common aligned spoke gives $2z_a=z_r+z_a\le x_r+x_a$.  Hence the total
gain is at most

\[
 (z_a-x_a)+(z_b-x_b)\le x_r-x_b.                    \tag{PXC53c}
\]

On the other hand, efficiency and the order in (PXC53a) give
$T\le x_r+x_a+(n-2)x_b$.  Eliminating $x_a$ with (PXC53b) yields
$x_r\le(n-3)x_b$, while $x_a\ge x_b$ in (PXC53b) yields
$x_r+x_b\le T/2$.  Their intersection gives

\[
 x_r-x_b\le \frac{n-4}{2(n-2)}T,
\]

which proves the first inequality in (PXC53).  The second is equivalent to
$\lfloor n^2/4\rfloor\ge2(n-2)$, immediate separately for even and odd
$n\ge6$.

This theorem explains the neutral control by hand.  There
$(r,a,b)=(3,0,1)$, the spokes are coalitions $9$ and $10$, and coalition
$9$ is common.  The resulting floor is $7/10$, exactly the independently
computed one-witness branch optimum and strictly above $7/12$.  The theorem
also applies to $46$ of the $374$ archived six-player records (with $90$
root/orientation certificates).  Those counts audit scope only.  The
dimension-free proof closes the entire two-gainer/two-spoke alignment class;
the remaining selection theorem must force this atom, its multi-gainer
analogue, a direct separator, or a quotient contraction.

The spoke argument extends to any number of gainers while requiring only one
Lmax alignment.  Let $G=\{i:z_i>x_i\}$ have size $g\ge2$, let
$r\notin G$, and put $X=x(G)$ and $u=\min_{i\in G}x_i$.  Suppose

\[
 x_r\ge x_i\ge x_j\quad(i\in G,\ j\notin G\cup\{r\}),               \tag{PXC54a}
\]

every spoke $\{r,i\}$, $i\in G$, is $x$-tight, and for some
$a\in G$ maximizing $z$ on $G$, the spoke $\{r,a\}$ is also $z$-tight
and $z_r\ge z_a$.  Then

\[
 \boxed{
 \frac{O(x,z)}T\ge
 \begin{cases}
  \dfrac{n}{2(n-2)},&g=2,\\[4pt]
  \dfrac{2(g-1)}{n-2},&g\ge3,
 \end{cases}
 \quad\ge\frac{n}{\lfloor n^2/4\rfloor}.}            \tag{PXC54}
\]

To prove it, sum the $g$ $x$-tight spoke inequalities against a root-zero
witness:

\[
 gx_r+X\le T.                                        \tag{PXC54b}
\]

The one common spoke and the $z$ order give

\[
 z(G)\le gz_a\le\frac g2(x_r+x_a).
\]

Since $x_a\le x_r$, the total gain $D=z(G)-X$ satisfies
$D\le gx_r-X$.  Efficiency and (PXC54a), with
$m=n-g-1$, also give

\[
 T\le x_r+X+mu,\qquad X\ge gu.
\]

Eliminating $u,X,x_r$ from these inequalities and (PXC54b) gives

\[
 gx_r-X\le\frac{n-2g}{n-2}T.                         \tag{PXC54c}
\]

(If $2g>n$, the hypotheses are consequently infeasible.)  Thus
$O=T-D\ge2(g-1)T/(n-2)$.  For $g\ge3$ this dominates the target because
$4\lfloor n^2/4\rfloor\ge n(n-2)$.  When $g=2$, retaining the identity of
the other gainer rather than replacing both by $z_a$ gives the sharper
(PXC53) argument.

This is the useful generalization: the Lmin side supplies the full star, but
the Lmax side need align only one highest-gainer spoke.  The exact archive
scan finds $90$ orientations across $46$ six-player records and closes eight
of the $16$ positive-floor residuals; those finite matches all have $g=2$.
None of the seven fixed hostile/equality branches has the full star, so the
theorem does not replace their already proved direct or rooted-facet arms.
The next selection problem is correspondingly combinatorial: show that a
role-minimal hard branch exposes such an aligned star, or use a missing spoke
to construct the strict separator/marked-facet alternative.

No new full-cone facet class is hidden here.  The incidence completion of the
$x$-star is

\[
 \sum_{i\in G}\one_{\{r,i\}}
 +\sum_{j\notin G\cup\{r\}}e_j-(g-1)e_r=\one_N.      \tag{PXC54d}
\]

Applying the proved rooted-pair shear to every outside singleton replaces it
by $\{r,j\}$ and changes the exceptional coefficient to $-(n-2)$.  The
result is the saturated rooted-star/complement-star system already classified
as indecomposable.  The one common-spoke comparison is a relative
two-selection alignment row, not a new characteristic-function facet.
Finally, both alternatives in (PXC54) are strict above the hybrid target for
$n\ge6$.  Consequently this whole aligned-star branch is excluded from the
equality classification; the balanced $G(p,k)$ orientations correctly remain
in their separate block-star mechanism.

The complementary rooted co-star admits a sharper theorem without the
equal-stratum assumptions of the earlier mixed block-star calculation.  Keep
$G=\{i:z_i>x_i\}$, $|G|=g\ge2$, $r\notin G$, and suppose

\[
 x_r\ge x_i\ge x_j\quad(i\in G,\ j\notin G\cup\{r\}).                \tag{PXC55a}
\]

For $a\in G$ put $C_a=\{r\}\cup(G\setminus\{a\})$.  Assume every $C_a$
is $x$-tight and that, for some $a$ maximizing $z$ on $G$, $C_a$ is also
$z$-tight and $z_r\ge z_a$.  Then

\[
 \boxed{\qquad
 O(x,z)\ge\frac{n}{g(n-g)}T
          \ge\frac{n}{\lfloor n^2/4\rfloor}T .\qquad}               \tag{PXC55}
\]

Let $X=x(G)$, $u=\min_{i\in G}x_i$, and $m=n-g-1$.  Weight every
$x$-tight co-star by $1/(g-1)$ and evaluate at a root-zero witness.  This
gives

\[
 \frac g{g-1}x_r+X\le T.                            \tag{PXC55b}
\]

Efficiency and (PXC55a) give $T\le x_r+X+mu$ and $X\ge gu$.
Subtracting (PXC55b) from the first inequality yields
$x_r\le m(g-1)u$.  Combining this with (PXC55b) and $X\ge gu$ gives

\[
 x_r-u\le\frac{g(n-g)-n}{g(n-g)}T.                  \tag{PXC55c}
\]

The one common co-star gives

\[
 z_r+z(G\setminus\{a\})=x_r+x(G\setminus\{a\}).
\]

Since $z_a\le z_r$, total gain is at most $x_r-x_a\le x_r-u$.
Subtracting (PXC55c) from $T$ proves the first inequality; the second is the
ordinary balanced-product bound $g(n-g)\le\lfloor n^2/4\rfloor$.

This theorem absorbs the earlier equal-stratum mixed block-star bound at the
selection level and exactly matches its facet system.  The incidence
completion is

\[
 \frac1{g-1}\sum_{a\in G}\one_{C_a}
 +\sum_{j\notin G\cup\{r\}}e_j-\frac1{g-1}e_r=\one_N.               \tag{PXC55d}
\]

After the rooted-pair shear its outside-pair coefficients are one, its hinge
coefficients are $1/(g-1)$, and its exceptional coefficient is
$-[m+1/(g-1)]$: precisely the already proved $G(p,k)$ rooted block-star
facet with $(p,k)=(m,g)$.  No new full-cone class is introduced.

Target equality inside this branch requires
$g(n-g)=\lfloor n^2/4\rfloor$ and equality throughout the scalar proof.  In
particular every nonroot $x$ coordinate equals $u$ and
$x_r=m(g-1)u$, the normalized Lmin profile of the sharp family.  The branch
argument alone does not force the remaining Lmax coordinates; their stage
equations do so in the two archived balanced seven-player orientations.

Among $374$ archived six-player records, (PXC55) applies to $84$ records and
$186$ root/omitted-gainer orientations, with gainer counts two, three, and
four.  More importantly, it covers all $16$ positive-floor residual rows
left after direct gluing, interpolation, and null descent.  Among the seven
fixed hostile/equality branches, it rejects all five hostile controls and
accepts the sharp $G(2,4)$ and $G(3,3)$ orientations at respectively four
and three balanced roots.  These counts do not prove universal selection.
They show that the former five-orbit finite residue is now explained by one
dimension-free ordered co-star inequality in an existing facet class.  The
remaining all-$n$ obligation is to force this co-star, the aligned star
(PXC54), a direct/separator certificate, or quotient contraction.

There is no additional structural branch between a rooted packet and the
proved carrier--separator--contraction alternative.  Fix a positive ceiling
root $r$, so $z_r=M=\max_i z_i$ and $x_r\ge M$, and assume $r$ is pinned by
at least one proper $x$-tight coalition.  For every gainer $a$,

\[
 x_a<z_a\le M\le x_r.                                \tag{PXC56a}
\]

Carrier monotonicity therefore implies that some $x$-tight coalition
contains $r$ and omits $a$: otherwise $a$ would be a full-menu carrier of
$r$, forcing $x_a\ge x_r$.

Let ${\cal T}_x(r)$ be the proper $x$-tight rows containing $r$, and define
the rooted packing number

\[
 \nu_r=\max\left\{\sum_{C\in{\cal T}_x(r)}\rho_C:
 \rho\ge0,
 \ \sum_{C\ni i}\rho_C\le1\ (i\ne r)\right\}.       \tag{PXC56b}
\]

Then exactly one of the following holds.

\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item $\nu_r>1$.  An extreme optimizer has at most $n-1$ positive rows and,
with $q_i=1-\sum_{C\ni i}\rho_C$ and $\delta=\nu_r-1>0$, gives
\[
 \sum_C\rho_C\one_C+\sum_{i\ne r}q_i e_i-\delta e_r=\one_N.      \tag{PXC56c}
\]
Thus the branch exposes a sparse singleton-exceptional rooted packet; its
support-minimal factors are tested by the existing semi-balanced
indecomposability criterion.
\item $\nu_r=1$.  Rooted packing/cover duality gives a nonnegative cover
$y$ of total one with $y(C)\ge1$ for every $C\in{\cal T}_x(r)$.  Since
$y(N\setminus\{r\})=1$, every player in the positive support of $y$ belongs
to every row in ${\cal T}_x(r)$.  Hence $r$ has a full-menu common carrier.
No such carrier is a gainer, by (PXC56a) and the preceding omission argument.
The carrier-cycle/separator theorem then supplies either a strict Lmax
separator payment or a common module and exact weighted contraction.
\end{enumerate}

Indeed a nonempty rooted row can be scaled to root load one, so
$\nu_r\ge1$; the two cases are exhaustive.  In the unit-cover case,
$y(C)\ge y(N\setminus\{r\})$ forces equality and puts the support of $y$ in
the intersection of all rooted rows.  This proves the asserted carrier.

Call this the ceiling-root gainer-exclusion trichotomy (PXC56).  It is a
dimension-free structural theorem, not the missing quantitative pricing
statement.  The exact six-player replay contains $703$ ceiling roots: $149$
have positive excess and $554$ are tied, exactly the previously audited
full-menu unaffordable roots.  Every one of the $16$ positive-floor residual
roots has positive excess.  On the seven fixed hostile/equality controls,
the sole all-tied case is the already proved top-and-bottom common-module
branch; all other relevant cases expose a positive packet.  The universal
gap is therefore narrowed to this statement: on the positive side of
(PXC56), prove target pricing/alignment (with (PXC54)--(PXC55) as complete
large classes), or factor the packet into a direct separator or one of the
already classified rooted facets.

The first incidence split on that positive side is now dimension-free.  Let
$G$ be the gainer set, $g=|G|\ge2$, and let an $x$-tight proper row $C$
contain the ceiling root $r$ while omitting a gainer.  Put
\[
 H=C\setminus G,\quad J=C\cap G,\quad
 h=|H|,\quad s=|J|,\quad p=n-g-h.
\]
Then $h\ge1$ because $r\in H$, and necessarily $p\ge1$.  Indeed, if $C$
contained every nongainer, its loss between $x$ and $z$ would be the total
gain $D$, while omission of a genuine gainer would make the gain inside
$J$ strictly smaller than $D$.  This would give $z(C)<x(C)=v(C)$,
contrary to $z\in\core(v)$.

Suppose now that $C$ omits at least two gainers, so $s\le g-2$.  The
cardinality base in the direct master identity is
\[
 b(n;g,h,p,s)=1-\frac{gp}{(g+h)(p+g-s)}.             \tag{PXC57a}
\]
For fixed $n,g$, the subtracted product decreases as $h$ increases, while
$b$ decreases as $s$ increases.  Hence its minimum is at $h=1$ and
$s=g-2$, where $p=n-g-1$ and
\[
 b\ge \frac{n+g+1}{(g+1)(n-g+1)}.                  \tag{PXC57b}
\]
For every $n\ge8$ this is at least the hybrid target:
\[
 \boxed{\qquad b(n;g,h,p,s)\ge
 \frac{4n}{n^2-(n\bmod2)}.\qquad}                  \tag{PXC57}
\]
To check the last comparison, write $n=2k$.  After multiplication by the
positive denominator, the relevant quadratic is
$2g^2-3kg+2k^2-3k-2$, whose continuous minimum is
$(k-4)(7k+4)/8$.  For $n=2k+1$, comparison with
$(2k+1)/[k(k+1)]$ has continuous minimum numerator
\[
 \frac{7(k-4)^4+102(k-4)^3+505(k-4)^2+898(k-4)+239}
 {4(2k+1)},
\]
which is strictly positive for $k\ge4$.  Equality in (PXC57) occurs only
at $(n,g,h,p,s)=(8,3,1,4,1)$.  The complete lower-dimensional failures are
$(6,2,1,3,0)$, $(6,3,1,2,1)$, $(7,2,1,4,0)$, and
$(7,3,1,3,1)$, with bases respectively $3/5,5/8,5/9,11/20$.

The upper-budget qualification is real.  At root $6$ of the fixed
eight-player primitive-coefficient control, the five $x$-tight
multi-omission rows $70,85,90,104,204$ all satisfy (PXC57) and have zero
lower comparison slack, but every residual budget is smaller than the loss
on its nongainer block.  Thus cardinality plus Lmin tightness cannot finish
this arm by termwise positivity; the remaining labelled payment must come
from Lmax colour, the low-block term, or an earlier separator.

Call (PXC57) the multi-omission direct-base gate.  Because $C$ is
$x$-tight, core feasibility of $z$ automatically supplies the lower
comparison inequality: the gain on $J$ is at least the loss on $H$.
Thus from eight players onward a multi-omission blocker can fail the direct
theorem only at its \emph{upper} labelled comparison budget; it cannot fail
because of cardinality.  If no such blocker exists, the gainer omissions
forced by (PXC56) select, one per gainer, rooted rows containing every other
gainer.  This is a decorated complete co-star: the remaining work is to
remove or price its nongainer decorations and force one Lmax-aligned row.
This dichotomy is a strict narrowing of the positive arm, not the missing
selection/gluing theorem itself.

The remaining budget has a useful exact Lmax form.  Let
$D=z(G)-x(G)$ be the total gain and define the below-mean deficit of the
omitted nongainer block
\[
 Q(P):=\sum_{i\in P}\left(\frac Tn-z_i\right).
\]
If $R_s$ denotes the direct residual budget for a packet $J$ of size $s$
and $L_H=x(H)-z(H)$, then
\[
 \boxed{\qquad
 R_s-L_H=\frac{n}{n-p}Q(P)-\frac{p+g-s}{g}D .
 \qquad}                                             \tag{PXC58}
\]
Indeed total gain equals total nongainer loss, so
$x(G)=z(G)-D$ and $x(P)=z(P)+D-L_H$.  Substitution in the direct residual
formula first gives
\[
 R_s-L_H=\frac{p}{g+h}z(G\cup H)-z(P)
          -\frac{p+g-s}{g}D.
\]
Using $g+h=n-p$ and $z(G\cup H)=T-z(P)$ yields (PXC58).
Consequently the exact combined comparison/residual term is nonnegative if
and only if
\[
 Q(P)\ge \frac{(n-p)(p+g-s)}{ng}D.                  \tag{PXC58a}
\]
For $s=g-1$ this is precisely the previously proved maximal-packet
deficit-quota identity; (PXC58) shows that each additional omitted gainer
raises the required quota by $(n-p)D/(ng)$.  Hence multi-omission buys the
cardinality gate (PXC57) at an exact Lmax price.

The primitive eight-player control fails (PXC58a) on all five rows.  Its
quota margins are
\[
 -\frac{48}{13},\quad -\frac{40}{39},\quad -\frac{124}{39},\quad
 -\frac{308}{195},\quad -\frac4{13}.
\]
Conversely every displayed co-star row in both balanced seven-player
equality orientations has quota margin zero.  Their direct cardinality bases
are below target, so this is equality compatibility rather than a direct
proof of the sharp cases.  The live Lmax-colour problem is now explicit: pay
the negative part of (PXC58), or use quota failure to select a
separator/contraction or a decorated co-star covered by (PXC55).  Merely
enlarging the uncoloured packing cannot change this scalar deficit.

The decorations in the co-star arm can nevertheless be priced rather than
deleted.  Put $q=n-g$ and $m=q-1$, and for every $a\in G$ suppose that

\[
 C_a=\{r\}\cup(G\setminus\{a\})\cup K_a
\]

is $x$-tight, where $K_a$ is a set of nongainers outside
$G\cup\{r\}$.  Assume $q\ge2$, the order condition (PXC55a), and the
packing condition that every outside player belongs to at most $g-1$ of the
$K_a$.  Define

\[
 A:=\frac1{g-1}\sum_{a\in G}x(K_a),\qquad
 \mu:=\frac{(g-1)n}{g(n-g)}.
\]

If, for one $a$ maximizing $z_a$ on $G$, the row $C_a$ is also $z$-tight
and $z_r\ge z_a$, then with $L_a:=x(K_a)-z(K_a)$,

\[
 \boxed{\qquad
 O\ge \frac{n}{g(n-g)}T+\mu A-L_a .
 \qquad}                                             \tag{PXC59}
\]

Indeed, weight the decorated co-stars by $1/(g-1)$ and evaluate them at a
root-zero exactness witness.  The packing hypothesis bounds every nonroot
witness load by one, and hence

\[
 \frac g{g-1}x_r+x(G)+A\le T.                       \tag{PXC59a}
\]

Efficiency and the order condition give
$T\le x_r+x(G)+m\,u$.  Comparison with (PXC59a) yields

\[
 x_r\le(g-1)(m\,u-A).                               \tag{PXC59b}
\]

The common decorated row and $z_a\le z_r$ give, for total gainer gain $D$,

\[
 D\le x_r-x_a+L_a\le x_r-u+L_a.                    \tag{PXC59c}
\]

Set $k=1-n/[g(n-g)]$.  Since $g,q\ge2$ and $n\ge6$, $k\ge0$; use
(PXC59a) as a lower bound on $T$, then (PXC59b) as an upper bound on $x_r$.
Exact simplification gives

\[
 D\le kT+L_a-\mu A,
\]

which is equivalent to (PXC59).  Therefore the hybrid target follows
whenever

\[
 L_a\le \mu A+
 n\left(\frac1{g(n-g)}-\frac1{\lfloor n^2/4\rfloor}\right)T.      \tag{PXC59d}
\]

At balanced cardinality this says simply $L_a\le\mu A$.  Clean co-stars
have $A=L_a=0$ and recover (PXC55), including both sharp seven-player
orientations.  In the six-player archive the priced theorem applies to $90$
of $374$ records, six more than the clean theorem.  It has $728$ labelled
certificates, of which $186$ are clean, and all $728$ reach the target.  The
six new records use genuine decorations; in a representative certificate
$A>0$ while the aligned decoration has $L_a=0$.  All $16$ positive-floor
residuals still close, every fixed hostile control is rejected, and the two
sharp orientations retain respectively four and three clean certificates.
These counts audit the signs and show that decoration pricing has nonvacuous
scope.  They do not select a packing-admissible family or force (PXC59d).
Decorated supports may also factor into known rooted facets or expose a new
support-minimal semi-balanced system; that facet classification remains an
objective-C obligation.

The packing hypothesis in (PXC59) has an exact fractional alternative.  Keep
the no-multi-omission branch of (PXC57), and for any rooted packing $\rho$
write
\[
 \nu=\sum_C\rho_C,\qquad
 \omega_a=\sum_{C\not\ni a}\rho_C\quad(a\in G).
\]
Every supported row omits at most one gainer.  Since the load of gainer $a$
is $\nu-\omega_a\le1$, one has $\omega_a\ge\nu-1$, while
$\sum_a\omega_a\le\nu$.  Therefore
\[
 \boxed{\qquad 1\le\nu_r\le\frac g{g-1}.\qquad}     \tag{PXC60}
\]
At equality every inequality just used is tight: no positive-weight row
omits zero gainers, and the total weight of rows omitting each $a$ is exactly
$1/(g-1)$.  Hence a saturated optimizer is a \emph{fractional decorated
complete co-star}.  Its gainer loads are one, its root load is $g/(g-1)$,
and every outsider load is at most one.  If
\[
 A_\rho:=\sum_C\rho_C x(K_C),
\]
the root-zero witness gives (PXC59a) with $A_\rho$ in place of $A$.
Consequently the proof of (PXC59) applies verbatim whenever a positive-weight
row omitting a highest-$z$ gainer is also $z$-tight and satisfies the same
root comparison.  Integral packing-admissible co-stars are only the special
case assigning weight $1/(g-1)$ to one row of each omission type.

Saturation itself has an exact weighted Hall criterion.  For each $a\in G$,
let ${\cal F}_a$ be the rooted tight rows omitting $a$, and write $K(C)$ for
the outsider decoration of $C$.  Then a fractional decorated co-star exists
if and only if, for every nonnegative outsider price vector $\beta$,
\[
 \boxed{\qquad
 \frac1{g-1}\sum_{a\in G}\min_{C\in{\cal F}_a}\beta(K(C))
 \le\sum_{j\notin G\cup\{r\}}\beta_j .
 \qquad}                                             \tag{PXC61}
\]
Necessity follows by pricing the outsider-capacity inequalities and then
minimizing separately within every omission type.  For sufficiency, the
product of the type simplices
\[
 \sum_{C\in{\cal F}_a}\rho_C=\frac1{g-1},\qquad \rho_C\ge0,
\]
has a compact convex image in outsider-load space.  If that image misses the
downward unit box, strict separation supplies a nonnegative $\beta$ violating
(PXC61); conversely such a violation separates every typed mixture from the
box.  This is finite-dimensional separation, equivalently exact LP duality.

Thus failure of saturation is not an unspecified selection failure: after
normalizing $\sum_j\beta_j=1$, it has a strict rational weighted-Hall
certificate.  For every omission type with positive minimum, every row in
that type meets $\operatorname{supp}\beta$; this is a labelled collective
carrier obstruction.  At $\nu_r=1$ it collapses to the common-carrier arm of
(PXC56).  For $1<\nu_r<g/(g-1)$, converting this weighted obstruction into a
strict Lmax separator payment or a target-reaching mixed rooted facet is the
remaining all-dimensional arrow.

There is a direct tangent interpretation of a strict Hall certificate.  Use
the normalization $\sum_j\beta_j=1$, put
\[
 m_a:=\min_{C\in{\cal F}_a}\beta(K(C)),
\]
and define a zero-sum direction $y$ by
\[
 y_j=\beta_j\quad(j\notin G\cup\{r\}),\qquad
 y_a=m_a-1\quad(a\in G),\qquad
 y_r=g-1-\sum_a m_a.                                \tag{PXC62a}
\]
A Hall violation gives $\sum_a m_a>g-1$, so $y_r<0$, while
$0\le m_a\le1$ makes every gainer coordinate nonpositive.  For a rooted row
$C\in{\cal F}_a$,
\[
 y(C)=\beta(K(C))-m_a\ge0.                          \tag{PXC62b}
\]

Order players increasingly by $x_i$, breaking every tie increasingly by
$y_i$.  Suppose every proper prefix has nonnegative $y$-sum and at least one
has positive sum.  This is exactly the condition that a sufficiently small
move from $x$ along $y$ strictly Lorenz-improves $x$; it holds in particular
when every positive-priced outsider is strictly below the root and every
gainer with $m_a<1$.  If $y(S)\ge0$ on every $x$-tight coalition, that small
move stays in the core, contradicting Lmin optimality.
Consequently some $x$-tight coalition $D$ has $y(D)<0$.  By (PXC62b) and the
no-multi-omission hypothesis, such a blocker is necessarily either root-free
or root-containing with every gainer present:
\[
 \boxed{\qquad
 r\notin D\quad\hbox{or}\quad \{r\}\cup G\subseteq D.
 \qquad}                                             \tag{PXC62}
\]
This Lorenz--Hall-blocker lemma is a conventional tangent-cone consequence,
not a claim that the blocker already pays the overlap target.  Weak Lmin ties
can prevent the exact prefix condition and remain a separate
contraction/stage-pinning case.

On the ordered arm the prefix premise is in fact automatic away from
boundary ties.  Each omission type has a row by (PXC56) once the root is
pinned by a proper Lmin-tight coalition, so $0\le m_a\le1$, and a
Hall violation normalizes to $\sum_a(1-m_a)=1-\epsilon<1$.  Orderedness
places every outsider weakly below every gainer, so in the increasing
order the running sum first accumulates nonnegative outsider prices, and
once every outsider has been passed it stays at least
\[
 1-\sum_{b\ {\rm processed}}(1-m_b)\ \ge\ \epsilon\ >\ 0,
                                                       \tag{PXC74}
\]
because the subtracted terms are nonnegative and total $1-\epsilon$.
Hence if no positively priced outsider ties the minimum gainer and no
gainer ties the root, every prefix is nonnegative and some prefix is
positive, the direction is a strict Lorenz tangent, and Lmin optimality
forces a root-free or all-gainer rooted blocker as in (PXC62).  The
prospective ordered-saturation theorem---every ordered no-multi-omission
ceiling root is saturated, with no exception in the six-player census or
among the designed seven-to-nine-player roots---is therefore equivalent,
away from boundary ties, to excluding those two blocker shapes.  Both
shapes are genuinely tight coalitions in general, so the exclusion must
use their interaction with the obstruction prices; this remains open.

On the saturated arm, even common $z$-tightness can be removed at an exact
scalar price.  Put $d_a=z_a-x_a$, let $d_{\max}=\max_{a\in G}d_a$, and
write $\ell_r=x_r-z_r\ge0$.  For any positive-weight co-star row $C$ of
omission type $a$, define its Lmax slack $S_C=z(C)-x(C)\ge0$.  Direct
expansion gives the row-independent identity
\[
 S_C+L_C=D-d_a-\ell_r,\qquad L_C=x(K_C)-z(K_C).      \tag{PXC63a}
\]
The comparison used in (PXC59) now gives
\[
 D\le x_r-x_a+L_C+S_C.
\]
The fractional packet proof gives, before using any common row,
\[
 x_r-u\le
 \left(1-\frac{n}{g(n-g)}\right)T-\mu A_\rho.
\]
Choose $a$ maximizing $d_a$ and substitute (PXC63a).  Then
\[
 \boxed{\qquad
 O\ge \frac{n}{g(n-g)}T+\mu A_\rho-
       (D-d_{\max}-\ell_r).
 \qquad}                                             \tag{PXC63}
\]
Thus an ordered saturated fractional co-star reaches the hybrid target
without any common Lmax row whenever
\[
 D-d_{\max}-\ell_r\le \mu A_\rho+
 n\left(\frac1{g(n-g)}-
         \frac1{\lfloor n^2/4\rfloor}\right)T.       \tag{PXC63b}
\]
The left side is nonnegative: by (PXC63a), it is $S_C+L_C$ for the
maximum-gain omission type.  It measures precisely the gain dispersed away
from the strongest gainer after crediting the root loss.  Equation (PXC63b),
not common-row existence, is therefore the remaining scalar statement on
the saturated arm.

The Lmax input is indispensable.  At the allocation level, take
\[
 x=(9,1,1,1,1,1,1,1),\qquad
 z=(7/2,25/8,25/8,25/8,25/8,0,0,0).
\]
With root zero and gainers $1,2,3,4$, the four clean co-stars are
$x$-tight by stipulation, their uniform packet saturates $T=16$, the root is
the Lmax ceiling, and every co-star has nonnegative comparison slack $7/8$.
Nevertheless $O=15/2<8$, the eight-player target, while (PXC63) gives only
$57/8$.  This is an exact relaxed allocation-level control, not an asserted
Lmin/Lmax pair of any exact game.  It proves that order, saturated packet
geometry, efficiency, and the individual core-side co-star comparisons do
not imply (PXC63b).  Any universal proof must use genuine Lmax optimality---
the sparse over-cover, staged Lmax pinning, or an equivalent property.

The first-stage Lmax payment can be isolated exactly.  Under the ordered
co-star hypotheses, every outsider $j$ satisfies
$z_j\le x_j\le u<z_a$ for each gainer $a$.  Hence the Lmax top stratum
$H_{\max}$ is contained in $\{r\}\cup G$, and $r$ is its only nongainer.
Let $\alpha$ be the probability on $H_{\max}$ supplied by a normalized
sparse first-stage Lmax over-cover, so $\alpha\cdot x\ge M=z_r$.  Then
\[
 \boxed{\qquad
 \alpha_r\ell_r-
 \sum_{a\in G\cap H_{\max}}\alpha_a d_a
 =\alpha\cdot x-M\ge0.
 \qquad}                                             \tag{PXC64}
\]
This is genuine normalized Lmax currency, but it is weighted currency.  It
does not by itself control the unweighted dispersed-gain term in (PXC63b).
Indeed all $89$ ordered saturated archive roots give positive nonroot top
mass, but only two have positive surplus in (PXC64); the other $87$ are
tight.  Thus top-anchor surplus is not the missing universal reserve.

The labels of the sparse over-cover are much sharper in the finite census.
At $88$ of the $89$ ordered saturated roots, its support contains a
positive-weight fractional co-star row.  The sole exception is
\texttt{harvest6\_601.json}, record $71$, root $4$: its one-row sparse Lmax support is
coalition $26=\{1,3,4\}$, which omits two gainers, and its (PXC63b) margin is
$19/12$.  This is discovery evidence for a common-co-star-or-Lmax-
multi-omission alternative, not a universal theorem.  It shows that the next
proof must retain the labelled sparse support rather than only $\alpha$.

The complete staged Lmax packet gives a second exact description of the
same missing reserve.  Choose $a^*\in G$ with
$d_{a^*}=d_{\max}$.  For every $a\ne a^*$, normalize a sign-coherent Lmax
pinning relation for $a$, and let $c_a$ be the coefficient of the root-loss
column.  Under the ordered hypotheses the root is the only positive-loss
source in the cumulative Lmax upper set: outsiders satisfy
$z_j\le x_j<z_a$, and gainers are not loss sources.  Evaluation at $x-z$
therefore gives
\[
 d_a\le c_a\ell_r.
\]
Put $\sigma_a=c_a\ell_r-d_a\ge0$ and
$\kappa=\sum_{a\ne a^*}c_a$.  Then, without an inequality,
\[
 D-d_{\max}-\ell_r
   =(\kappa-1)\ell_r-\sum_{a\ne a^*}\sigma_a.       \tag{PXC65a}
\]
Substitution into (PXC63) gives the mixed Lmin--Lmax bound
\[
 \boxed{\qquad
 O\ge \frac{n}{g(n-g)}T+\mu A_\rho
       -(\kappa-1)\ell_r+\sum_{a\ne a^*}\sigma_a.
 \qquad}                                             \tag{PXC65}
\]
Thus coefficient congestion is not itself the invariant obstruction:
increasing a root price also creates the evaluated transport surplus that
must be retained with it.  The target follows exactly when
\[
 (\kappa-1)\ell_r\le \mu A_\rho+
 n\left(\frac1{g(n-g)}-\frac1{\lfloor n^2/4\rfloor}\right)T
 +\sum_{a\ne a^*}\sigma_a.                          \tag{PXC65b}
\]
This is an exact coupling of the fractional Lmin packet to complete staged
Lmax pinning, not a proof of (PXC65b): by (PXC65a), the latter is equivalent
to (PXC63b).  In the exact six-player audit the canonical minimum-root-price
relations have $\kappa\in[1,3]$.  The tempting unit-congestion claim holds
on only $77/89$ ordered saturated roots, and the raw congestion inequality
closes only $79/89$, with minimum margin $-25/12$.  After retaining the
transport surpluses, all $89$ close and the minimum margin is zero.  The next
load-bearing task is therefore to factor or aggregate the labelled
$\sigma_a$ certificates; no scalar bound on $\kappa$ alone can supply the
missing theorem.

The surplus in (PXC65) has a labelled nonnegative decomposition.  Write the
normalized sign-coherent Lmax relation for gainer $a$ as
\[
 \sum_C\lambda^a_C\mathbf1_C-
 \sum_{j\in Q_a}p^a_je_j=c^a\mathbf1_N+e_a,
 \qquad \lambda^a,p^a\ge0,                          \tag{PXC66a}
\]
where $Q_a$ is the cumulative Lmax upper set and $p^a_r=c_a$.  Put
$R_C=x(C)-z(C)\ge0$ on its $z$-tight coalition support.  Orderedness again
makes $r$ the only loss coordinate in $Q_a$; every other nonzero term is a
gainer $b$ with $z_b\ge z_a$.  Dotting (PXC66a) with $x-z$ gives
\[
 \boxed{\qquad
 \sigma_a=c_a\ell_r-d_a
 =\sum_C\lambda^a_CR_C+
   \sum_{b\in G\cap Q_a}p^a_bd_b.
 \qquad}                                             \tag{PXC66}
\]
Thus every unit of transport surplus is labelled either by a genuine
reverse-payment coalition or by another gainer in the same or a higher Lmax
stratum.  In the latter terms the directed edge $a\to b$ weakly increases
$z$; consequently every directed cycle lies within one tied Lmax stratum.
If all $R_C$ on a transport component vanish, all its coalition columns are
common $x$--$z$ tight columns.  A terminal tied component is therefore
either split by a common tight row, supplying the common-hinge arm, or is
unsplit by the full common menu and is a common module eligible for the
established contraction theorem.  This is a qualitative handover, not yet
the quantitative assertion that the selected hinge or contracted quotient
pays (PXC65b); root-paid terminal leaves must also be aggregated without
reintroducing the false scalar-congestion claim.

The exact minimum-root-price audit records the complete decomposition rather
than only $c_a$.  Of the ten ordered saturated roots where raw congestion
fails, three use a positive reverse-payment row: two have a root-free row
and one has a rooted multi-omission row.  The other seven have pure
cross-gainer surplus on common coalition support.  Nine of the ten also
have a fractional co-star row in the canonical sparse top over-cover; the
sole exception is the rooted multi-omission case.  Restricting to the four
positive-floor residual roots leaves two root-free reverse-payment cases and
two pure-pin cases, and all four have a sparse common co-star.  These are
finite support classifications and depend on the canonical exact LP
solutions; they motivate, but do not prove, the dimension-free
common-co-star/separator/module alternative.

There is a coefficient-only way to aggregate the transport bundles without
double-counting their imported gains.  Fix a maximum-gain gainer $a^*$ and
put
\[
 B_{ab}=\mathbf1_{\{a=b\}}+p^a_b\qquad(a,b\in G).
\]
For one normalized relation per gainer, define the fractional bundle-cover
number
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \tau(a^*)&=\min\left\{\sum_{a\in G}c_at_a:
       \sum_{a\in G}B_{ab}t_a\ge1\ (b\ne a^*),\ t\ge0\right\} \\[-2pt]
 &=\max\left\{\sum_{b\ne a^*}y_b:
       \sum_{b\ne a^*}B_{ab}y_b\le c_a\ (a\in G),\ y\ge0\right\}.
 \end{aligned}                                                  \tag{PXC67a}
\]
The equality is ordinary finite LP duality.  Dropping only the nonnegative
reverse-coalition term in (PXC66) gives
$c_a\ell_r\ge\sum_bB_{ab}d_b$.  Multiply by any feasible $t_a$ and use the
cover constraints.  Then
\[
 \sum_{b\ne a^*}d_b\le\tau(a^*)\ell_r.
\]
Consequently the saturated co-star bound strengthens to the conditional
mixed-packet theorem
\[
 \boxed{\qquad
 O\ge \frac{n}{g(n-g)}T+\mu A_\rho-
       \bigl(\tau(a^*)-1\bigr)\ell_r.
 \qquad}                                             \tag{PXC67}
\]
In particular the hybrid target follows whenever
\[
 \bigl(\tau(a^*)-1\bigr)\ell_r\le \mu A_\rho+
 n\left(\frac1{g(n-g)}-\frac1{\lfloor n^2/4\rfloor}\right)T.
                                                               \tag{PXC67b}
\]
Unlike (PXC65b), this is a genuinely stronger certificate condition rather
than an algebraic restatement of (PXC63b): it replaces the actual dispersed
gain by an independently checkable fractional cover of normalized Lmax
relations.  It is also exactly the sought sparse Lmax over-cover dual to the
fractional Lmin co-star packet.

The exact six-player audit solves both sides of (PXC67a).  It proves
(PXC67b) at all $89$ ordered saturated roots, with minimum margin zero.
At $88$ roots the optimal aggregate root price is at most one.  The sole
value $4/3$ occurs at
\texttt{harvest6\_602.json}, record $22$, root $2$; that record is already
direct-closed, has $A_\rho=1/4$, and retains margin $7/30$ even after all
reverse-coalition credit is discarded.  For the actual-gain-weighted cover,
$88/89$ optima cover demand exactly; the same exceptional record overcovers
by $1/10$.  These facts are exact bounded evidence, not a proof of the
dimension-free budget (PXC67b).  The new load-bearing problem is to prove
that the bundle-cover dual prices are paid by decoration/cardinality reserve,
or else force a separator, common-hinge, or contraction arm.

The dual in (PXC67a) already forces that labelled alternative whenever its
price exceeds one.  Extend an optimal dual by $y_{a^*}=0$, so
$\sum_{b\in G}y_b=\tau$, and define the zero-sum player price
\[
 w_r=-\tau,\qquad w_b=y_b\ (b\in G),\qquad
 w_j=0\ (j\notin G\cup\{r\}).                       \tag{PXC68a}
\]
Dot (PXC66a) with $w$.  The dual constraint gives, for every normalized
gainer relation,
\[
 \boxed{\qquad
 \sum_C\lambda^a_C w(C)
 =\sum_{b\ne a^*}B_{ab}y_b-c_a\tau
 \le c_a(1-\tau).
 \qquad}                                             \tag{PXC68}
\]
Because $d_a>0$ forces $c_a>0$, $\tau>1$ makes this average strictly
negative.  Hence every relation support contains a proper $z$-tight row
$C$ with $w(C)<0$.  A root-free row has $w(C)\ge0$, while a rooted row has
\[
 w(C)=-\sum_{b\notin C}y_b.
\]
Thus the selected row is rooted and omits positive dual price.  This is a
dimension-free aggregate Lmax separator theorem.  If its reverse slack
$R_C$ is positive, it supplies genuine strict comparison payment.  If
$R_C=0$, it is a common $x$--$z$ tight row; according as it omits several
positive-price gainers, exactly one, or is unsplit across a tied component,
it enters the rooted multi-omission, common co-star/common-hinge, or common-
module arm.  What remains is quantitative: prove that one of those selected
rows pays the excess $(\tau-1)\ell_r$ not already covered by decoration and
cardinality reserve.

The only audited root with $\tau>1$ is the decorated direct-closed control
above.  Its exact dual is $(y_4,y_5)=(1/3,1)$, and every normalized relation
exposes a negative rooted omission row.  The returned rows $28,45,46$ all
have zero reverse slack and are common co-stars; the decoration reserve
already proves (PXC67b).  This is a sign and label calibration of (PXC68),
not evidence that the same quantitative payment holds in every dimension.

The staged relations behind (PXC67a) are only one certificate basis.  The
same evaluation argument accepts any nonnegative combination of full-menu
Lmax-tight rows.  Call a representation
\[
 \sum_C L_C\mathbf1_C=C_0\mathbf1_N+\sum_{i\in N}q_ie_i,
 \qquad L_C\ge0,                                     \tag{PXC69a}
\]
with $C$ ranging over the proper $z$-tight coalitions, $C_0$ free,
$q_b\ge1$ for every gainer $b\ne a^*$, $q_{a^*}\ge0$, $q_j\le0$ at every
outsider with $x_j>z_j$, and $q_j$ free at tied outsiders, a \emph{menu
cover} at $(r,a^*)$, and let $\tau_{\rm menu}(a^*)$ be the minimum of $q_r$
over all menu covers.  Aggregating any admissible staged relation system
with optimal (PXC67a) weights yields a menu cover whose root coefficient is
its cover price: coverage gives $q_b\ge1$, orderedness keeps strict
outsiders out of the transport columns, and tied outsiders may enter with
either sign.  Hence $\tau_{\rm menu}(a^*)\le\tau(a^*)$ for every admissible
system, and $\tau_{\rm menu}$ is the exact value of the best cover-type
certificate.  Dotting (PXC69a) with $x-z$ kills the ambient term, pays each
used row its nonnegative reverse slack, and keeps every outsider term
nonpositive, so $\sum_{b\ne a^*}d_b\le\tau_{\rm menu}(a^*)\ell_r$ and the
saturated co-star theorem strengthens to
\[
 \boxed{\qquad
 O\ge \frac{n}{g(n-g)}T+\mu A_\rho-
       \bigl(\tau_{\rm menu}(a^*)-1\bigr)\ell_r.
 \qquad}                                             \tag{PXC69}
\]
Ordinary LP duality computes the same number as a root-exchange rate:
\[
 \tau_{\rm menu}(a^*)=
 \max\Bigl\{\sum_{b\ne a^*}y_b\Bigr\}               \tag{PXC69b}
\]
over all $y$ with $y(C)\le0$ on every proper $z$-tight coalition,
$y(N)=0$, $y_r=-1$, $y\ge0$ on the gainers, $y\le0$ on strict outsiders,
and $y=0$ on tied outsiders.  Negated, an optimal $y$ is a core tangent
direction at the Lmax point that raises the root by exactly one unit,
weakly lowers every gainer, weakly raises every strict outsider, and
freezes the tied outsiders.  Thus $\tau_{\rm menu}$ is the exact
root-versus-gainer exchange rate of the Lmax tangent cone in the selector
orthant.  The direction $(x-z)/\ell_r$ is feasible for (PXC69b)---and
$\ell_r>0$ on this arm, since the staged pinning inequality
$d_a\le c_a\ell_r$ of (PXC65) forbids a zero root loss once a gainer
exists---so
\[
 \tau_{\rm menu}(a^*)\ge\frac{D-d_{\max}}{\ell_r}.
\]
No cover-type certificate prices below the actual dispersed gain, and
equality states that the selector pair itself is an extremal exchange.
The target therefore follows from (PXC69) whenever
\[
 \bigl(\tau_{\rm menu}(a^*)-1\bigr)\ell_r\le \mu A_\rho+
 n\left(\frac1{g(n-g)}-\frac1{\lfloor n^2/4\rfloor}\right)T,
                                                     \tag{PXC69c}
\]
and in the sharper unit-exchange form whenever $\tau_{\rm menu}\le1$.  The
unit-exchange bound forces $D-d_{\max}\le\ell_r$, equivalently, through
efficiency, the outsider-loss bound $\sum_j(x_j-z_j)\le d_{\max}$ over the
outsiders; the two statements coincide exactly when the menu cover is
exchange-exact.

The exact audit solves both programs at all $89$ ordered saturated archive
roots and both sharp seven-player orientations.  Every optimum is
exchange-exact and equal to one:
$\tau_{\rm menu}=(D-d_{\max})/\ell_r=1$, so the outsider loss equals the
maximum gain exactly at every audited root, and (PXC69c) closes all $89$
roots and all $16$ positive-floor residuals with minimum margin zero.
Every optimal cover burns no reverse slack, so its support consists of
common $x$--$z$ tight rows only.  The extremal exchange direction is the
literal selector direction $-(x-z)/\ell_r$ at $25$ roots and a genuinely
different tied optimum at the other $64$.  Eleven roots admit a strictly
cheaper menu cover than the canonical staged bundle cover, with maximum
saving one; in particular the sole $\tau=4/3$ exception of (PXC67) prices
at $\tau_{\rm menu}=1$ through the single common row $52=\{2,4,5\}$ and
needs no decoration reserve.  Both sharp orientations sit at
$\tau_{\rm menu}=1$ with zero margin, so the unit-exchange bound is
equality-compatible.  The eight-player relaxed control has outsider loss
$3$ and maximum gain $17/8$, violating the unit-exchange bound at the
allocation level; genuine Lmax optimality remains indispensable.  These are
exact finite calculations.  The uniform equality of outsider loss and
maximum gain is a striking archived regularity, possibly a harvest
artifact, and is not asserted as a theorem.  At this stage the working
problem was the unit root-exchange bound $\tau_{\rm menu}\le1$, or its
reserve-padded form (PXC69c); both were later refuted, as the sequel and
the saturated-arm summary record.

The archived optima localize that bound to one row.  Call an Lmax-tight
coalition $C$ an \emph{aligned co-star row} at $(r,a^*)$ if $r\in C$,
$G\setminus\{a^*\}\subseteq C$, $a^*\notin C$, and $C$ contains no strict
outsider.  Gainers in $C$ contribute $-d_b$ to the selector difference,
the root contributes $\ell_r$, and tied outsiders contribute nothing, so
\[
 x(C)-z(C)=\ell_r-(D-d_{a^*}).                       \tag{PXC70a}
\]
Core feasibility of $x$ on the $z$-tight row makes the left side
nonnegative.  One aligned co-star row therefore already pays the entire
saturated arm:
\[
 \boxed{\qquad
 D-d_{\max}\le\ell_r,
 \qquad \tau_{\rm menu}(a^*)\le1
 \hbox{ via the menu cover } L_C=1,
 \qquad}                                             \tag{PXC70}
\]
and (PXC69) reaches the target with the whole decoration and cardinality
reserve to spare.  Dually, if the Lmin packet contains an $x$-tight
type-$a^*$ co-star row all of whose decorations are tied, core feasibility
of $z$ on that row reverses the sign in (PXC70a) and gives
$D-d_{\max}\ge\ell_r$.  A common aligned row therefore forces the exact
equality $D-d_{\max}=\ell_r$, equivalently outsider loss equal to maximum
gain.

The exact audit searches the complete menu at all $89$ ordered saturated
archive roots and both sharp orientations.  Every root has an aligned
co-star row, and every root has one that is moreover common and lies in
the Lmin tight support.  This explains the uniform unit exchange rate: the
archived equality is exactly the co-existence of both memberships in one
row.  In the sharp orientations every gainer has maximum gain and each of
the $g$ aligned rows is common and Lmin-tight, matching the shared-hinge
geometry of $G(p,k)$.  The four clean co-star rows of the eight-player
relaxed control all carry Lmax slack $7/8$, so none is aligned there and
the penalty is positive.  At this stage the working frontier was one
selection statement---an Lmax-tight aligned co-star row at some
maximum-gain gainer, with the reserve-padded (PXC69c) as fallback; the
next subsection refutes the universal existence of both.

Half of that localized statement is in fact unconditional, and the other
half is false.  For any gainer $a$ and any Lmin-tight rooted co-star row
$C$ omitting exactly $a$, core feasibility of the Lmax selector on the
$x$-tight row gives, by the coordinate expansion of (PXC70a),
\[
 \boxed{\qquad
 z(C)-x(C)=(D-d_a)-\ell_r-
 \sum_{j\in C\cap O}(x_j-z_j)\ \ge\ 0,
 \qquad}                                             \tag{PXC71}
\]
so $D-d_a$ exceeds $\ell_r$ by at least the strict decoration loss of the
row.  Saturation supplies a positive-weight row of every omission type,
so on the entire ordered saturated arm
\[
 D-d_{\max}\ \ge\ \ell_r,\qquad
 \tau_{\rm menu}(a^*)\ \ge\ \frac{D-d_{\max}}{\ell_r}\ \ge\ 1.
\]
The menu-relaxed root price is never below one, and the following are
equivalent at an ordered saturated root: the unit-exchange bound
$D-d_{\max}\le\ell_r$; the exact equality $D-d_{\max}=\ell_r$; existence
of an aligned co-star row; commonness, with tied decorations, of every
positive-weight maximum-gain packet row; and $\tau_{\rm menu}=1$.
Equality collapses (PXC71) on every maximum-type row simultaneously,
which explains the archive: all $202$ positive-weight maximum-gain packet
rows across the $89$ ordered saturated roots are common aligned rows, and
the uniform unit exchange rate has a trivially proved half.

The open half fails at seven players.  An exact design search over
singleton-normalized lower envelopes of integer generators with a common
total produced two permanent controls, both with exactly certified
selectors.  The lower envelope of $(2,0,6,2,0,2,0)$ and $(0,4,0,0,4,0,4)$
has $x=(4/3,4/3,4,4/3,4/3,4/3,4/3)$ and
$z=(4/5,12/5,12/5,4/5,12/5,4/5,12/5)$.  Root $2$ is ordered and saturated
with gainers $\{1,4,6\}$, $D-d_{\max}=32/15>8/5=\ell_r$, no aligned
co-star row, and $\tau_{\rm menu}=4/3$; the cover is still
exchange-exact, and the decoration reserve pays (PXC69c) with margin
$9/5$.  The lower envelope of $(0,7,2,0,1,2,0)$, $(4,0,0,5,0,0,3)$, and
$(0,11,1,0,0,0,0)$ is sharper: root $1$ is ordered and saturated with
$x=(2,4,1,2,1,1,1)$, $z=(14/5,14/5,3/5,14/5,3/5,3/5,9/5)$, reverse
penalty $D-d_{\max}-\ell_r=2/5$, and $\tau_{\rm menu}=2$, strictly above
the demand ratio $4/3$: menu exchange-exactness fails, and the menu-cover
condition (PXC69c) fails with margin $-37/60$.  Both controls satisfy the
raw dispersed-gain reserve (PXC63b), with margins $9/5$ and $11/60$, and
both lie far above the hybrid target ($O=44/5$ and $48/5$ against $7$).
Hence the six-player uniform equality, universal aligned rows, and menu
exchange-exactness are small-dimension artifacts.  Among the
saturated-arm certificates only the reserve-padded dispersed-gain
condition (PXC63b) survived this round of archived and designed
controls; it too fell to the extended search below, and both witnesses
join the mandatory hostile battery for any future saturated-arm lemma.

That reinstatement is also too optimistic.  First, (PXC71) pins the common
rows exactly: for a common co-star row of omission type $a$, the vanishing
left side gives
\[
 \boxed{\qquad
 \sum_{j\in C\cap O}(x_j-z_j)=(D-d_a)-\ell_r.
 \qquad}                                             \tag{PXC72}
\]
The strict decoration loss of every common single-omission row is
therefore not a selectable quantity but a scalar invariant of its type.
Consequently the common-row bounds of (PXC55) and (PXC59) close a type-$a$
row exactly when $\mu A_\rho$ plus the cardinality reserve covers
$D-d_a-\ell_r$: every packet-side closure on this arm---(PXC55), (PXC59),
(PXC63b), (PXC67b), (PXC69c)---is governed by the single scalar family
$D-d_a-\ell_r$ against the single reserve $\mu A_\rho+{\rm slack}$.

An extended exact design search then refutes that last reserve condition
as well; its seeded drivers and complete progress logs are archived in
\texttt{verifiers/divergence/search\_archive}.  Over $4{,}624$
singleton-normalized integer lower envelopes with certified selectors ($2{,}034$ divergent pairs, $4{,}269$ candidate
ceiling roots, $109$ ordered saturated roots), every ordered
no-multi-omission root was saturated, $99$ of the $109$ roots satisfy
alignment and equality, and seven violate (PXC63b).  Three pinned controls
summarize the boundary.  The mutation control, the lower envelope of
$(2,0,7,2,0,1,0)$ and $(0,4,0,0,4,0,4)$, has
$x=(4/3,4/3,13/3,4/3,4/3,1,4/3)$ and the same Lmax point as the first
control: its root $2$ has (PXC63b) margin $-1/5$, (PXC69c) margin
$-29/45$, no aligned row, and all three of its common co-star rows carry
the pinned loss $1/5$, so (PXC55) and the canonical selection-free
evaluation of (PXC59) fail as well.  It is a genuine positive-floor
residual---neither direct, ceiling, nor null-preclosed---whose overlap
$44/5$ still clears the target $7$: no selection-free packet certificate
of this arm closes it.  The chosen-family evaluation of (PXC59) does
close it, as (PXC76) below records.  The clean control, the
lower envelope of $(0,3,0,0,0,3,6)$ and $(1,0,1,2,8,0,0)$, violates
(PXC63b) by $5/7$ with no decorations, zero cardinality reserve, and no
Lmax-tight top-omission row at all; it is direct-closed.  The boundary
control, the lower envelope of $(0,7,2,0,1,2,0)$, $(3,0,0,5,1,0,3)$, and
$(0,11,1,0,0,0,0)$, sits at exact margin zero in both (PXC63b) and
(PXC69c) with $\tau_{\rm menu}=1$ and an aligned row, so any corrected
reserve statement must be equality-compatible with it.  All three retain
the hybrid target.  Hence (PXC63b) is not universal either, and no selection-free
packet reserve can be: closing the arm requires selection---of a chosen
decorated family (PXC76), of a rooted Lmax multi-omission row, separator,
hinge over-cover, or mixed facet in the (PXC66)--(PXC68) handover, or of
the spectral certificate---and at the time of this refutation the
mutation control was the sharpest outstanding residual.

The coloured certificate that closes the mutation control is already in
the program: the sharpened spectral-anchor bound of the sparse first-stage
Lmax over-cover reaches $127T/180$ there, above the target $7T/12$, with
dispersion parameter $\theta=2/5$.  This is not an accident of one
instance.  Write the packet margin for the reserve condition (PXC63b) and
the spectral margin for the sharpened anchor bound minus the target.  On
every audited and designed ordered saturated root the two are
complementary:
\[
 \boxed{\qquad
 \max\bigl({\rm packet\ margin},\ {\rm spectral\ margin}\bigr)\ \ge\ 0.
 \qquad}                                             \tag{PXC73}
\]
On the six-player archive the reserve closes all $89$ roots while the
spectral bound fails on $20$ of them, with margin down to $-2T/21$; every
one of those failures is a two-gainer root paid exactly by the normalized
cardinality slack $T/12$.  On the designed side the roles reverse: in the
two-generator family with uniform gainer weights, heavy root, and integer
outsider weights, the packet violation grows with the dimension (from
$-1/5$ at $n=7$ through $-3/7$ at $n=8$ and $-1$ at $n=9$ to $-4/3$ at
$n=11$ on the audited grid) while the spectral margin rescues every
instance with slack growing even as a fraction of the total ($7/45$
against $-2/45$ at $n=11$); the doubled-light equality member persists at
$n=11$ and $n=12$, while its even-dimensional $n=10$ sibling leaves the
equality surface.  The boundary is two-sided: the audited grid also
contains packet-tight instances whose spectral margin is strictly
negative (margins $0$ against $-1/12$ and $-1/6$), and hill-climbing
toward combined failure from those mirror seeds again plateaus at zero
packet margin, even while the spectral deficit deepens.  Both failure
directions are walled at the boundary.  The two margins coincide exactly
on part of the closing region ($25$ of the $89$ archive games, and on
part of the audited family grid); since both margins share the true
overlap, that coincidence is equivalent to the co-star bound's
unexplained slack equalling the spectral top-selection and label currency
total, and it is a partial-regime phenomenon, not a law.
Hill-climbing the combined failure margin from five independent seeds
plateaus at the exact double boundary: both margins vanish together, and
every audited double-boundary instance---including the all-unit and
doubled-light members of the parametric family and both plateau
witnesses---has overlap exactly equal to the hybrid target and realizes
the scaled sharp-family selector profile, one heavy Lmin player at
$T-(n-1)$ over a unit floor against the two-stratum Lmax point, in game
realizations outside $G(p,k)$ itself.
One plateau witness is a single unit move away from the mutation control:
the equality surface of the conjecture and the total failure of the packet
arm are adjacent in design space.  These are exact finite calculations and
a design-search law, not a theorem.  The spectral-or-reserve alternative
(PXC73) remains the governing empirical law of the arm, with the sharp
profile as its equality boundary; its open half is proved below on the
regimes of (PXC75) and (PXC78), and the consolidated statement is the
selection conjecture (PXC79) of the saturated-arm summary.

That open half is a theorem under the Lmax geometry the designed
violations actually exhibit.  Call an ordered saturated ceiling root
\emph{two-stratum} if $z_r=z_a=M$ for every gainer and $z_j=\zeta$ for
every outsider, with $M>\zeta\ge0$, and \emph{co-star fed} if every
decorated co-star $\{r\}\cup(G\setminus\{a\})\cup\{j\}$ is Lmax-tight.
Put $\Delta=gk-g-1$, where $k=n-1-g\ge2$ is the outsider count.  On the
menu of a co-star fed root the uniform weights
$\lambda_0=1/(g\Delta)$ on the $gk$ decorated co-stars satisfy the
first-stage over-cover system exactly, with ambient level
$\eta=g\lambda_0$ and top weights $\alpha_a=(gk-k-g)\lambda_0$ and
$\alpha_r=g(k-1)\lambda_0$ summing to one; hence the canonical
dispersion obeys
\[
 \theta\ \le\ \frac{k-1}{\Delta},                     \tag{PXC75a}
\]
with equality whenever no other menu row helps.  Now let $f=\min x$.
Every outsider is a nongainer, so its Lmin value is at least its Lmax
value $\zeta$; orderedness lifts every gainer above every outsider, and
the ceiling root has $x_r\ge M$; hence $f\ge\zeta$.  Because every non-top Lmax value equals $\zeta$ and every
Lmin value is at least $\zeta$, the residual of every candidate top
block is exactly $k\zeta$.  Assume every nonroot Lmin value is at most
$M$.  An eligible block $P$ satisfies
$\sum_{i\in P}(x_i-f)\ge(M+s^*-f)/\theta\ge(M-f)\Delta/(k-1)$ by
(PXC75a) and the nonnegative over-cover surplus; a rooted block then
pays at least $M+k\zeta$ plus the eligible remainder, and a rootless
block pays at least the same plus $x_r-M\ge0$.  In both cases
\[
 \boxed{\qquad
 B\ \ge\ \frac{(M-f)\Delta}{k-1}+(g+1)f+k\zeta+M-x_r
 \ \ge\ \frac{(M-\zeta)\Delta}{k-1}+n\zeta+M-x_r,
 \qquad}                                             \tag{PXC75}
\]
the second step from $f\ge\zeta$ and the positive $f$-coefficient
$(g+1)-\Delta/(k-1)=k/(k-1)$.  In particular a two-stratum co-star fed
root reaches the hybrid target whenever
\[
 x_r\ \le\ M+\frac{(M-\zeta)\Delta}{k-1}+n\zeta
 -\frac{nT}{\lfloor n^2/4\rfloor}.                    \tag{PXC75b}
\]
This is the coloured closure the packet arm cannot supply, and it is
dimension-free: no packing, decoration, or reserve enters.

The uniform outsider stratum is moreover only a normal form.  If the
outsiders split into several Lmax strata with values $\zeta_j<M$ and
minimum $\zeta_{\min}$, then the non-top Lmax multiset is fixed, so for
every candidate block the residual is at least
$z(O)-\sum_j(\zeta_j-f)_+$, a block- and pairing-independent bound, and
the identical argument gives
\[
 B\ \ge\ \frac{(M-f)\Delta}{k-1}+(g+1)f
 +z(O)-\sum_j(\zeta_j-f)_+ +M-x_r
 \ \ge\ \frac{(M-\zeta_{\min})\Delta}{k-1}+n\zeta_{\min}+M-x_r,
\]
so (PXC75b) holds verbatim with $\zeta_{\min}$ in place of $\zeta$.  The
exact audit checks the hypotheses---uniform top stratum, Lmax-tight
decorated co-stars, nonroot Lmin values at most $M$---instance by
instance on a fixed family grid: $37$ roots qualify ($27$ with uniform
outsiders), and the chain closes all $37$, including all $16$
packet-reserve violations in the grid and a battery of nine named
violation controls with minimum margin $7/10$; the weaker (PXC75b)
closes $26$.  The mutation control closes at $23/3$ against target $7$,
and the three-stratum $(5,5,5;8;(4,2,1))$ member is now covered.  The
canonical dispersion attains (PXC75a) with equality on $32$ of the $37$
roots; the strict cases exploit rootless extra menu rows, which only
sharpen the bound.  The theorem is genuinely complementary to the
archive: none of the $89$ archived ordered saturated roots satisfies its
hypotheses ($19$ have split tops and $70$ lack a fed co-star), yet all
are packet-closed, so on the archive the two regimes are disjoint and the
packet side suffices.  Packet failure does not force the hypotheses: the clean
undecorated control has uneven gainer weights, hence a split top, and
its designed relabellings recur in random search as packet violations
outside the theorem, each still spectral-closed with margin $2$.  What
remains of the general spectral-or-reserve conjecture is therefore the
split-top branch, unfed co-stars, and the archived two-gainer spectral
failures.

The audit discipline then reverses the necessity conclusion once more.
The decorated family in (PXC59) is a selectable object: each omission
type may use any $x$-tight decorated co-star, subject only to the
outsider load cap, and by (PXC72) the aligned common row's decoration
loss is pinned at $D-d_{a^*}-\ell_r$ independently of its decorations.
The strongest packet-side certificate on the arm is therefore the exact
finite maximum
\[
 \boxed{\qquad
 \max_{\{K_a\}}\ \Bigl(\mu A+{\rm slack}\Bigr)
 -\bigl(D-d_{a^*}-\ell_r\bigr),
 \qquad}                                             \tag{PXC76}
\]
over admissible families with one aligned common row at a $z$-maximal
gainer---a pure Lmin-side packing optimization.  Enumerating all
admissible families exactly closes every named uniform-family
packet-reserve violation: the mutation control closes at margin $29/30$,
through the family $K_1=K_4=\varnothing$, $K_6=\{5\}$ and its
enlargements, and the named battery closes with margins between $29/30$
and $27/10$.  Only the split-top clean control leaves the certificate's
hypotheses---it has no aligned common row at all---and it is
direct-closed.  Hence no audited root forces the spectral arm: the
refutations of (PXC70)--(PXC72) establish the failure of the
selection-free packet certificates, not of the packet colour itself, and
the mutation control is no longer an outstanding residual.  The
selection problem in its consolidated five-mechanism form is stated as
(PXC79) in the saturated-arm summary.  The disjunction (PXC73) and the two-stratum
theorem stand as stated, with the spectral arm a proved alternative
rather than a forced necessity, and the split-top clean family remains
the one violation class outside every packet-side hypothesis.

Two further exact facts sharpen that remaining class.  Across every
audited packet violation---the sixteen grid violations of the
two-stratum audit and the ten archived search generator classes---
top-uniformity and co-star feeding coincide: eighteen violations are
top-uniform and fed, eight are split-top and unfed, and no mixed class
occurs, so the two (PXC75) hypotheses stand or fall together and the
remaining branch of (PXC73) is one, not two.  And the split-top branch
now has an exact structural atlas (PXC77): the heavy gainer joins the
root at the ceiling; the Lmax-tight menu is built from double co-stars
containing the root, the heavy gainer, all sub-top gainers but one, and
one outsider; the canonical over-cover concentrates its largest weight
on the heavy gainer, with dispersion $4/7$ and weights $(3/7,4/7)$ at
the canonical control; both spectral currencies vanish, so the sharpened
bound equals the actual-top bound; and the bound is exactly tight,
$B=O=9$ against target $7$, at the control.  On the audited split-family
grid of $31$ split-top roots the sharpened bound closes every instance,
including both packet violations, and an archived extended grid of $75$
split-top roots closes as well; the double co-stars are Lmax-tight at
$11$ of the $31$, and the bound is tight at $14$.  Any split-top closure
theorem must therefore be sharp, and its over-cover is heavy-gainer
weighted rather than root weighted.  These are exact finite structure
facts; the single-heavy geometry now has its theorem.  Suppose the top
stratum is exactly $\{r,h\}$, the $s\ge2$ sub-top gainers share one
level and the $k\ge2$ outsiders sit below it, every double co-star and
the heavy-block row $\{h\}\cup O$ are Lmax-tight, and every nonroot
Lmin value is at most $M$.  Weighting the double co-stars uniformly by
$\lambda_0=1/(sk+k-s)$ and the heavy-block row by
$\nu=((s-1)k-s)\lambda_0\ge0$ satisfies the over-cover system exactly,
with ambient level $(s-1)k\lambda_0$ and top weights
$\alpha_r=k\lambda_0$, $\alpha_h=s(k-1)\lambda_0$, so the canonical
dispersion obeys
\[
 \theta\ \le\ \frac{s(k-1)}{sk+k-s},              \tag{PXC78a}
\]
the heavy weight dominating the root weight.  With $f=\min x$, the
non-top Lmax multiset bounds every residual by
$sf+\sum_j\min(\zeta_j,f)$, since $f$ lies below the sub-top level, and
the eligibility self-bounding argument of (PXC75) gives
\[
 \boxed{\qquad
 B\ \ge\ \frac{(M-f)(sk+k-s)}{s(k-1)}+(s+2)f
 +\sum_j\min(\zeta_j,f)+M-x_r.
 \qquad}                                             \tag{PXC78}
\]
On the audited grid the eleven class-A roots---exactly those carrying
both menu hypotheses---satisfy (PXC78a) with the canonical dispersion
attaining the bound, and the chain closes all eleven, including both
packet-reserve violations, with the canonical control at $31/4$ against
target $7$.  The class-B and class-C sub-geometries of the atlas remain
outside the theorem, but they carry no packet violation: all twenty
audited such roots are reserve-closed.  Every audited packet violation
therefore lies in a regime with a proved closure theorem---(PXC75) for
the uniform top, (PXC78) for the single-heavy split top, with the
chosen-family certificate (PXC76) closing the uniform side
independently---so on all audited data the outstanding universal
problem is selection alone: prove that an ordered saturated ceiling
root always verifiably enters one of the proved regimes or the direct
arm.  The two named hostile controls moreover carry three independent
proved closures apiece: the exact mixed-witness projection of their
labelled branches returns branch minima $11/15$ and $16/25$ against the
target ratio $7/12$, each equal to its one-witness minimum after a
single cut.

\subsection*{Saturated-arm summary}

The preceding development corrected itself repeatedly; this summary
states what survives, in one place and with full hypotheses.  Throughout,
$r$ is an ordered saturated ceiling root of an exact singleton-normalized
selector pair $x\ne z$: $z_r=M=\max_iz_i$, $x_r\ge M$, $r$ is not a
gainer, the gainer set $G$ has $g\ge2$ members, no Lmin-tight rooted row
omits two gainers, the rooted packing is saturated at $g/(g-1)$, and
$x_r\ge x_a\ge x_j$ for gainers $a$ and outsiders $j$; there are
$k=n-1-g$ outsiders, $d_a=z_a-x_a$, $D=\sum_ad_a$, $\ell_r=x_r-z_r$, and
$f=\min_ix_i$.

Four dimension-free theorems stand.  \emph{Reverse inequality} (PXC71):
core feasibility of $z$ on any Lmin-tight co-star row omitting exactly
$a$ gives $z(C)-x(C)=(D-d_a)-\ell_r-{\rm strictloss}(C)\ge0$; saturation
supplies rows of every type, so $D-d_{\max}\ge\ell_r$; together with
the staged pinning inequality of (PXC65) the root loss is strictly
positive, and the menu-relaxed root price is never below one;
moreover the unit bound, the exact equality, aligned-row existence,
commonness with tied decorations of every maximum-gain packet row, and
$\tau_{\rm menu}=1$ are all equivalent.  \emph{Loss invariant} (PXC72):
a common co-star row of type $a$ has strict decoration loss exactly
$D-d_a-\ell_r$, so every packet-side reserve condition is one scalar
family against the reserve $\mu A_\rho+{\rm slack}$.  \emph{Prefix
lemma} (PXC74): a weighted-Hall violation has total gainer deficit below
one, so away from boundary ties its tangent is strictly
Lorenz-improving and Lmin optimality forces a root-free or all-gainer
rooted blocker.  \emph{Spectral closures} (PXC75, PXC78): if the Lmax
top stratum is $\{r\}\cup G$ and every decorated co-star is Lmax-tight,
then $\theta\le(k-1)/(gk-g-1)$ and
$B\ge(M-f)(gk-g-1)/(k-1)+(g+1)f+z(O)-\sum_j(\zeta_j-f)_++M-x_r$; if
instead the top stratum is exactly $\{r,h\}$ with $s\ge2$ sub-top
gainers on one level, $k\ge2$ outsiders below it, and both the double
co-stars and the heavy-block row $\{h\}\cup O$ Lmax-tight, then
$\theta\le s(k-1)/(sk+k-s)$ and
$B\ge(M-f)(sk+k-s)/(s(k-1))+(s+2)f+\sum_j\min(\zeta_j,f)+M-x_r$; both
under the harmless normalization that every nonroot Lmin value is at
most $M$.

Six closure mechanisms are proved, each with instance-checkable
hypotheses:

\begin{center}
\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{lll}
mechanism & hypotheses & audited closures \\[2pt]
packet reserve (PXC63b) & none beyond the arm & all $89$ archive roots \\
direct packet & direct-arm criterion & $18$ archive roots; clean control \\
chosen family (PXC76) & aligned common row at a $z$-maximal gainer &
every audited uniform violation \\
spectral chain (PXC75) & uniform top, fed co-stars &
$37/37$ audited, $16$ violations \\
spectral chain (PXC78) & single-heavy top, double-fed, heavy block &
$11/11$ audited, $2$ violations \\
branch projection & complete tight supports &
both named control branches \\
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

The open problem of the arm is the selection conjecture: every ordered
saturated ceiling root is closed by a mechanism whose hypotheses it
verifiably satisfies,
\[
 \boxed{\qquad
 {\rm reserve}\ \vee\ {\rm direct}\ \vee\
 {\rm (PXC75\ hypotheses)}\ \vee\ {\rm (PXC78\ hypotheses)}\ \vee\
 {\rm (chosen\ family)}.
 \qquad}                                             \tag{PXC79}
\]
The reserve disjunct is essential: none of the $89$ archived roots
satisfies a spectral hypothesis set, yet all are reserve-closed.  The
evidence is exact and complete over the audited landscape: every audited
packet violation is either uniform-top and co-star fed ($18$, closed by
(PXC75) and independently by (PXC76)) or single-heavy split-top and
unfed ($8$, all class-A, closed by (PXC78), with the clean control also
direct-closed), and no mixed class occurs; the feeding implication
``violation and uniform top force fed co-stars'' held at $56$ of $56$
within two- and three-generator envelopes but fails in wider ones, as
recorded below;
the class-B/C split-top sub-geometries carry no violation at all.  One
step of the feeding implication is a theorem: evaluating the (PXC71)
identity on a clean co-star or on the full top block shows that at a
packet violation neither is ever Lmax-tight---$z$-tightness of a clean
type-$a$ row forces $D-d_a\le\ell_r$, hence $a$ maximum-gain and the
penalty nonpositive, and $z$-tightness of $\{r\}\cup G$ forces
$D\le\ell_r$---so every Lmax-tight type row at a violation is
decorated; the implication asks that all single-decoration rows be
among them.  A scope caution stands, in corrected form:
every singleton-normalized exact game is the lower envelope of its core
witnesses, which are nonnegative, so monotonicity is automatic and is no
restriction at all; the genuine narrowness of the designed evidence is
the generator count---two or three generators against up to
exponentially many witnesses in general---and the implication has not
been probed against envelopes of more generators.  The
boundary data any proof of (PXC79) must respect are the plateau
witnesses (both certificate margins and the target margin vanish
together, on the scaled sharp selector profile), the archived two-gainer
spectral failures (paid exactly by the normalized cardinality slack
$T/12$), and the zero-margin boundary control.

Historically, this summary replaces a chain of six sharper universal
statements---the unit root-exchange bound, universal aligned rows, menu
exchange-exactness, the reserve conditions (PXC69c) and (PXC63b) as
universal closures, and the necessity of coloured selection---each
refuted in turn by exact designed controls that remain pinned in the
verifiers and the protected-results ledger; the six-player census
uniformities that suggested them are small-dimension artifacts.  The
surviving structure is the two-regime complementarity above.

The wide probe promptly exercised this summary.  Every
singleton-normalized exact game is the lower envelope of its
nonnegative core witnesses, so the designed evidence's real narrowness
was generator count; seeded envelopes of four to eight generators---
perturbed copies of the violation shapes---immediately refute the
feeding implication ($185$ counterexamples in the first archived run:
uniform-top violations whose co-stars of one omission type are all
Lmax-slack) and then (PXC79) as first stated: fourteen roots, all with
the selector profile $x=(9/2,5/4,\dots,5/4)$ against the familiar
two-stratum Lmax point, fail the reserve, the direct arm, the fully fed
hypotheses, the (PXC78) hypotheses, and the chosen-family certificate
simultaneously, the last because no common aligned row exists at all.
The repair costs nothing.  Suppose, in the uniform-top setting of
(PXC75), only that the decorated co-stars of at least two omission
types are Lmax-tight, say of the types in $F\subseteq G$ with
$|F|\ge2$.  Weighting those $|F|k$ rows uniformly satisfies the
over-cover system with ambient level $|F|\lambda_0$ and top weights
$|F|(k-1)\lambda_0$ on the root and every unfed gainer and
$(|F|k-k-|F|)\lambda_0\ge0$ on each fed gainer, and the normalizing
denominator collapses by the identity
$(k-1)(g-|F|+1)+|F|k-k-|F|=g(k-1)-1$, independently of $|F|$:
\[
 \boxed{\qquad
 \theta\ \le\ \frac{k-1}{g(k-1)-1}
 \quad\hbox{for every }|F|\ge2,
 \qquad}                                             \tag{PXC80}
\]
the same dispersion bound as the fully fed case, so the entire (PXC75)
chain holds verbatim under the weaker hypothesis.  The chain closes the
escape profile at $327/40$ and the archived feeding counterexample at
$239/30$, both against target $7$.  Accordingly the selection
conjecture (PXC79) is amended in place: its fully fed disjunct is
replaced by the partially fed hypotheses of (PXC80), and no audited
root escapes the amended disjunction.

Even the partially fed hypothesis is only a sufficient shape.  Let
$E\subseteq G\times O$ be the \emph{tightness graph} of decorated
pairs, $(a,j)\in E$ when $C_{a,j}$ is Lmax-tight.  Any over-cover
supported on such rows gives every outsider the common ambient level
$\eta$ and satisfies the normalization exactly, and writing $L$ for
the total row weight these force $L=k\eta$ and $(g+1)(L-\eta)=1+L$,
hence $\eta=1/(g(k-1)-1)$ and
\[
 \theta_{\rm cover}\ =\ L-\eta\ =\ \frac{k-1}{g(k-1)-1}
\]
for \emph{every} pair-supported cover: the shared dispersion of
(PXC75) and (PXC80) is not a coincidence but the unique value on this
support class.  Existence is therefore a pure transportation question
with demand one per outsider, supply exactly $k-1$ per used type, and
arcs $E$; Gale's criterion over column subsets collapses, because a
proper subset has at most $k-1$ columns and needs only one reachable
type, while the full column set needs two:
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \text{the cover exists}\\
 \Longleftrightarrow\ \text{every outsider lies in}\\
 \text{some tight pair}\\
 \text{and at least two omission types appear}
 \end{gathered}                                      \tag{PXC81}
\]
This is strictly weaker than two fully fed types, the characterization
is verified by exhaustion over all bipartite shapes with
$g,k\le4$ in the pinned verifier, and the (PXC75) chain holds verbatim
under it.  The selection conjecture is amended a second time: its
feeding disjunct is now the pair-graph condition (PXC81), and the
audited pair graphs already exercise the extra freedom, with partial
types occurring alongside full ones.  The condition itself is not
universal at violations: three grid instances---uniform-top violations
whose only tight pairs decorate the single heavy outsider---fail it and
are closed by the direct arm and the chosen family, with chosen-family
margins $219/70$ and $8/3$.  It is the disjunction, not any single
disjunct, that carries the conjecture.  An extended family grid
($g\le5$, $a\le8$, $n\le9$) sharpens this: of $508$ uniform-top
violations, $89$ fail the condition, eleven of those escape the direct
arm, and three---the first such on the two-generator family---escape
the chosen family as well, carrying the rooted-triple menu of the
wide-probe escapes; all three close under the rooted-menu chain
(PXC83) with surplus $1/2$ and chain $131/6$ against target $56/3$,
and one is pinned exactly.  The same grid refutes the floor law: an
uncovered outsider can sit strictly above the Lmin floor.

The same support-class analysis closes a door on the split top.  In the
single-heavy split-top geometry of (PXC78), consider covers supported on
the fed double co-stars---the rows of $t$ of the $s$ sub-top omitted
types---together with the heavy-block row.  An unfed sub-top type $u$
lies in every fed row and not in the heavy block, so the ambient
equality gives $u$ the full row weight $L$, the same as the root's
coverage; hence $\alpha_r=0$, each fed type's weight is $L-\eta=0$,
so $L=\eta=0$ and the normalization $\alpha_r+\alpha_h=1$ fails:
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \text{no pair-supported cover exists}\\
 \text{on a split top with an unfed sub-top type;}\\
 \text{feasible iff }t=s\text{ and }(s-1)k\ge s
 \end{gathered}                                      \tag{PXC82}
\]
The dichotomy is verified by exact linear programming over
$2\le s\le5$, $2\le k\le4$, $1\le t\le s$ in the pinned split-top
verifier.  The collapsing denominator of (PXC80) is therefore special
to the uniform top, where unfed types sit in the top stratum and absorb
their over-coverage as top weight; any class-B split-top closure must
leave the pair support entirely.

The probe then delivered a third refutation, deeper than the first
two.  An eight-generator envelope on eight players produces a
uniform-top packet violation, with the familiar escape profile
$x=(11/2,3/2,\dots,3/2)$ against the two-stratum Lmax point
$z=(8/3^{\,\times5},8/9^{\,\times3})$, whose Lmax-tight menu contains
\emph{no decorated co-star at all}: the tight rows are exactly the five
rooted triples $\{r,a,b\}$ with $a,b$ gainers.  The pair graph is
empty, so reserve, the direct arm, the pair-graph condition, the
split-top hypotheses, and the chosen family all fail simultaneously---
the selection conjecture as amended is refuted a third time, now
because the menu itself leaves the decorated-co-star class.  The
spectral certificate is untouched: the canonical over-cover on the
triple menu has dispersion $\theta=1/3$ with ambient level zero, the
sharpened bound closes at $34/3$ against target $8$, and the
spectral-or-reserve disjunction (PXC73) survives every refutation
round to date.  The witness is pinned exactly, menu included, in the
partially fed verifier.  The sixth mechanism exists, and its proof is three lines,
because over-cover feasibility on a rooted menu is trivial.  Suppose
the top stratum is uniform, every nonroot Lmin value is at most $M$,
and some rooted undecorated row $\{r\}\cup P$ with
$\emptyset\ne P\subsetneq G$, $|P|=p$, is Lmax-tight.  Weighting that
row alone satisfies the over-cover system with ambient level zero and
$\alpha_r=\alpha_a=1/(p+1)$ on the row members, so
\[
 \theta\ \le\ \frac{1}{p+1},                          \tag{PXC83a}
\]
and if the row can be chosen with nonnegative surplus
$x_r-M\ge\sum_{a\in P}(M-x_a)$, the (PXC75) chain---which uses only
the dispersion, the nonnegative surplus, the residual bound, and the
caps, never the support---holds verbatim:
\[
 \boxed{\qquad
 B\ \ge\ (p+1)(M-f)+(g+1)f
 +z(O)-\sum_j(\zeta_j-f)_+ +M-x_r.
 \qquad}                                             \tag{PXC83}
\]
At a packet violation no clean row is tight, so $p\le g-2$ there and
the mechanism is disjoint in support from the pair-graph regime.  The
chain closes the pinned escape exactly: the surplus is $1/2$ on any
tight triple, the dispersion bound $1/3$ is attained, and the chain
pays $65/6$ against target $8$, under the certificate value $34/3$.
The selection conjecture is amended a third time, its mechanism list
now six: reserve, direct, the pair-graph condition, the split-top
hypotheses, the chosen family, and the rooted-menu hypotheses of
(PXC83); the completed design search around the escape closes every
rooted menu ($4{,}666$ of $4{,}666$), and the completed wide probes
produce four further escapes of the five pre-(PXC83) mechanisms---all
rooted-triple menus, all closed by the (PXC83) chain out of sample
(surpluses $1/2$ and $5/4$, chains $65/6$ and $149/12$).  The probes
also produce the first ordered-but-unsaturated ceiling roots ever
observed (seven, rooted packing value $5/4$ against the saturation
value $4/3$ at $g=4$, every one target-safe with overlap $50/3$
against $10$; one pinned): the ordered geometry does not force
saturation, and the saturated-arm theorems are scope-bounded by the
saturation hypothesis.

The singleton-cover principle behind (PXC83) is in fact
stratum-agnostic, and it is what closes class B.  Let $T$ be the full
Lmax top stratum.  Any Lmax-tight row $R$ with $r\in R\subseteq T$ is
by itself an over-cover with ambient level zero and uniform weights, so
\[
 \theta\ \le\ \frac{1}{|R|},                          \tag{PXC84a}
\]
and with the surplus hypothesis $\sum_{i\in R}x_i\ge|R|M$ and the
nonroot Lmin cap, the (PXC75) argument---dispersion, nonnegative
surplus, the block- and pairing-independent residual bound, block size
$|T|$---gives the master chain
\[
 \boxed{\qquad
 B\ \ge\ |R|(M-f)+|T|f
 +z(N\setminus T)-\hskip-4pt\sum_{i\in N\setminus T}\hskip-4pt(z_i-f)_+
 +M-x_r,
 \qquad}                                             \tag{PXC84}
\]
of which the (PXC75), (PXC78), and (PXC83) chains are the uniform,
split-top, and rooted-menu instances.  On the split top this reads:
if the top pair $\{r,h\}$ is Lmax-tight, then $\theta\le1/2$ and, with
$x_r+x_h\ge2M$, the chain closes without any feeding or heavy-block
hypothesis---exactly the class-B geometry (PXC82) proved unreachable
by pair-supported covers.  The design search that found class B
confirms the mechanism: every observed split-top violation with a
slack heavy-block row has the top pair Lmax-tight ($41$ of $41$), the
first such instance is pinned exactly (dispersion $1/2$, surplus $1$,
chain $29/2$ against target $35/3$ under certificate $31/2$), and the
class-B closure conjecture---at a single-heavy split-top violation the
class-A hypotheses hold or the top pair is tight---joins the amended
selection disjunction.

One universal structural fact about the Lmax point itself emerged from
these hunts and is worth recording.  At the Lmax point, whenever
$z_i>z_j$ there is a z-tight coalition containing $i$ and avoiding
$j$: otherwise the transfer of $\varepsilon$ from $i$ to $j$ stays in
