the core---only coalitions containing $i$ without $j$ lose, and all
are slack---and lexicographically improves the sorted vector, since
the entry at $i$'s position falls while everything above it is
unchanged.                                             \hfill(PXC85)
The mirror holds at the Lmin point with the roles reversed.  Between
players at a common value no separation is forced---transfers between
equal tops worsen the egalitarian objective in both directions---and
the pinned menus realize this freedom: the triple-menu escape has no
tight row containing a gainer and avoiding the root.  The lemma
bounds every future mechanism from below: the tight menu must
separate every strictly ordered pair, so a violation's menu always
carries at least the separating family.

There is now a human explanation for the class-A/class-B handover on the
continuous family through the pinned class-B control.  Replace only its
fourth generator by
\[
 q^3(t)=(0,9-t,6,5+t,0,0,0),\qquad 0\le t\le1,
\]
leaving the other five generators displayed in the exact verifier.  The
lower selector is constant, $x=(6,4,2,2,2,2,2)$, while the upper selector is
\[
 z(t)=
 \begin{cases}
 ((9-t)/2,(9-t)/2,4+2t,4+2t,1-t,1-t,1-t),&0\le t\le1/7,\\
 (31/7,31/7,30/7,30/7,6/7,6/7,6/7),&1/7\le t\le1.
 \end{cases}                                         \tag{PXC86a}
\]
This is not a sampled formula.  Three fixed lower rows, after
complementation, successively pin the five coordinates at level $2$, the
coordinate at level $4$, and the remaining coordinate at level $6$.  On the
first upper branch $v(\{r,h\})=9-t$ pins both top coordinates; summing the six
double-co-star inequalities pins the two sub-top coordinates, and efficiency
then equalizes the outsiders.  On the second branch put
$R=y_r+y_h$, $A=y_a+y_b$, and $O=\sum_jy_j$.  The heavy-block inequality and
the same six double-co-star inequalities give
\[
 O\ge7-\max_i y_i,\qquad 4R+A\ge44,\qquad
 4R+A=3R+(R+A)\le13+7\max_i y_i,
\]
so $\max_i y_i\ge31/7$; equality pins $R$, then $A$, then $O$, giving the
second line of (PXC86a).  Full affine-envelope checking verifies core
membership on the entire two parameter intervals.  Consequently the two
relevant row gaps are exactly
\[
 2M-v(\{r,h\})=(t-1/7)_+,
 \qquad z(\{h\}\cup O)-v(\{h\}\cup O)=\tfrac12(1-7t)_+ .  \tag{PXC86}
\]
The packet margin is $-(1+t)$ before the breakpoint and $-8/7$ after it.
Thus (PXC84) closes the pair-tight class-B side, (PXC78) closes the
heavy-block-tight class-A side, and both apply at the breakpoint: the two
active sets meet with no local gap.  This continuous family theorem explains
why direct perturbations of the control hand over between the two mechanisms;
it does not prove the universal class-B closure conjecture.

That conjecture is in fact false, and its failure exposes a third split-top
support rather than a failure of the overlap bound.  Let \(P\) be the
nonnegative total-\(20\) polytope on players \(0,\ldots,6\) defined by
\[
\begin{gathered}
 y(0134)\ge14,\quad y(0125)\ge14,\quad y(16)\ge37/7,\\
 y(012)\ge12,\quad y(013)\ge12,\quad y(023)\ge10,\\
 y(0126)\ge13,\quad y(0136)\ge13,
\end{gathered}                                      \tag{PXC87a}
\]
and put \(v(S)=\min_{y\in P}y(S)\).  This is an exact game by construction,
and every singleton minimum is zero.  Its selectors are
\[
 x=(6,4,2,2,2,2,2),\qquad
 z=\tfrac17(31,31,23,23,13,13,6).                  \tag{PXC87b}
\]
There is a short proof independent of selector software.  Complements of
the tight lower rows \(012\) and \(013\) bound the minimum coordinate by two;
equality pins players \(2,\ldots,6\) at two, and efficiency together with
\(023\) then pins \((x_0,x_1)=(6,4)\).  For the upper selector, adding the
first three inequalities of (PXC87a) gives
\(20+y_0+2y_1\ge233/7\), so \(\max_i y_i\ge31/7\).
Equality pins \(y_0=y_1=31/7\) and all three rows; \(16\) pins \(y_6=6/7\),
the last two rows pin \(y_2=y_3=23/7\), and efficiency pins
\(y_4=y_5=13/7\).

The unique ordered saturated root has packet margin \(-1/7\), while the
top-pair and heavy-block gaps are respectively \(6/7\) and \(26/7\), and the
six double-co-star gaps are \((2,0,0,2,0,0)\).  Thus neither (PXC84) nor the
full (PXC78) menu applies, refuting the proposed ``class A or tight top pair''
selection.  The game is far from a counterexample to the main bound:
\(O(x,z)=17\) against target \(35/3\).

What replaces the failed dichotomy on this control is a
\emph{split-matching cover}.  The three tight rows \(0134,0125,16\), each
with weight \(1/3\), have ambient load \(1/3\) and top weights
\((\alpha_0,\alpha_1)=(1/3,2/3)\), hence
\[
 \theta\le\tfrac23.                                  \tag{PXC87}
\]
The incidence construction is dimension-free: for \(k\) outsiders, use one
tight double co-star of each omission type on distinct outsiders and a tight
heavy--outsider pair on every remaining outsider.  Uniform weight \(1/k\)
gives \(\eta=1/k\), top weights \((1/k,(k-1)/k)\), and
\(\theta\le(k-1)/k\).  This proves a seventh support mechanism, not its
universal selection or a universal target-closing chain.  The control is
already closed by the direct packet arm, so it does not prove that a seventh
closure arm is necessary.  Exact-LP replay and an independently formulated
\(80\)-vertex rational replay are pinned in the supplement.

The split-matching arm is nevertheless genuinely nonredundant.  In
(PXC87a), replace the first three lower bounds by
\[
 y(0134)\ge89/7,\qquad y(0125)\ge97/7,\qquad
 y(16)\ge41/7,
\]
replace the last two by $y(0126),y(0136)\ge97/7$, and leave the three
Lmin-pinning rows unchanged.  The same proofs give
\[
 x=(6,4,2,2,2,2,2),\qquad
 z=\tfrac17(29,29,27,27,4,12,12).                  \tag{PXC88}
\]
Here the packet margin is again $-1/7$, but exhaustive expansion of the
direct master criterion finds no target-reaching direct packet.  The
top-pair and heavy-block gaps are $4/7$ and $16/7$, while the six
double-co-star gaps are $(5/7,0,0,13/7,0,0)$.  Thus the pair, full
(PXC78), and direct arms all fail, whereas the same three split-matching
rows remain tight and carry the (PXC87) cover.  The overlap is $113/7$,
exceeding the target $35/3$ by $94/21$.  A second verifier enumerates
the $76$ core vertices with a local rational eliminator and independently
expands every direct-packet choice.  This proves nonredundancy of the
split-matching arm, not universal selection of it.

As a first bounded attack on selection, at the selector profile in (PXC88)
there are $65$ proper rows compatible with the comparison sign and $158$
positive
normalized top over-covers supported on exactly three rows.  Among the
$18$ covers whose rows all have size at least three and whose two top
weights are positive, exact minimal lifts leave only six ordered saturated
roots with singleton normalization.  All six land on packet
margin zero and make the top pair tight; the other twelve fail singleton
normalization.  Hence this complete three-row/profile cell contains no
escape.  The four-row census has $714$ positive-row-weight covers, of
which $336$ have both top coefficients positive and $168$ are all-large-row
lift candidates.  Of their exact minimal lifts, $156$ fail singleton
normalization and twelve are valid, but none of the
twelve is a packet violation.  Thus the fixed-profile escape requires at
least five rows.  At five rows, collapsing duplicate incidence columns and
the swaps of the two sub-top players and the two equal outsiders leaves
$720$ positive column covers, $924$ labelled covers, and $252$ orbits.  Of
the representative lifts, $250$ fail singleton normalization;
the remaining two are genuine packet violations, but both make the top pair
tight.  Hence no all-large-row fixed-profile support escape of size at most
five exists.  A zero-ambient cover on a two-player top can use only rows
inside that top; singleton normalization excludes the singleton rows, so it
would force the top pair tight.  Thus pair slack forces positive ambient
load.  With both top coefficients positive, the six equality rows then bound
an extreme support by five rows.  A zero top coefficient contributes one
additional active equation and can permit six rows.  The remaining
fixed-profile cells therefore contain two-player rows or six-row one-sided
top covers.  This is a finite incidence-and-lift theorem only: different
selector profiles and different Lmin certificates remain live.

The apparent one-sided-top exception can in fact be removed.  Suppose the
Lmax top is exactly $T=\{r,h\}$.  Lexicographic optimality excludes every
core-tangent direction with $d_r,d_h\le0$ and at least one strict inequality:
for sufficiently small steps the strict gap below $T$ is preserved and the
sorted ceiling vector improves.  The polyhedral Gordan--Stiemke alternative
therefore gives a normalized top over-cover with
$\alpha_r,\alpha_h>0$.  At a single-heavy split packet,
$x_r>M>x_h$, and the selector-payment inequality
\[
 \alpha_r(x_r-M)\ge\alpha_h(M-x_h)
\]
rules out $\alpha_r=0$ for every normalized top over-cover.  Decompose the
strictly positive cover into extreme covers.  Recession rays have zero top
weight, so some extreme cover has $\alpha_h>0$, and the payment inequality
also gives $\alpha_r>0$.  If the top pair is slack, its ambient load is
positive by the preceding zero-ambient argument.  After eliminating the two
top weights, the normalized incidence system has $n-1$ equality rows; an
extreme cover with positive ambient variable consequently uses at most
$n-2$ tight coalitions.                                      \hfill(PXC89)

For the seven-player coordinates in (PXC88), (PXC89) makes the incidence
census complete through the extreme-support bound rather than merely
all-large.  Allowing every nonsingleton comparison-compatible row gives, at
support sizes one through five,
respectively $1,6,66,312,2576$ strictly-positive-top labelled covers and
$1,2,20,96,719$ exact symmetry orbits.  At sizes one and two every orbit is
a valid packet-boundary lift with the top pair tight.  At sizes three and
four, respectively $16$ of $20$ and $92$ of $96$ representative minimal
lifts fail singleton normalization; every remaining lift retains either the
top-pair or split-matching support.  At size five, $717$ of $719$
representatives fail singleton normalization and the other two are genuine
packet violations, both with the top pair tight relative to their recomputed
selectors.  These are exact counts and a useful support atlas, but not a
fixed-profile completion theorem: the census recomputes each minimal lift's
selectors and does not assert that they remain the prescribed pair $(x,z)$
from (PXC88).  Singleton-normalization failure is completion-monotone, but a
mechanism tight at a changed minimal-lift selector need not be tight at the
prescribed $z$.  The failure is realized explicitly in (PXC92a).

Those other profiles do refute the corrected four-arm classification.  Let
$P$ be the nonnegative total-$20$ polytope cut out by the three Lmin rows
$y(012),y(013)\ge12$, $y(023)\ge10$, and
\[
\begin{gathered}
 y(0124),y(0134)\ge94/7,\qquad
 y(0125),y(0136)\ge95/7,\qquad y(1456)\ge55/7,
                                                               \tag{PXC90a}
\end{gathered}
\]
and define $v(S)=\min_{y\in P}y(S)$.  Then $v$ is exact and
singleton-normalized, with
\[
 x=(6,4,2,2,2,2,2),\qquad
 z=\tfrac17(29,29,28,28,8,9,9).                    \tag{PXC90b}
\]
The lower selector has the same short proof as (PXC87): the complement of
$012$ bounds the floor by two, equality and $013$ pin players $2,\ldots,6$,
and $023$ pins the remaining ordered pair.  For the upper selector, weight
the five rows in (PXC90a), in their displayed order, by
$(1,1,2,2,1)/7$.  Their incidence load is
\[
 \frac37\one_N+\frac37e_0+\frac47e_1,
 \qquad \text{and their weighted lower bound is }89/7.
\]
Thus every core point has maximum at least $29/7$.  Equality pins players
$0,1$ at $29/7$ and all five positive-weight rows; those equations with
efficiency uniquely give the displayed $z$.

The unique ceiling root is ordered and saturated, with one heavy top gainer,
two equal sub-top gainers, and packet margin $-2/7$.  Nevertheless the top
pair has gap $29/28$, the full (PXC78) menu fails because two double co-stars
have gap $11/7$, no (PXC87) split matching is tight, and exhaustive expansion
of the direct criterion returns false.  Hence
\[
 \boxed{\text{top pair}\ \vee\ \text{full (PXC78)}\ \vee\
 \ \text{(PXC87) matching}\ \vee\ \text{direct packet}}
                                                               \tag{PXC90}
\]
is false even for seven-player singleton-normalized exact games.  The paying
top dual is a new forked support: both omission types meet outsider $4$, one
continues to outsider $5$, the other to outsider $6$, and the tight heavy
block closes the outsider loads.  It has weights $(1,1,2,2,1)/7$, ambient
load $3/7$, and top weights $(3/7,4/7)$.  This refutes support selection, not
the main overlap bound: $O(x,z)=111/7$, exceeding $35/3$ by $88/21$.
An independent verifier enumerates all $84$ core vertices, reconstructs every
coalition minimum, checks both human selector certificates, and independently
expands the direct and matching predicates.

The exact boundary between the preceding theorem and its finite census can
now be stated without reference to a menu shape.  Let ${\cal C}_x$ be a
complete staged Lmin certificate at $x$, let $\mathcal R$ support a normalized
$z$-tight top over-cover, and let $P_{\mathcal R}$ be the polytope obtained from
efficiency and nonnegativity by retaining the rows in ${\cal C}_x\cup\mathcal R$
at their displayed $x$- and $z$-values.  Write
$w_{\mathcal R}(Q)=\min_{y\in P_{\mathcal R}}y(Q)$ for its exact lower-envelope
game.  Two facts are profile-independent.  First, the incidence identity and
equal totals give the exact selector payment
\[
 \sum_{S\in\mathcal R}\lambda_S\bigl(x(S)-z(S)\bigr)
 =\alpha_r(x_r-M)-\alpha_h(M-x_h)\ \ge 0 .          \tag{PXC91a}
\]
This is the equality behind (CB5), rather than a new inequality.  Second, if
$P$ is any completion with $z\in P\subseteq P_{\mathcal R}$ and lower-envelope
game $v$, then
\[
 w_{\mathcal R}(Q)\le v(Q),\qquad
 w_{\mathcal R}(Q)=z(Q)\Longrightarrow v(Q)=z(Q).  \tag{PXC91b}
\]
Consequently a positive singleton minimum in the minimal lift rules out every
singleton-normalized completion, while any row already tight at $z$ remains
tight in every completion retaining $z$.  This is the monotone minimal-lift
lemma used by the census.

What is fixed in that census is now explicit.  The comparison-compatible row
set (65 rows and 52 nonsingleton incidence columns), the swaps
$2\leftrightarrow3$ and $5\leftrightarrow6$, the three staged Lmin rows, the
minimal-lift selector calculations, and all labelled-cover and orbit counts
belong to the particular pair (PXC88).  Only comparison compatibility,
(PXC91a)--(PXC91b), strict positivity of the two top weights, positive ambient
load under pair slack, and the $n-2$ extreme-support bound survive a change of
profile.  In particular, ``nonviolating'' is not itself monotone under
completion.  No completion lift follows unless the witnessing closing rows
are also verified tight at the prescribed $z$; the incidence census did not
perform that fidelity check.

The tempting profile-independent bridge is false.  Namely, one might ask
whether every residual root has a strictly positive extreme top cover
$\mathcal R$, $|\mathcal R|\le n-2$, for which
\[
       \Lmin(w_{\mathcal R})=x,
       \qquad \Lmax(w_{\mathcal R})=z.             \tag{PXC91c}
\]
Here ${\cal C}_x$ is fixed complete staged-certificate data for the root;
(PXC91c) is the unrestricted question over residual roots, not yet restricted
to the later $s=2$, $k\ge2$, reserve/direct-failing cell.
The game (PXC88) is already an exact counterexample.  Its only proper
nonsingleton rows tight at $z$ are
\[
 0134,\quad0125,\quad16,\quad0126,\quad0136.
\]
Writing their cover weights as $(a,b,c,d,e)$ and the common outsider load as
$\eta$, the outsider equations are
\[
 a=\eta,\quad b=\eta,\quad b+d=\eta,\quad
 a+e=\eta,\quad c+d+e=\eta.
\]
Thus $d=e=0$, $a=b=c=\eta$, and normalization gives
$\eta=1/3$.  Consequently the unique normalized cover with both top
coefficients positive has positive support
$\mathcal R=\{0134,0125,16\}$ with weights $1/3$.  Retaining this cover and
the complete staged Lmin rows $012,013,023$ preserves $x$ but changes the
upper selector to
\[
 z'=\tfrac17(29,29,26,26,5,13,12)\ne z.            \tag{PXC92a}
\]
These are all proper nonsingleton rows tight at $x$, and an exact replay of
all seven proper subsets shows that each subset changes Lmin; hence no
alternative complete retained row pool repairs this example.
Indeed the cover equality fixes the first ceiling and gives
$y_6=12/7$, $y_2+y_5=39/7$, and $y_3+y_4=31/7$; the retained Lmin rows then
pin the second ceiling at $26/7$.  The two zero-cover-weight rows $0126$ and
$0136$ separately raise the two second-stratum coordinates to $27/7$ in the
full game.  Each is indispensable, and retaining both restores $z$.  Hence
an extreme top cover can certify the first Lmax stage while losing later
stages.  PXC90 remains the positive calibration: equality in its top-cover
bound also pins $y_0=y_1=29/7$; those coordinate equalities, the five row
equalities, and efficiency have rank seven and uniquely pin its displayed
$z$.

There is an exact replacement for the failed bridge.  For a compact
retained-row polytope $P_H$ and $p\in P_H$, let $T_H(p)$ be its active tangent cone:
$\one_N\mathbin\cdot d=0$, $d(S)\ge0$ for every retained row active at $p$,
and $d_i\ge0$ whenever $p_i=0$.  Write the payoff strata of $z$ in descending
order as $B_1,\ldots,B_m$, and $E_{k-1}=B_1\cup\cdots\cup B_{k-1}$.  Then
\[
 \Lmax(P_H)=z\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \forall k\ \forall i\in B_k:\ \nexists d\in T_H(z):
 d|_{E_{k-1}}=0, d_j\le0\ (j\in B_k), d_i<0       \tag{PXC92b}
\]
The Lmin statement uses ascending strata and excludes directions with
$d|_{E_{k-1}}=0$, $d_j\ge0$ on $B_k$, and a designated $d_i>0$.
This is just the staged
lexicographic optimality condition, but it exposes the missing hypothesis in
(PXC91c).  For example, $z'-z=(0,0,-1,-1,1,1,0)/7$ is an admissible
second-stage direction in the PXC88 minimal lift.

Equivalently, each forbidden direction in (PXC92b) is excluded by a Farkas
identity using active retained-row normals, the previously fixed coordinate
normals, and sign normals on the current block.  A complete staged Lmin
certificate therefore preserves $x$ automatically.  Rows of $\mathcal R$
and all auxiliaries below are tight at the original $z$; comparison
compatibility $x(S)\ge z(S)$ keeps $x$ feasible, while $z(C)\ge x(C)$ on
the $x$-tight rows keeps $z$ feasible.  The nonautomatic requirement is a
complete collection of the later Lmax identities.

Assume $\operatorname{Top}(z)=\{r,h\}$ and begin with a normalized $z$-tight
top cover $\mathcal R$ having $\alpha_r,\alpha_h>0$.  For each non-top player,
choose one basic Farkas identity for its $z$-stage using the full original
$z$-tight menu, and let $\mathcal A$ be the union of its coalition supports
outside ${\cal C}_x\cup\mathcal R$.  Retaining $\mathcal A$ preserves every
later ceiling stage and hence both selectors.  After quotienting by efficiency
and previously fixed coordinate equalities, conic Carath\'eodory in a space of
dimension at most $n$ gives a certificate using at most $n$ coalition normals
(sign and nonnegativity normals only lower that count).
Taking the union over the $n-2$ non-top players gives the safe bound
\[
 |\mathcal A|\le n(n-2)                              \tag{PXC92c}
\]
on additional rows when the top stratum has two players.  Repeated rows can
make the actual set much smaller: PXC88 needs exactly two and PXC90 needs
none.  Thus sparse cover selection has been replaced by a rigorous bounded
auxiliary-row theorem.

There is a genuine six-player obstruction to compressing the PXC88 failure.
Write the roles as $r,h,a,b,j,k$, where $\{r,h\}$ is the top stratum,
$z_a=z_b=q<M$, and $j,k$ are the two strict outsiders.  Work in the ordered
saturated strict single-heavy cell: the game is exact and
singleton-normalized, $x,z$ are efficient, $x_r>M>x_h$, the gainers are
exactly $h,a,b$, the outsiders are nongainers, and the stated Lmin order
holds.  Suppose the following two rows are $z$-tight.  The two-row split
matching
\[
 R_j=\{r,h,b,j\},\qquad R_k=\{r,h,a,k\}
\]
has weights $1/2$, ambient load $1/2$, and top weights $(1/2,1/2)$.
The only opposite-omission rows on the same two outsiders are
\[
 A_j=\{r,h,a,j\},\qquad B_k=\{r,h,b,k\}.
\]
But $A_j,B_k$, again with weights $1/2$, form a second normalized positive
top cover.  Using the other assignment merely reproduces $R_j,R_k$.
Consequently the two later-stage repair rows cannot both be forced to have
zero coefficient in every positive cover, as $0126,0136$ are in PXC88.

The two natural four-role alternatives are also impossible.  Put
$d_i=z_i-x_i$ on the three gainers, $L_j=x_j-z_j$, $L_k=x_k-z_k$, and
$\ell=x_r-M$.  Efficiency gives
$d_h+d_a+d_b=\ell+L_j+L_k$.  Hence
\[
 z(\{h,a,b,j\})-x(\{h,a,b,j\})=\ell+L_k>0,
 \qquad z(\{h,a,b,k\})-x(\{h,a,b,k\})=\ell+L_j>0,
\]
so neither row can be $z$-tight while $x$ remains in the core.  On the other
hand the split cover implies $y_r+y_h\ge2M$ at every core point.  If, say,
$\{r,a,b,j\}$ were $z$-tight, singleton exactness would supply a core point
with $y_r=0$, and then
\[
 y(\{r,a,b,j\})\le T-2M=2q+z_j+z_k
        < M+2q+z_j=z(\{r,a,b,j\}),
\]
a contradiction; the $\{r,a,b,k\}$ case is symmetric.

In fact the literal two-row split cover admits a complete no-go theorem.
On the face where the earlier Lmax stratum is pinned, a tangent direction
$u$ has $u_r=u_h=0$.  Put $t=u_a$ and $s=u_b$.  Tightness of $R_j,R_k$
forces
\[
 (u_a,u_b,u_j,u_k)=(t,s,-s,-t).
\]
Every later $z$-tight row therefore cuts this two-dimensional tangent cone
by one of the eight nonzero forms
\(
 \pm t,\ \pm s,\ \pm(t-s),\ \pm(t+s).
\)
Any family excluding $(t,s)\in\mathbb R_-^2\setminus\{0\}$ has a closing
subfamily of at most two.  After comparison compatibility with $x$, the
canonical $a$-blocker types and their $b$-symmetric counterparts give the
following pair table; the one-row $t+s$ variants are handled separately:
\[
\begin{array}{c|cccccccc}
 &bk&hbk&rb&rab&rbk&rabk&rhb&rhbk\\ \hline
aj   &I&I&F&X&I&F&X&I\\
haj  &I&I&X&X&I&X&X&I\\
ra   &F&X&F&X&F&F&X&F\\
rab  &X&X&X&X&X&X&X&X\\
raj  &I&I&F&X&I&F&X&I\\
rabj &F&X&F&X&F&F&X&F\\
rha  &X&X&X&X&X&X&X&X\\
rhaj &I&I&F&X&I&F&X&I
\end{array}                                             \tag{PXC93}
\]
Here $I$ means that a common negative ray remains, $X$ means that cover
compatibility and the necessary reserve-failure inequality are inconsistent,
and $F$ means scalar feasibility but failure at the exactness witness with
$y_r=0$.  The load-bearing identities are short.  For example, compatibility
of $rha$ gives $L_j+L_k\le d_b$, hence
$d_h+d_a=\ell+L_j+L_k-d_b\le\ell$, contradicting reserve failure;
compatibility of $rab$ gives $d_a+d_b\le\ell$.  In an $F$ cell the
split cover first gives $y_h\ge2M$.  For $aj,rb$, the two blocker rows
then give
\[
 T\ge3M+2q+z_j>2M+2q+z_j+z_k=T,
\]
and a row $rabj$ already gives the same contradiction by itself.  The swap
$a,b\leftrightarrow b,a$, $j,k\leftrightarrow k,j$ supplies the genuine
symmetric cases; no monotonicity in top membership is used.  An exact
rational verifier classifies every displayed cell first without and then
with the root witness, reproducing the table.  Independently of that
canonicalization it enumerates all $44$ proper nonsingleton labelled
coalition masks with nonzero tangent form and all $275$ closing labelled
one- or two-row families, including the four one-row $t+s$ variants; none
survives, while the weaker base necessary system is feasible.  It also
exhausts all $192$ closing subfamilies of the eight-form set and verifies
that no minimal closing family needs more than two rows.

Thus, in the ordered saturated exact cell just fixed, reserve failure and the
root singleton witness prevent the later $z$-tight rows from closing the
second-stage Lmax tangent cone.  A six-player game cannot realize the PXC88
failure through the literal two-row split cover at all.  The unused third
outsider is genuinely load-bearing.  This excludes a whole cover mechanism,
not the other nonsplit extreme-cover incidences through four rows.

The direct-packet route can be written as an explicit two-outsider inequality.
Let $D=d_h+d_a+d_b$,
$d_{\max}=\max(d_h,d_a,d_b)$, $E=D-d_{\max}$, and let $A_\rho$ be the
saturated decorated co-star load.  Since $n=6$ and $g=3$, the cardinality
term in (PXC63b) vanishes, so reserve failure is exactly
\[
 E-\ell>\frac43A_\rho.                              \tag{PXC93a}
\]
For $p\in\{j,k\}$ let $u$ denote the other outsider, take
$P=\{p\}$, $H=\{r,u\}$, and omit a maximum-gain player from the direct
packet.  The master identity has cardinality base $7/10>2/3$ and its exact
admissible interval is
\[
 \ell+L_u\le E\le
 B_p:=\frac{-x(G)-3x_p-(M+z_u)+\frac85(T-z_p)}3.    \tag{PXC93b}
\]
It would suffice to prove that (PXC93a) forces (PXC93b) for one of the two
outsiders.  This statement is not a consequence of allocation arithmetic
alone.  The following rank argument bypasses that implication and the false
generic premise that every later blocker can be pivoted into a positive
cover.

Assume first that the top pair is slack.  By (PXC89), choose a normalized
$z$-tight extreme top cover with positive ambient load $\eta$, positive top
coefficients, and support $\mathcal R=\{S_1,\ldots,S_m\}$, $m\le4$.  Let
$A\in\{0,1\}^{4\times m}$ be its incidence matrix on the four non-top players
$a,b,j,k$.  The outsider equations are
$A\lambda=\eta\one_4$.  Extremality implies
\[
                       \operatorname{rank}A=m.       \tag{PXC94a}
\]
Indeed, since $\eta>0$, the column $-\one_4$ lies in the span of $A$.
If $\operatorname{rank}A<m$, the outsider-balance solution cone has dimension
at least two, and the one remaining normalization equation cannot isolate the
strictly positive ray $(\lambda,\eta)$.

This rank statement turns support four into selector fidelity.  If
$m=4$ and $y$ is any retained-row feasible point with
$\max_i y_i\le M$, the cover inequality and efficiency give
\[
 \eta T+\alpha_r y_r+\alpha_hy_h\ge\eta T+M.
\]
Because $\alpha_r,\alpha_h>0$, their sum is one, and both top coordinates are
at most $M$, equality is forced: $y_r=y_h=M$ and every positive cover row is
tight.  Thus $A^T(y_{\{a,b,j,k\}}-z_{\{a,b,j,k\}})=0$; (PXC94a) gives $y=z$.
The cover therefore makes $z$ the unique first-ceiling minimizer, while a
complete staged ${\cal C}_x$ preserves $x$ as in (PXC92b).  The original
singleton witnesses remain feasible in this relaxed polytope, so its lower
envelope remains singleton-normalized.

The smaller supports cannot occur in the reserve-failing cell.  Support one
is impossible when $\eta>0$.  At support two the outsider columns are a
nontrivial complementary partition, both rows contain $r,h$, and both weights
are $1/2$.  Singleton-gainer and $ab\mid jk$ partitions contradict comparison
compatibility and (PXC93a); the two cross partitions are exactly the split
faces excluded by (PXC93).  The only new boundary is
$rhj\mid rhabk$ (or its symmetric copy), where compatibility can force
$L_j=0$.  Its second-stage tangent has precisely the six nonzero forms
$\pm t,\pm s,\pm(t+s)$.  An exact rational necessary-system enumeration finds
$40$ closing form families, each with a closing subfamily of at most two,
and all $381$ labelled closing one- or two-row families become infeasible
when the blocker and root-zero witness are added to the full displayed
necessary system.

At support three the balanced rank-three $0$--$1$ hypergraphs have six
colored placements of two complementary unweighted trace types,
\[
 \{a,b,jk\},\ \{a,j,bk\},\ \{ab,j,k\},
 \quad\text{and their complements}.                 \tag{PXC94b}
\]
The first and third shapes and all complement shapes are excluded by top
positivity, comparison compatibility, efficiency, and the pair-sum consequence
of (PXC93a).  Of the $37$ colored support-three orbits, $34$ already fail
that scalar system.  Exactly three decorations of the delicate second trace
survive it; the root-zero witness immediately excludes the versions
$(ra,hj,rhbk)$ and $(ra,rhj,rhbk)$.  The only unresolved equality boundary
has rows $ra,rhj,hbk$ and forces
$d_a=\ell$, $L_j=0$, $L_k=d_h+d_b$.  Lmax optimality would then require a
tight row containing $b$ and omitting $k$.  All $15$ labelled possibilities
are infeasible after adjoining that blocker to the full necessary system.
Possible $z$-tight zero-valued outsider singletons account for $41$ of the
$807$ labelled covers in $14$ colored orbits: six size-three covers in two
orbits and $35$ size-four covers in $12$ orbits.  Every such orbit is already
infeasible under the weaker scalar reserve system.

Consequently, in the ordered saturated strict single-heavy six-player cell,
reserve failure and top-pair slack force every positive extreme top cover to
have support four, and hence force a selector-faithful minimal lift:
\[
 \exists\mathcal R,\quad |\mathcal R|=4,\qquad
 \Lmin(w_{\mathcal R})=x,
 \quad\Lmax(w_{\mathcal R})=z.                       \tag{PXC94c}
\]
The complete incidence audit enumerates $807$ labelled positive extreme
covers in $259$ role-preserving colored orbits.  The sole zero-ambient cover
is the tight top pair; removing it leaves $806$ covers in $258$ orbits.
Every positive-ambient cover has the full outsider-incidence column rank in
(PXC94a).  Independent exact boundary verifiers retain feasible base systems,
so the zero-survivor conclusions are not artifacts of an inconsistent
relaxation.

The top-pair-tight case must be separated.  It is the unique $\eta=0$ cover
$\{r,h\}$ and does not by itself preserve later Lmax stages.  Nevertheless the
target residual cell is empty.  After the top block is pinned, enumerate the
inclusion-minimal non-top incidence-trace families that close the second Lmax
stage.  There are $11$, of size at most three.  Decorating them by all
admissible $r,h$ memberships gives $445$ labelled families and $5{,}340$
branches after choosing a maximum gainer and one of the two strict failure
sides of each interval (PXC93b).  A continuous exact-rational necessary
system retains role order, efficiency, comparison compatibility, reserve
failure, both direct-interval failures, and a root-zero exactness witness.
It has $642$ feasible branches before the witness and none after it:
\[
 \{r,h\}\text{ tight},\quad\text{reserve failure},\quad
 \text{both (PXC93b) packets fail}
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad z\ne\Lmax.               \tag{PXC95}
\]
This is a finite incidence theorem over continuous rational variables, not a
bounded payoff search; the feasible pre-witness branches and a separate
fail-open base prevent vacuous infeasibility.  Hence every exact
singleton-normalized game in the stated ordered cell with a tight top pair is
already closed by the reserve or one of the two direct packets.  PXC84 gives
an independent closure of the same face for the hybrid-overlap inequality.

Together, (PXC94) and (PXC95) close the six-player reserve/direct-failing
top-cover frontier.  They do not reinstate the unrestricted conjecture
(PXC91c), which fails at seven players by (PXC92a), nor do they prove pure
selector fidelity for tight-top-pair games outside this residual cell.

The sharp dimension statement for the restricted frozen-domain conjecture is
therefore now complete.  The single-heavy $s=2$ arm has three gainers---the heavy player
and two sub-top players---and, by definition here, at least two outsiders;
hence $n\ge6$.  At $n=6$, reserve and direct failure rule out a tight top pair
by (PXC95), while top-pair slack invokes (PXC94c).  Thus every game in the
frozen domain satisfies the faithful sparse-lift assertion at six players.
The exact game (PXC88) satisfies the same hypotheses at seven players and
fails it by (PXC92a).  Consequently
\[
 \boxed{\quad n=6:\ \text{restricted (PXC91c) holds};\qquad
              n=7:\ \text{restricted (PXC91c) can fail}.\quad}
                                                        \tag{PXC96}
\]
Here ``restricted'' means precisely the ordered, saturated, single-heavy
$s=2$, $k\ge2$, reserve/direct-failing domain fixed above; this is not the
unrestricted assertion (PXC91c).  PXC88 is the smallest exact counterexample
in that restricted domain.  Its unique
positive extreme cover omits exactly the two indispensable later-stage rows
$0126,0136$; (PXC92c) is the corrected lift theorem, with at most
$n(n-2)$ additional rows in general and exactly those two rows in PXC88.

For the remaining global minimum-dimension question, the quantifiers are
fixed as follows.  An unrestricted instance is a tuple
$(v,x,z,{\cal C}_x)$ on $4\le n\le6$ players such that $v$ is exact and
singleton-normalized, $x=\Lmin(v)\ne z=\Lmax(v)$,
$\operatorname{Top}(z)=\{r,h\}$, and ${\cal C}_x$ is any fixed collection of
original $x$-tight nonempty proper-coalition rows whose retained polytope
(with efficiency and nonnegativity) has Lmin equal to $x$.  Collections are
identified when they define the same retained polytope, so repetitions and
rows already supplied by efficiency or nonnegativity do not create distinct
certificates.  Thus the strong question is
universal over every such fixed ${\cal C}_x$; the weaker question permits
choosing ${\cal C}_x$ after $(v,x,z)$ is known.  A qualifying top cover means
an extreme normalized identity
\[
 \sum_{S\in\mathcal R}\lambda_S\one_S
 =\eta\one_N+\alpha_r e_r+\alpha_h e_h,
 \qquad \lambda_S,\alpha_r,\alpha_h>0,
 \quad \eta\ge0,\quad \alpha_r+\alpha_h=1,
                                                        \tag{PXC97a}
\]
using original $z$-tight proper-coalition rows and
$|\mathcal R|\le n-2$.  Ambient positivity is not assumed: when the top pair
is tight, $\mathcal R=\{rh\}$ is the canonical $\eta=0$ cover.  The strong
unrestricted assertion in dimension $n$ is
\[
 \forall(v,x,z,{\cal C}_x)\ \exists\mathcal R:\qquad
 \Lmin(w_{\mathcal R})=x,
 \quad \Lmax(w_{\mathcal R})=z.                    \tag{PXC97b}
\]
No orderedness, saturation, gain/loss pattern, reserve or direct condition,
named support shape, or later-stratum tie pattern is part of (PXC97b).
The global minimum failure dimension is the least $n$ for which (PXC97b)
fails; PXC88 previously proved only that it is at most seven.

Three unrestricted reductions are immediate.  First, there are no instances
of (PXC97b) for $n\le4$, because the small-game coincidence theorem gives
$\Lmin=\Lmax$.  Second, suppose a qualifying cover has $\eta>0$ and its
non-top incidence matrix $A_{\mathcal R}$ has full row rank $n-2$.  At any
point with ceiling at most $M$, equality in the cover bound pins both top
coordinates to $M$ and every positive-weight cover row to equality.  Hence
$A_{\mathcal R}^{\mathsf T}(y_{N\setminus\{r,h\}}-
z_{N\setminus\{r,h\}})=0$, so full row rank pins $y=z$.  Such a cover is
faithful through every later stratum, independently of all role geometry.
Third, if $\eta=0$, positivity and the outsider load equations force every
support row to avoid all non-top players.  Singleton normalization excludes
the top singletons from the $z$-tight menu, leaving only the tight pair
$\{r,h\}$, with weight $1/2$.  Thus
\[
 \boxed{\ \operatorname{rank}A_{\mathcal R}=n-2
         \Longrightarrow \mathcal R\text{ is faithful};\qquad
         \exists\text{ a qualifying }\eta=0\text{ cover}
         \Longleftrightarrow z(rh)=2M.\ }             \tag{PXC97c}
\]
At five players Theorem~\ref{thm:n5closed} gives $M=\nu$, and an attaining
coalition lies inside the Lmax top stratum by
Lemma~\ref{lem:anchors}(a).  With exactly two top players it must be
$\{r,h\}$; consequently every five-player instance of (PXC97b) is in the
pair-tight, $\eta=0$ cell.  The five-player statement is therefore precisely
whether the common-tight rows of every fixed ${\cal C}_x$, together with the
top pair and active nonnegativity, supply all PXC92b polars on the remaining
three coordinates.

The analogous six-player question is already false, however, and it fails in
both the pair-tight and positive-ambient cells at once.  On players
$(r,h,a,b,j,k)=(0,1,2,3,4,5)$ let $P$ be the nonnegative total-$17$
polytope
\[
 y(rha)\ge11,\qquad y(rhb)\ge11,\qquad
 y(rj)\ge6,\qquad y(rhjk)\ge11,                 \tag{PXC98a}
\]
and define $v(S)=\min_{y\in P}y(S)$.  The polytope is nonempty and compact,
every minimum is attained at a point of $P\subseteq\core(v)$, and hence $v$
is exact.  The points $(0,11,0,0,6,0)$ and $(17,0,0,0,0,0)$ give a zero
witness for $r$ and for each other player respectively, so $v$ is
singleton-normalized.  Exact staged optimization gives
\[
 x=\Lmin(v)=(9/2,9/2,2,2,2,2),\qquad
 z=\Lmax(v)=(4,4,3,3,2,1).                     \tag{PXC98b}
\]
Indeed the complements of the first two rows in (PXC98a) have sums at most
$6$, so the Lmin floor is at most $2$; equality pins $a,b,j,k$ at $2$ and
then equalizes $r,h$ at $9/2$.  For Lmax, $v(rh)=8$ forces the first ceiling
to be at least $4$.  At equality $r=h=4$; the first two rows force
$a,b\ge3$, efficiency pins $a=b=3$ and $j+k=3$, and the last two rows then
give $j=2,k=1$ by the later ceiling stages.

The complete proper $x$-tight menu is exactly
$\{rha,rhb\}$, and neither the empty row-set nor either one-row subset has
Lmin equal to $x$.  Thus
$\mathcal C_x=\{rha,rhb\}$ is the unique complete certificate even under the
weaker existential-$\mathcal C_x$ reading.  The complete proper $z$-tight
menu is
\[
 \{rh,rha,rhb,rj,rhjk\}.
\]
Writing its cover weights as $(A,B,C,D,E)$ in that order, the four outsider
load equations and normalization reduce to
\[
 B=C=E=\eta,\qquad D=0,\qquad 2A+4\eta=1.       \tag{PXC98c}
\]
Consequently the normalized cover polytope is a segment and has exactly two
positive extreme supports of size at most four:
\[
 \{rh\}:\ (A,\eta)=(1/2,0),\qquad
 \{rha,rhb,rhjk\}:\ (B,C,E,\eta)=(1/4,1/4,1/4,1/4),
\]
both with $(\alpha_r,\alpha_h)=(1/2,1/2)$.  The later blocker $rj$ has
coefficient zero in every normalized cover.  Retaining either extreme cover
with the unique $\mathcal C_x$ preserves Lmin and singleton normalization but
changes the upper selector to
\[
 \Lmax(w_{\mathcal R})=(4,4,3,3,3/2,3/2)\ne z,
 \qquad
 d=(0,0,0,0,-1/2,1/2).                         \tag{PXC98d}
\]
The direction $d$ fixes every earlier $z$-stratum and is invisible to all
retained rows, while $d(rj)=-1/2$.  Thus (PXC98) refutes both the strong
fixed-$\mathcal C_x$ and weak existential-$\mathcal C_x$ versions of
(PXC97b) at $n=6$.  A standalone verifier exhausts all four $x$-tight
subsets and every positive extreme top-cover support through size four, and
an independent verifier imports no project LP, selector, cover, or game
implementation.  Their reproduction commands are
\begin{verbatim}
cd verifiers/divergence/search_archive
python3 unrestricted_pxc91c_exact_search.py \
  --output unrestricted_pxc91c_exact_search.json
cd ..
python3 verify_unrestricted_pxc91c_pxc98_independent.py \
  --output unrestricted_pxc91c_pxc98_independent.json
\end{verbatim}
Therefore the unrestricted minimum failure dimension is at most six.  Since
$n\le4$ is impossible, the five-player pair-tight residual problem is the
only remaining lower-dimensional case.
As finite evidence on that last cell, an exact audit of the existing
five-player bank finds $165$ distinct exact games, of which $36$ have a
two-player Lmax top stratum.  It exhausts every $x$-tight row subset and all
$37$ complete certificates and $146$ qualifying extreme covers across those
games, with no strong or weak $\mathcal C_x$ failure.  This is an exhaustive
audit of the pinned bank, not of the five-player exact cone.

The remaining five-player statement has a compact exact polar form.  Put
$U=\{a,b,c\}$, let
${\cal C}_0=\{C\in\mathcal C_x:x(C)=z(C)\}$, let
$Z_0=\{u\in U:z_u=0\}$, write
$q_C=\one_{C\cap U}$, and define
\[
 K=\operatorname{cone}\bigl(\{q_C:C\in\mathcal C_0\}
              \cup\{e_u:u\in Z_0\}\bigr).
\]
After the pair row pins $r=h=M$, the PXC92b tangent criterion and Farkas'
lemma give the following necessary-and-sufficient list, modulo relabelling:
\[
\begin{array}{c|c}
\text{residual strata of }z&\text{fidelity condition}\\ \hline
\{a,b,c\}&\text{automatic},\\
\{a\}>\{b,c\}&e_a\in\operatorname{span}\{\one_U\}+K,\\
\{a,b\}>\{c\}&
 \exists\theta\in(0,1):\
 \theta e_a+(1-\theta)e_b\in\operatorname{span}\{\one_U\}+K,\\
\{a\}>\{b\}>\{c\}&
 e_a\in\operatorname{span}\{\one_U\}+K,\quad
 e_b\in\operatorname{span}\{\one_U,e_a\}+K.
\end{array}                                                   \tag{PXC99}
\]
The bank contains one important warning against a simpler argument: an
$x$-tight row can be slack at $z$.  In that case a smaller common-tight
subcollection is already complete, but this common-subcertificate property
need not be assumed in the proof below.

The finite exact-cone route closes the last case.  Normalize $v(N)=1$.
For each residual order in (PXC99), enumerate every weak order of $x$ and
introduce a Boolean selector for every nonempty proper coalition row.
The selected rows are required to be $x$-tight, and the full staged Farkas
identities require them to certify $x=\Lmin$ in their retained polytope.
The mirror identities over all original $z$-tight rows require $z=\Lmax$.
Finally impose one normalized forbidden direction from the applicable line
of (PXC99), including every active zero-coordinate normal.  Exactness is
encoded by the complete $280$-inequality rational description of the
five-player exact cone.  Thus a feasible cell is exactly a failure of the
canonical pair lift for some fixed complete $\mathcal C_x$, and is a PXC97b
counterexample candidate whose other extreme covers would still require
exhaustion; infeasibility of all cells is universal over the fixed certificate.

For a transparent exhaustive indexing, relabel players so that the weak
order of $x$ is increasing.  Its tie blocks are then one of the $16$
compositions of five.  Independently choose the two top players of $z$ in
$\binom52=10$ ways, order the other three in $3!=6$ ways, and choose their
tie blocks from the four compositions of three.  This gives
\[
             16\binom52 3!\,4=3840                         \tag{PXC99a}
\]
cells.  (Tied outsider orders create harmless duplicate cells.)  Exact
QF--LRA returns unsatisfiable in all $3840$, with no unknown or error.

Two algebraically separate encodings give the same census.  The primary
driver uses the raw PXC92b polar/Farkas identity at every stage, with free
coefficients on earlier pinned coordinates.  The independent driver instead
uses normalized nonnegative incidence identities: the designated current
coefficient is scaled to one, other current coefficients are nonnegative,
and coefficients on earlier pinned strata remain free.  Both include active
nonnegativity normals at every zero coordinate, quantify an arbitrary
Boolean subset of all $30$ proper rows for the fixed $\mathcal C_x$, and test
every forbidden later-stage tangent.  In a representative strict residual
cell, separately deleting either singleton normalization or the exact-game
cone changes UNSAT to SAT with an exact rational model.  Thus the controls
fail open rather than reporting UNSAT by construction.

The two census artifacts pin both drivers, the exact-cone catalogue, cell
keys, solver version, and verdict counts.  Consequently, for every five-player unrestricted
instance (PXC97b) and every fixed complete staged Lmin certificate
$\mathcal C_x$, the canonical tight-pair cover $\mathcal R=\{rh\}$ preserves
both selectors.  Hence unrestricted PXC91c holds through five players.
Together with the fixed- and existential-$\mathcal C_x$ failure in PXC98,
this gives the sharp threshold
\[
 \boxed{\quad n\le5:\ \text{(PXC97b) holds};\qquad
                n=6:\ \text{both formulations can fail}.\quad} \tag{PXC100}
\]
In particular, the global minimum failure dimension is exactly $6$.
This is a computer-assisted theorem: the reduction to the pair cover and the
polar criterion are conventional, while the last finite implication uses
the exact five-player cone and the $3840$ rational cells.  Reproduction is
\begin{verbatim}
python3 \
  verifiers/divergence/search_archive/unrestricted_pxc91c_n5_all_cells.py \
  --workers 4 \
  --cell-timeout-ms 120000 \
  --output \
  verifiers/divergence/search_archive/unrestricted_pxc91c_n5_all_cells.json
python3 \
  verifiers/divergence/search_archive/unrestricted_pxc91c_n5_all_cells.py \
  --workers 4 \
  --cell-timeout-ms 120000 \
  --cell-driver \
  verifiers/divergence/search_archive/verify_unrestricted_pxc91c_n5_cell_ni.py \
  --encoding-label independent-NI \
  --output \
  verifiers/divergence/search_archive/unrestricted_pxc91c_n5_all_cells_ni.json
\end{verbatim}

The six-player witness is not isolated and is not a null-player padding
artifact.  It belongs to a rational chamber that persists in every larger
dimension.  Let \(k\ge2\), put
\[
 N=\{r,h,a,b\}\mathbin{\dot\cup}O,\qquad
 O=\{j,o_1,\ldots,o_{k-1}\},
\]
and choose rational parameters
\[
 M>q>J>K>0,\qquad q+(k-1)K\ge M,\qquad
 (k+2)q+(k-1)K>(2k+1)J.                       \tag{PXC101a}
\]
This rational chamber has nonempty interior for every \(k\ge2\).  Indeed,
the rational point
\[
 (M,q,J,K)=(15/4,3,2,1)
\]
satisfies every displayed inequality strictly.
Set
\[
\begin{split}
 T&=2M+2q+J+(k-1)K,\\
 L&=2M+q,\qquad B=M+J,\qquad
 C=2M+J+(k-1)K,
\end{split}
\]
and let \(P_k(M,q,J,K)\) be the nonnegative total-\(T\) polytope
\[
 y(rha)\ge L,\qquad y(rhb)\ge L,\qquad
 y(rj)\ge B,\qquad y(rhO)\ge C.                \tag{PXC101b}
\]
As before define \(v(S)=\min_{y\in P_k}y(S)\).  This produces a
positive-dimensional rational family of exact singleton-normalized games.
Indeed \(P_k\) is nonempty because it contains the allocation displayed
below.  Compactness and the lower-envelope construction give exactness.
For singleton normalization, the all-root allocation \(T e_r\) zeros every
player other than \(r\).  A zero witness for \(r\) assigns
\[
 y_h=L,\qquad y_j=B,\qquad
 \sum_{\ell=1}^{k-1}y_{o_\ell}=T-L-B
    =q+(k-1)K-M\ge0,
\]
and zero to \(r,a,b\).  It satisfies the fourth row because its full mass
outside \(a,b\) is \(T>C\).

Put
\[
 u=\frac{q+J+(k-1)K}{k+1},\qquad
 p=\frac{T-(k+2)u}{2}.
\]
Then exact staged optimization gives
\begin{equation}
\begin{split}
 \Lmin(v)&=(p,p,u,u,u^k),\\
 \Lmax(v)&=(M,M,q,q,J,K^{k-1}).
\end{split}
\tag{PXC101c}
\end{equation}
Here and below exponents denote repetition.  For the lower selector, if
\(R=y_r+y_h\) and \(W=y(O)\), the first two rows give
\[
 2L\le2R+y_a+y_b=T+R-W.
\]
If every coordinate is at least \(m\), then
\(R\le T-(k+2)m\) and \(W\ge km\), whence
\[
 m\le\frac{T-L}{k+1}=u.
\]
Equality pins \(a,b\) and all \(k\) outsiders at \(u\), and the next stage
equalizes \(r,h\) at \(p\).  The inequalities in (PXC101a) give \(p>u\).
They also give
\[
 \begin{split}
 2(k+1)(p-u)
  &=2M(k+1)+(k-2)q-3J-3(k-1)K>0,\\
 2(k+1)(p+u-B)
  &=(k+2)q+(k-1)K-(2k+1)J>0,\\
 2p+ku-C&=2(q-u)>0,
 \end{split}
\]
so the blocker and the all-outsider row are slack at \(\Lmin(v)\).
The only proper \(x\)-tight rows are therefore \(rha,rhb\).  A one-row
retention pins the complementary \(k+1\) players at \(u\) and then
equalizes the remaining triple at \(L/3\ne(p,p,u)\).  Consequently
\[
 {\cal C}_x=\{rha,rhb\}
\]
is the unique complete proper-row certificate.

For the upper selector, the first, second and fourth rows imply
\[
 2L\le T+R-W\le T+2R-C,
\]
and hence \(R\ge2M\).  At ceiling \(M\), this pins \(r=h=M\);
the first two rows then pin \(a=b=q\), while efficiency and the fourth row
pin \(y(O)=J+(k-1)K\).  The blocker gives \(y_j\ge J\).
Since \(q>J>K\), the remaining stages pin \(j=J\) and
\(o_1=\cdots=o_{k-1}=K\), proving (PXC101c).

The normal-cone calculation is dimension-free.  At \(x\) only the first two
displayed rows are active.  At \(z\), solving
\(\mathbf1_S=\beta\mathbf1+\sum_{t=1}^4\lambda_t\mathbf1_{C_t}\),
\(\lambda_t\ge0\), in the six coordinate types \(r,h,a,b,j,o\) means that
the six coordinates of \(\mathbf1_S\) are
\[
 \begin{split}
 &\beta+\lambda_1+\lambda_2+\lambda_3+\lambda_4,\quad
 \beta+\lambda_1+\lambda_2+\lambda_4,\quad
 \beta+\lambda_1,\quad \beta+\lambda_2,\\
 &\beta+\lambda_3+\lambda_4,\quad \beta+\lambda_4.
 \end{split}
\]
Requiring each to be zero or one gives exactly
\[
 \{rh,rha,rhb,rj,rhO\}                         \tag{PXC101d}
\]
as the proper \(z\)-tight menu.  If their normalized cover weights are
\((A_0,A_1,A_2,D,E)\), respectively, outsider balance and normalization give
\[
 A_1=A_2=E=\eta,\qquad D=0,\qquad
 2A_0+4\eta=1.                                 \tag{PXC101e}
\]
Thus the cover polytope is the same segment in every dimension.  Its two
extreme supports are
\[
 \{rh\}:\ A_0=\tfrac12,\ \eta=0,\qquad
 \{rha,rhb,rhO\}:\ A_1=A_2=E=\eta=\tfrac14.
\]
The blocker \(rj\) has coefficient zero in every normalized top cover.
For either extreme cover, the retained upper selector is
\[
 z'=(M,M,q,q,\bar K^k),\qquad
 \bar K=\frac{J+(k-1)K}{k},                    \tag{PXC101f}
\]
which differs from \(z\) because \(J>K\).  The common forbidden direction is
\[
 d=z'-z=
 (0,0,0,0,-(k-1)(J-K)/k,((J-K)/k)^{k-1}).
\]
It vanishes on \(rh,rha,rhb,rhO\), but
\(d(rj)=-(k-1)(J-K)/k<0\).  Since every complete
\({\cal C}_x\) equals the displayed pair and both extreme covers fail,
both certificate quantifiers in (PXC97b) fail throughout this chamber.

The integer specialization
\[
 (M,q,J,K)=(4,3,2,1),\qquad T=k+15=n+11        \tag{PXC102a}
\]
satisfies (PXC101a) for every \(k\ge2\).  It gives the explicit
all-dimension selector pair
\begin{equation}
\begin{split}
 x&=\left(\left(\frac{10k+7}{2(k+1)}\right)^2,
              \left(\frac{k+4}{k+1}\right)^{k+2}\right),\\
 z&=(4,4,3,3,2,1^{k-1}),\qquad
 z'=(4,4,3,3,((k+1)/k)^k).
\end{split}
\tag{PXC102b}
\end{equation}
Consequently unrestricted sparse fidelity fails for every \(n\ge6\), by a
genuine \(n\)-player exact game rather than by adjoining null players.
Indeed
\[
 v(rha)-v(rh)=v(rhb)-v(rh)=q,\qquad v(rj)-v(r)=M+J,
\]
and for every \(o\in O\),
\[
 v(rhO)-v\bigl(rh(O\setminus\{o\})\bigr)=J+(k-1)K>0.
\]
Either top player has grand-complement marginal \(T\).  Thus every displayed
player has a strictly positive marginal in an explicit coalition.

There is also an exact general formulation of the mechanism.  Fix an
instance, a complete \({\cal C}_x\), and let \(\mathfrak R\) be the finite
set of qualifying extreme top covers.  Write
\[
 {\cal U}_z=\bigcup_{{\cal R}\in\mathfrak R}{\cal R}
\]
for the cover-eligible \(z\)-tight rows.  At a later Lmax stage, suppose
\(d\) has zero total, vanishes on the top and earlier strata, is nonpositive
on the current stratum with one strict entry, and respects the common-tight
\({\cal C}_x\) rows and active nonnegativity normals.  Then
\[
\boxed{\quad
 d\text{ is tangent to every }({\cal C}_x\cup{\cal R})\text{ lift}
 \ \Longleftrightarrow\
 d(S)=0\quad\text{for every }S\in{\cal U}_z .
\quad}                                                       \tag{PXC103}
\]
Indeed tangency gives \(d(S)\ge0\) on a cover support, whereas dotting its
normalized cover identity with \(d\) gives
\(\sum_{S\in{\cal R}}\lambda_Sd(S)=0\).  Positive support weights force
each term to vanish.  The converse is immediate.  Because the full game has
upper selector \(z\), any such common forbidden direction must be cut by
some original \(z\)-tight row \(Q\notin{\cal U}_z\) with \(d(Q)<0\);
that row has coefficient zero in every top cover.  This is a
necessary-and-sufficient characterization of the \emph{common-direction}
forced-zero-blocker mechanism.  It must not be enlarged without proof to
all universal-cover failures: different covers may in principle leave
different forbidden directions.

That warning is sharp.  There is a second, genuinely cover-dependent
six-player mechanism.  Let \(N=\{r,h,a,b,j,k\}\), impose nonnegativity and
efficiency \(y(N)=23\), and define \(v\) as the lower envelope of
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
 y(rha)&\ge16,& y(rhb)&\ge16,& y(rhj)&\ge13,\\
 y(rhaj)&\ge18,& y(rhk)&\ge13,& y(rhak)&\ge18.
\end{aligned}                                                 \tag{PXC104a}
\end{equation}
Then the game is exact and singleton-normalized and
\begin{equation}
 x=\left((41/6)^2,(7/3)^4\right),\qquad
 z=(6,6,5,4,1,1).                                             \tag{PXC104b}
\end{equation}
Indeed, writing \(R=y_r+y_h\), the first two rows give
\(2R+y_a+y_b\ge32\), hence \(R-(y_j+y_k)\ge9\).  If every
coordinate is at least \(m\), then
\(9\le23-6m\), so \(m\le7/3\).  Equality pins all four outsiders
at \(7/3\), after which the next Lmin stage equalizes \(r,h\) at
\(41/6\).  The other four displayed rows are strict there.  Thus the
only proper \(x\)-tight rows, and the unique complete proper-row
certificate, are
\[
             {\cal C}_x=\{rha,rhb\}.                           \tag{PXC104c}
\]

For the upper selector, either of the two identities below gives \(R\ge12\),
so a ceiling-six point has \(r=h=6\).  The five remaining tight rows give
\(b\ge4\), \(j,k\ge1\), \(a+j\ge6\), and \(a+k\ge6\).  Their
minimum outsider mass is already \(5+4+1+1=11\), the efficient residual,
which pins \(z\).  Exactness follows from compact lower-envelope attainment.
For singleton normalization, use
\((0,12,5,4,1,1)\) and its \(r,h\) swap for the two top-player
witnesses; the all-root allocation supplies every outsider witness.

The proper \(z\)-tight menu is exactly
\[
 \{rh,rhb,rhj,rhaj,rhk,rhak\}.
\]
Put weights \((A,B,J,P,K,Q)\) on these rows.  Outsider balance and
normalization reduce exactly to
\begin{equation}
 B=\eta,\quad J=\eta-P,\quad K=P,\quad Q=\eta-P,
 \quad A+2\eta=\tfrac12,
 \quad 0\le P\le\eta\le\tfrac14.                              \tag{PXC104d}
\end{equation}
Hence the normalized-cover polytope is a triangle with precisely three
extreme covers:
\begin{equation}
 \{rh\}:\tfrac12;\qquad
 \{rhb,rhj,rhak\}:\left(\tfrac14\right)^3;\qquad
 \{rhb,rhaj,rhk\}:\left(\tfrac14\right)^3.                    \tag{PXC104e}
\end{equation}
With the unique \({\cal C}_x\), their reduced Lmax selectors are respectively
\begin{equation}
 (6,6,4,4,3/2,3/2),\qquad
 (6,6,4,4,1,2),\qquad
 (6,6,4,4,2,1),                                                \tag{PXC104f}
\end{equation}
so every qualifying extreme cover fails while Lmin and singleton
normalization are preserved.

This failure has no PXC103 common direction.  After the top block is fixed,
the union of the three cover supports imposes
\(d_b=d_j=d_k=0\) and \(d_a+d_j=d_a+d_k=0\), hence \(d=0\).
Instead the second cover leaves
\(d^-=(0,0,-1,0,0,1)\), cut by \(rhaj\); that blocker belongs
positively to the third cover.  The third leaves
\(d^+=(0,0,-1,0,1,0)\), cut by \(rhak\); that blocker belongs
positively to the second cover.  The pair-cover descent is cut by both.
Thus there is no load-bearing row whose coefficient is zero in every cover:
the obstruction is a two-cycle of \emph{cross-cover blockers}.  In
particular, the proposed extension of (PXC103) from common-direction failures
to all universal-cover failures is false.

The phenomenon is not isolated.  For every rational parameter
\(4<A<11/2\), let \(v_A\) be the lower envelope on
\(N=\{r,h,a,b,j,k\}\) with total \(18+A\) of
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
 y(rha)&\ge16,& y(rhb)&\ge16,\\
 y(rhj)&\ge13,& y(rhk)&\ge13,\\
 y(rhaj)&\ge13+A,& y(rhak)&\ge13+A.
\end{aligned}                                                 \tag{PXC105a}
\end{equation}
Then \(v_A\) is exact and singleton-normalized, and its selectors are
\begin{equation}
 x_A=\left(\left(\frac{46-A}{6}\right)^2,
            \left(\frac{A+2}{3}\right)^4\right),\qquad
 z_A=(6,6,A,4,1,1).                                          \tag{PXC105b}
\end{equation}
The only proper \(x_A\)-tight rows are \(rha,rhb\), so
\({\cal C}_{x_A}=\{rha,rhb\}\) is the unique complete proper-row
certificate.  The proper \(z_A\)-tight menu and the three extreme covers
are exactly those in (PXC104d)--(PXC104e).  With this unique certificate,
the three reduced upper selectors are
\begin{equation}
 \left(6,6,4,4,\frac{A-2}{2},\frac{A-2}{2}\right),\qquad
 (6,6,4,4,1,A-3),\qquad
 (6,6,4,4,A-3,1).                                            \tag{PXC105c}
\end{equation}
Thus every rational \(A\) in the open interval is a universal-cover
fidelity failure of cross-cover type, and (PXC104) is the member \(A=5\).

Here is a direct proof.  A coalition omitting at least one of \(r,h\) has
value zero: put the entire total on an omitted top player.  For
\(U\subseteq\{a,b,j,k\}\), direct elimination of the four outsider bits
gives
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
 v_A(rh\cup U)=\max\bigl(&\{12\}
 \cup\{16:a\in U\text{ or }b\in U\}\\
 &\cup\{13:j\in U\text{ or }k\in U\}
 \cup\{13+A:a\in U,\ U\cap\{j,k\}\ne\varnothing\}\bigr).
\end{aligned}                                                 \tag{PXC105d}
\end{equation}
with the efficiency value at \(N\).  The lower bounds are the displayed
rows and either three-row cover identity; the reverse inequalities follow
from the following explicit endpoint minimizers.  Coordinates are ordered
\((r,h,a,b,j,k)\); the two point columns correspond to \(A=4\) and
\(A=11/2\), respectively.

\begin{center}
\scriptsize
\begin{tabular}{c|c|c}
\(U\)&\(w^4_U\)&\(w^{11/2}_U\)\\ \hline
\(\varnothing\)&\((12,0,4,4,1,1)\)&\((12,0,11/2,4,1,1)\)\\
\(a\)&\((16,0,0,4,1,1)\)&\((16,0,0,5/2,5/2,5/2)\)\\
\(b\)&\((16,0,6,0,0,0)\)&\((16,0,15/2,0,0,0)\)\\
\(ab\)&\((16,0,0,0,5,1)\)&\((16,0,0,0,5,5/2)\)\\
\(j\)&\((13,0,6,3,0,0)\)&\((13,0,15/2,3,0,0)\)\\
\(aj\)&\((17,0,0,5,0,0)\)&\((37/2,0,0,5,0,0)\)\\
\(bj\)&\((16,0,6,0,0,0)\)&\((16,0,15/2,0,0,0)\)\\
\(abj\)&\((17,0,0,0,0,5)\)&\((37/2,0,0,0,0,5)\)\\
\(k\)&\((13,0,6,3,0,0)\)&\((13,0,15/2,3,0,0)\)\\
\(ak\)&\((17,0,0,5,0,0)\)&\((37/2,0,0,5,0,0)\)\\
\(bk\)&\((16,0,6,0,0,0)\)&\((16,0,15/2,0,0,0)\)\\
\(abk\)&\((17,0,0,0,5,0)\)&\((37/2,0,0,0,5,0)\)\\
\(jk\)&\((13,0,6,3,0,0)\)&\((13,0,15/2,3,0,0)\)\\
\(ajk\)&\((17,0,0,5,0,0)\)&\((37/2,0,0,5,0,0)\)\\
\(bjk\)&\((16,0,6,0,0,0)\)&\((16,0,15/2,0,0,0)\)\\
\(abjk\)&\((22,0,0,0,0,0)\)&\((47/2,0,0,0,0,0)\)
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

Each \(w^e_U\) is feasible at its endpoint and attains the value in
(PXC105d).  Both the constraint right sides and the applicable branch of
the maximum in (PXC105d) are affine on the closed interval, so the affine
interpolation of \(w^4_U,w^{11/2}_U\) is a feasible attaining allocation for
every intermediate \(A\).  This proves the reverse inequality and hence
exactness without an implicit attainment claim.  The allocations
\((0,12,A,4,1,1)\), its top swap, and the all-root allocation prove singleton
normalization.  No player is null: adjoining \(a\) or \(b\) to \(rh\) raises
the value by \(4\), adjoining \(j\) or \(k\) raises it by \(1\), and adjoining
either top player to its complement raises the value from zero to \(18+A\).

Writing \(R=y_r+y_h\), the first two rows of (PXC105a) give
\(R-(y_j+y_k)\ge14-A\).  If all coordinates are at least \(m\), then
\(R-(y_j+y_k)\le18+A-6m\), whence \(m\le(A+2)/3\).  Equality pins all
four outsiders at this value and the next stage equalizes the tops at
\((46-A)/6\).  The two long cross rows have slack
\((11-2A)/3>0\); formula (PXC105d) therefore leaves only \(rha,rhb\)
tight.  With only one of them retained, the last three coordinates outside
that row are pinned first at \((A+2)/3\), while the three coordinates in
the row equalize at \(16/3\); hence neither proper subset is complete.

Adding the three inequalities in either positive-ambient cover gives
\(2R+(18+A)\ge42+A\), so \(R\ge12\).  A ceiling-six point therefore has
\(r=h=6\).  The remaining rows give
\(b\ge4\), \(j,k\ge1\), \(a+j\ge A+1\), and \(a+k\ge A+1\).
Their lower bounds sum to the residual \(A+6\), pinning \(z_A\).
The cover triangle is unchanged because its incidence equations do not
depend on \(A\), and the three reduced selectors in (PXC105c) follow by
the same equalization.  Since \(A>4\), none equals \(z_A\).  The two
cover-specific forbidden directions are positive multiples of
\((0,0,-1,0,0,1)\) and \((0,0,-1,0,1,0)\); the opposite cross row blocks
each one.  Their cover-union kernel is zero for every \(A\), completing
the positive-dimensional cross-cover theorem.  At \(A=4\) the \(a,b\)
strata merge, while at \(A=11/2\) the long cross rows become \(x_A\)-tight;
the open endpoints are therefore load-bearing, not cosmetic.

The cross-cover construction also lifts genuinely in dimension.  Let
\(J,K\) be disjoint nonempty blocks of sizes \(p,q\), put \(m=p+q\), and
take a rational \(A\) satisfying
\begin{equation}
 4<A<6,\qquad
 p(A-1)>(m+1)(A-4),\qquad
 q(A-1)>(m+1)(A-4).                                          \tag{PXC106a}
\end{equation}
On \(N=\{r,h,a,b\}\dot\cup J\dot\cup K\), with total \(16+A+m\),
define the lower-envelope game by
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
 y(rha),y(rhb)&\ge16,\\
 y(rhJ)&\ge12+p,& y(rhaJ)&\ge12+A+p,\\
 y(rhK)&\ge12+q,& y(rhaK)&\ge12+A+q.
\end{aligned}                                                 \tag{PXC106b}
\end{equation}
Writing
\[
 u=\frac{A+m}{m+1},\qquad t=\frac{16-u}{2},
\]
the selectors are
\begin{equation}
 x=(t,t,u^{m+2}),\qquad z=(6,6,A,4,1^m).                     \tag{PXC106c}
\end{equation}
The unique complete proper-row certificate remains \(\{rha,rhb\}\).
The proper \(z\)-tight menu is
\[
 \{rh,rhb,rhJ,rhaJ,rhK,rhaK\},
\]
and its three extreme covers are
\begin{equation}
 \{rh\},\qquad \{rhb,rhJ,rhaK\},\qquad
 \{rhb,rhaJ,rhK\},                                          \tag{PXC106d}
\end{equation}
with the same weights as in (PXC104e).  Their reduced upper selectors are
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
 &(6,6,4,4,c^m),&&c=1+\frac{A-4}{m},\\
 &(6,6,4,4,1^p,c_K^q),&&c_K=1+\frac{A-4}{q},\\
 &(6,6,4,4,c_J^p,1^q),&&c_J=1+\frac{A-4}{p}.
\end{aligned}                                                 \tag{PXC106e}
\end{equation}
Consequently every member fails for every qualifying extreme cover while
preserving exactly two Lmax-top players, the unique complete \({\cal C}_x\),
exactness, and singleton normalization.

For completeness, the proof is dimension-free.  As in (PXC105d), every
coalition omitting a top player has value zero.  For a coalition
\(rh\cup U\), its value is the maximum of \(12\) and the bounds among
\[
\begin{gathered}
 16\quad(a\in U\text{ or }b\in U),\qquad
 12+p\quad(J\subseteq U),\qquad
 12+A+p\quad(a\in U,\ J\subseteq U),\\
 12+q\quad(K\subseteq U),\qquad
 12+A+q\quad(a\in U,\ K\subseteq U),
\end{gathered}
\]
with the efficiency value at \(N\).  Each lower bound is displayed or follows
from a positive cover.  The reverse inequality has the following uniform
attainment construction.  For a proper trace \(U\), let \(L\) be the displayed
maximum, put \(y_r=L,y_h=0\), and initially put zero on every selected
non-top coordinate.  If \(a\notin U\), put \(y_a=A\); if \(b\notin U\), put
\(y_b=(16-L)_+\).  For a block \(B\in\{J,K\}\) of size \(s\) that is not
fully contained in \(U\), choose \(o_B\in B\setminus U\) and put
\[
 y_{o_B}=\max\{0,\ 12+s-L,\ 12+A+s-L-y_a\},
\]
leaving the other coordinates of \(B\) at zero.  A fully selected block
receives zero.  These assignments satisfy all six rows.  Their used mass
\(W\) is at most \(16+A+m\): if \(a\notin U\), substituting \(y_a=A\)
gives the explicit expression
\[
 W=L+A+{\bf1}_{b\notin U}(16-L)_+
   +\sum_{\substack{B\in\{J,K\}\\B\not\subseteq U}}
      (12+|B|-L)_+,
\]
whose eight choices of whether \(b,J,K\) are fully selected are each at most
the total;
if \(a\in U\), then \(L\ge16\), and the cases of zero, one, or two fully
selected blocks give respectively
\[
 W=8+2A+m,\qquad
 W\le12+A+\max\{p,q\},\qquad
 W\le12+A+\max\{p,q\},
\]
all at most \(16+A+m\) (the first uses \(A<6\)).  Put the unused mass
\(16+A+m-W\) on any omitted non-top coordinate.  It does not enter the
coalition objective and can only increase the row left sides.  Thus the
constructed point attains \(L\); the grand coalition is attained by the
all-root point.  This proves the asserted lower envelope and tight menus
without an implicit projection claim.  The allocations
\((0,12,A,4,1^m)\), its top swap, and the all-root allocation prove singleton
normalization.

The two short rows give
\[
 R-y(J)-y(K)\ge16-A-m.
\]
If all \(m+4\) coordinates are at least \(s\), the reverse side is at most
\(16+A+m-(2+2m)s\), so \(s\le u\).  Equality pins every non-top
coordinate at \(u\), then equalizes the tops at \(t\).  The two long rows
have respective slack
\[
 \frac{p(A-1)}{m+1}-(A-4),\qquad
 \frac{q(A-1)}{m+1}-(A-4),
\]
which is positive precisely by (PXC106a); the shorter block rows are also
strict.  A one-row certificate instead equalizes its three members at
\(16/3\) after pinning its \(m+1\)-player complement at \(u\), proving
uniqueness of the complete pair.

Either positive cover in (PXC106d) partitions all non-top players among
three rows.  Adding its bounds gives
\(2R+(16+A+m)\ge40+A+m\), hence \(R\ge12\).  At ceiling six the top
players are fixed, and the remaining rows force
\[
 y_b=4,\qquad y(J)=p,\qquad y(K)=q,\qquad y_a=A;
\]
block equalization gives \(z\).  The same calculation after row deletion
gives (PXC106e).  The exact cover equations again form the triangle
(PXC104d), now because each block member has the same incidence column.
The cover union forces \(d_a=d_b=d(J)=d(K)=0\).  Internal block exchanges
remain algebraically, but cannot be a staged Lmax descent on a tied block:
all its entries would be nonpositive and their block sum zero.  Thus there
is no common forbidden direction, while \(rhaJ\) and \(rhaK\) cross-block
the two cover-specific descents exactly as before.

Finally, for every \(n\ge6\), take
\begin{equation}
 p=1,\qquad q=n-5,\qquad A=4+\frac{1}{n-3}.                \tag{PXC106f}
\end{equation}
Both inequalities in (PXC106a) are then strict.  Every added player has
payoff one and is non-null: for \(i\in J\),
\(v(rhJ)-v(rh(J\setminus\{i\}))=p>0\), and the analogous \(K\)-marginal
is \(q>0\).  The \(a,b\) marginals over \(rh\) equal \(4\), while either
top player's grand-complement marginal is \(16+A+m\).  Thus this is not
null-player padding.  This proves a genuine cross-cover counterexample for
every dimension \(n\ge6\).

There is an exact general obstruction alternative behind these two families.
Fix an instance and complete \({\cal C}_x\), let \(\mathfrak R\) be its
finite set of qualifying extreme top covers, and let
\({\cal U}_z=\bigcup_{{\cal R}\in\mathfrak R}{\cal R}\).  For each cover
\({\cal R}\), write \({\cal D}_{\cal R}\) for the union of all nonzero
homogeneous later-stage directions satisfying the corresponding PXC92b
tangent and descent conditions for the \({\cal C}_x\cup{\cal R}\) lift.
Then
\begin{equation}
 \text{every extreme-cover lift fails}
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 {\cal D}_{\cal R}\ne\varnothing
 \quad\text{for every }{\cal R}\in\mathfrak R.               \tag{PXC107a}
\end{equation}
Whenever (PXC107a) holds, there is an exact dichotomy.

First, if
\(\bigcap_{{\cal R}\in\mathfrak R}{\cal D}_{\cal R}\ne\varnothing\),
PXC103 applies: a common direction vanishes on every row in
\({\cal U}_z\), and
full-game Lmax optimality supplies a blocker outside that union.  Second, if
there is no common direction, every \(d\in{\cal D}_{\cal R}\) satisfies
\begin{equation}
                 d(Q)<0\qquad\text{for some }Q\in{\cal U}_z. \tag{PXC107b}
\end{equation}
Indeed, if instead \(d(Q)\ge0\) on the entire union, dotting \(d\) with the
normalized identity of every cover \({\cal R}'\) gives
\(\sum_{Q\in{\cal R}'}\lambda_Qd(Q)=0\).  Strictly positive weights force
\(d(Q)=0\) on every cover support, so \(d\) belongs to every
\({\cal D}_{{\cal R}'}\), contradicting the absence of a common direction.

Consequently form a directed graph on \(\mathfrak R\): choose
\(d_{\cal R}\in{\cal D}_{\cal R}\), choose a row
\(Q\in{\cal U}_z\) with \(d_{\cal R}(Q)<0\), and draw
\({\cal R}\to{\cal R}'\) for a cover \({\cal R}'\ni Q\).  Every vertex has
an outgoing edge and no self-loop, so the graph contains a directed cycle of
length at least two:
\begin{equation}
 {\cal R}_1\longrightarrow{\cal R}_2\longrightarrow\cdots
 \longrightarrow{\cal R}_s\longrightarrow{\cal R}_1,
 \qquad s\ge2.                                           \tag{PXC107c}
\end{equation}

The proof uses no enumeration.  Positive weights and the normalized cover
identity force \(d(S)=0\) on every row \(S\in{\cal R}\).  Since the full
game has upper selector \(z\), a forbidden lift direction must violate some
original active coalition row.  In the common case every such blocker is
outside the cover union; otherwise (PXC107b) supplies the displayed outgoing
edge and finiteness gives the cycle.  Thus PXC98 realizes the common
forced-zero branch and PXC104--PXC106 realize the cross-cover-cycle branch.
There is no third cover-local forced-zero case: the cover identities collapse
it to the common branch.

The preceding graph can be made invariant; no choices of directions or
blockers are needed in its definition.  Define the
\emph{cover--blocker incidence digraph}
\(\Gamma(v,x,z,{\cal C}_x)\) to have vertex set \(\mathfrak R\) and the edge
\begin{equation}
 {\cal R}\longrightarrow{\cal R}'
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \exists\,d\in{\cal D}_{\cal R}\ \exists\,Q\in{\cal R}'
 \quad d(Q)<0.                                                \tag{PXC108a}
\end{equation}
This relation is determined by the retained-row tangent cones, not by a
selected witness.  It has no loop because positive cover weights force
\(d(S)=0\) for every \(S\in{\cal R}\).  Under universal-cover failure and
absence of a common direction, the collapse argument in (PXC107b) says that
every vertex has positive outdegree.  Hence every shortest directed cycle in
\(\Gamma\) has length at least two and is chordless: any additional directed
edge between nonconsecutive cycle vertices, or a reverse edge between
consecutive vertices on a cycle of length greater than two, closes a shorter
directed cycle.  Thus the invariant obstruction core is
\begin{equation}
 \begin{cases}
 (d,Q),&
 d\in\bigcap_{\cal R}{\cal D}_{\cal R},\
 Q\notin{\cal U}_z,\ d(Q)<0;\\[2mm]
 ({\cal R}_1,Q_1,d_1;\ldots;{\cal R}_s,Q_s,d_s),&
 {\cal R}_1\to\cdots\to{\cal R}_s\to{\cal R}_1
 \text{ a shortest cycle},
 \end{cases}                                                 \tag{PXC108b}
\end{equation}
where \(Q_i\in{\cal R}_{i+1}\) and \(d_i(Q_i)<0\).  The digraph and the set
of its shortest cycles are canonical, although a graph with symmetry need
not have a unique shortest cycle.  Deleting any edge from the second
certificate destroys that displayed cyclic certificate; this is the precise
deletion-minimal meaning used here.  It is not a claim that arbitrary removal
of an extreme cover from the original cover polytope preserves the same
quantified problem.

There are useful rank and support bounds.  Let
\(T=\operatorname{Top}(z)=\{r,h\}\), \(U=N\setminus T\), \(u=n-2\), and let \(A_{\cal R}\)
be the \(U\)-by-\(|{\cal R}|\) incidence matrix of a positive-ambient
extreme cover.  Its outsider equations are
\[
 A_{\cal R}\lambda=\eta{\bf1}_U.
\]
Since \(\eta>0\), \({\bf1}_U\) lies in the column span of \(A_{\cal R}\).
If \(A_{\cal R}\) had column rank below \(|{\cal R}|\), adjoining the one
normalization equation could not isolate an extreme point with all displayed
weights positive.  Therefore
\begin{equation}
 \operatorname{rank} A_{\cal R}=|{\cal R}|\le u.              \tag{PXC108c}
\end{equation}
If equality holds, fixing the two top coordinates and using equality in the
positive cover identity gives
\(A_{\cal R}^{\mathsf T}d_U=0\), hence \(d_U=0\).  Such a cover is faithful
at every later Lmax stratum.  Consequently a failing positive-ambient cover
has support at most \(n-3\).  When \(\eta=0\), nonnegative incidence and
positive row weights force every support row to avoid \(U\); singleton
normalization excludes a positive-payoff top singleton, leaving only the
tight top pair \(rh\), with support one.  If \(M_z\) proper \(z\)-tight rows
are cover-eligible, the crude finite bounds are therefore
\[
 2\le s\le|\mathfrak R|
 \le 1+\sum_{j=1}^{n-3}\binom{M_z}{j}
\]
on a universal-failure cycle, with the initial \(1\) needed only for the
possible zero-ambient pair cover.

The word circuit can now be used precisely, but only at the correct level
\citep{BjornerEtAl1999}.
For a cover \({\cal R}\), append to each row column its number of top
players, append the ambient column
\((-{\bf1}_U,-2)\) when \(\eta>0\), and put
\(b=(0_U,1)\).  The active normalized-cover equations are
\[
             B_{\cal R}\theta=b,\qquad \theta>0.
\]
At an extreme cover the active columns are independent.  Because every
coefficient in the displayed representation of \(b\) is nonzero,
the columns of \(B_{\cal R}\) together with \(-b\) form a minimally dependent
signed set: an oriented-matroid circuit containing the distinguished
normalization target.  By contrast, an edge direction \(d\) supplies a
covector sign pattern on the contracted coalition-normal arrangement.
Accordingly (PXC108b) is a circuit--covector incidence cycle; in general the
whole cycle is not one larger oriented-matroid circuit.  Nor does the
argument alone make it an indecomposable semi-balanced system or a facet of
the exact-game cone: those are game-value relations, whereas
\(\Gamma\) also depends on \({\cal C}_x\), the payoff strata, and staged
selector signs.  Any such stronger identification requires a separate
projection/facet theorem.

This distinction also separates the construction from the three closest
coalitional precedents.  Reijnierse--Derks exact covers characterize
coalition collections through their zero core; they do not carry ordered
payoff strata or a pair of selected core points
\citep{ReijnierseDerks1998}.  Minimal exact balanced collections allow the
single exceptional sign needed to test exactness and have their own
three-type generation theory \citep{LohmannBormHerings2012}; the augmented
positive top-cover circuit above is not identified with one of those types.
Finally, indecomposable semi-balanced systems correspond to facets of the
full exact-game cone \citep{StudenyKratochvil2022}, whereas the edge relation
in (PXC108a) changes when \({\cal C}_x\) or the Lmax stratum order changes
while the underlying game-value cone does not.  The standard ingredients are
therefore prior art.  The candidate new statement is only their
selector-specific coupling in the invariant cover--blocker graph and its
forced common/cyclic alternative; that novelty assessment remains subject to
specialist review.

This gives an exact recognition algorithm.  Enumerate the qualifying extreme
covers; for every cover, later payoff stratum, and designated player solve
the rational PXC92b tangent feasibility problem.  A cover with no feasible
problem is faithful.  If all fail, impose every cover-union row as an
equality and solve the same finite list to detect the common branch.
Otherwise, for each ordered cover pair and target-support row, add the
normalized inequality \(d(Q)\le-1\); feasibility creates exactly the edge
in (PXC108a).  Breadth-first search returns a shortest chordless cycle and
the LP solutions give checkable rational certificates.  With
\(c=|\mathfrak R|\), this uses \(O(c^2M_zn)\) rational LPs after cover
enumeration and it tests every later stratum, not only the first residual
ceiling.

On the displayed calibrations, the primary verifier exhausts the
complete-\({\cal C}_x\) and extreme-cover quantifiers.  A solver-free rational
active-set implementation independently reconstructs the games, selectors,
cover systems, reduced lifts, and common kernels:
\begin{verbatim}
cd verifiers/divergence
python3 verify_pxc104_cover_dependent_counterexample.py \
  --output pxc104_cover_dependent_counterexample.json
python3 verify_pxc104_cover_dependent_\
counterexample_independent.py \
  --output pxc104_cover_dependent_\
counterexample_independent.json
\end{verbatim}
The affine family has a separate primary audit of all sixty-four endpoint
coalition programs and five interior rational anchors, and an independently
formulated active-set replay:
\begin{verbatim}
python3 verify_pxc105_cross_cover_family.py \
  --output pxc105_cross_cover_family.json
python3 verify_pxc105_cross_cover_family_\
independent.py \
  --output pxc105_cross_cover_family_\
independent.json
\end{verbatim}
The block lift is checked by exact full-game replays at dimensions six,
seven, and eight and by a standalone dimension-free incidence audit:
\begin{verbatim}
python3 verify_pxc106_cross_cover_dimension_lift.py \
  --output pxc106_cross_cover_dimension_lift.json
python3 verify_pxc106_cross_cover_dimension_\
lift_independent.py \
  --output pxc106_cross_cover_dimension_\
lift_independent.json
\end{verbatim}
The obstruction alternative has a standalone exact calibration on PXC98,
PXC104, and the faithful full-rank PXC90 control:
\begin{verbatim}
python3 verify_pxc107_obstruction_alternative.py \
  --output pxc107_obstruction_alternative.json
python3 verify_pxc108_invariant_cover_blocker_circuits.py \
  --output pxc108_invariant_cover_blocker_circuits.json
python3 verify_pxc108_invariant_cover_blocker_circuits_independent.py \
  --output pxc108_invariant_cover_blocker_circuits_independent.json
\end{verbatim}

The exact-cone projection can now be stated precisely.  Fix \(n\) and a
finite \emph{selector signature} \(\Sigma\): ordered equality strata for
\(x,z\), their active proper core rows, a fixed complete \({\cal C}_x\), the
active zero-coordinate normals, the qualifying extreme-cover supports, and
an edge/nonedge pattern for (PXC108a).  In the variables \((v,x,z)\), let

\begin{equation}
\begin{split}
 {\cal K}_\Sigma=\{(v,x,z):\;&v\in{\cal E}_n,\quad v(i)=0,\\
 &x(N)=z(N)=v(N),\quad x(S),z(S)\ge v(S),\\
 &x(S)=v(S)\ (S\in A_x),\quad z(S)=v(S)\ (S\in A_z),\\
 &\text{the prescribed inactive-row and stratum inequalities are strict}\}.
\end{split}
\tag{PXC109a}
\end{equation}
Here \({\cal E}_n\) is the exact-game cone, equivalently the cone cut out by
the semi-balanced inequalities of \citet{StudenyKratochvil2022}.  The
PXC92b tangent test depends only on the incidence normals in
\(A_x,A_z,{\cal C}_x\), the zero set, and the ordered strata.  Thus an
admissible signature records its rational Farkas certificates once and for
all; the same is true of every cover vertex and every edge or nonedge in
(PXC108a).  It follows that \({\cal K}_\Sigma\) is a relatively open rational
polyhedral cone.  Linear projection gives

\begin{equation}
 {\cal F}_n=
 \bigcup_{\substack{\Sigma:\ {\mathfrak R}_\Sigma\ne\varnothing\\
                    {\cal D}_{\cal R}\ne\varnothing\ \forall {\cal R}}}
       \pi_v({\cal K}_\Sigma),                              \tag{PXC109b}
\end{equation}
where \({\cal F}_n\) is the fixed-\(n\), exactly-two-top,
fixed-\({\cal C}_x\) failure locus after retaining \({\cal C}_x\) as part of
the signature.  Its projection to games records existence of a bad fixed
certificate.  The alternative quantifier requiring failure for every
complete \({\cal C}_x\) is also a finite Boolean combination of these cells
and, after distributing that finite combination, a finite union of
relatively open rational polyhedra.  The union in (PXC109b) is finite, and
every member is the projection of a relatively open rational polyhedral
cone.  This is an explicit selector-augmented cone description: one obtains
inequalities by eliminating \(x,z\) and the finitely many rational Farkas
variables.  It is not a claim that \({\cal F}_n\) is convex, closed, or a
