face of \({\cal E}_n\).  Indeed the strict payoff order and descent signs
normally put a PXC108 type in the relative interior of a selector cell rather
than on one of its walls.

There is nevertheless a useful dimension-free facet bridge.  Let
\(T\subsetneq N\), and let the nonempty blocks
\(B_1,\ldots,B_m\), \(m\ge2\), partition \(N\setminus T\).  Then

\begin{equation}
 \sum_{i=1}^m {\bf1}_{T\cup B_i}-(m-1){\bf1}_T={\bf1}_N.     \tag{PXC110a}
\end{equation}
The supported columns are independent: looking on each block first forces
the coefficient of its unique row, and then the coefficient of \(T\), to
vanish in any homogeneous dependence.  Thus (PXC110a) is support-minimal,
and its empty-set coefficient is zero.  It is in fact an indecomposable
min-semi-balanced system.  To see this directly, suppose a new exceptional
coalition \(E\) has coefficient \(-1\), let \(c\) be the common load, and put
\(e_T,e_i\in\{0,1\}\) for its membership on \(T,B_i\).  Membership must be
constant on every block, and the nonnegative old coefficients are forced to
be

\begin{equation}
 u_i=c+e_i,\qquad
 u_T=-(m-1)c+e_T-\sum_i e_i.                    \tag{PXC110b}
\end{equation}
If some block is outside \(E\), then \(c\ge0\), and (PXC110b) leaves only
\(E=T\) or \(E=T\cup B_i\), besides the empty set.  If every block is in
\(E\), it leaves only \(E=N\).  Hence no unsupported nonempty proper
exceptional coalition decomposes (PXC110a), and the
Studen\'y--Kratochv\'il criterion makes its inequality

\begin{equation}
 \sum_i v(T\cup B_i)-(m-1)v(T)\le v(N)             \tag{PXC110c}
\end{equation}
a facet of the exact-game cone.

This facet appears naturally when a two-top normalized cover has equal top
coefficients.  If \(T=\{r,h\}\),
\(\alpha_r=\alpha_h=\alpha>0\), and the positive support partitions the
outsiders into the traces \(B_i\), the cover identity can be scaled to
(PXC110a).  For unequal top coefficients no single coalition contained in
\(T\) can absorb both exceptional coordinates.  Thus equal-top
\emph{pair shear} is a sufficient exact-cone bridge, not a consequence of
cover extremality alone.

The displayed families now have a sharp exact-cone interpretation.  For
(PXC101), the positive cover and the tight pair give the partition-star
facet

\begin{equation}
 v(rha)+v(rhb)+v(rhO)-2v(rh)\le v(N).              \tag{PXC111a}
\end{equation}
The forced-zero row \(rj\) is external to that facet support: the obstruction
is a facet--blocker flag, not the facet by itself.  At the generic
six-player point \((M,q,J,K)=(15/4,3,2,1)\), the complete published
six-player catalogue has \(552\) tight facet inequalities whose normals have
rank \(54\).  Thus the game lies on a nine-dimensional minimal exact-cone
face; the selector obstruction is much finer than merely lying on one
facet.

For (PXC104)--(PXC105), the two positive covers give the two facets

\begin{equation}
\begin{split}
 v(rhb)+v(rhj)+v(rhak)-2v(rh)&\le v(N),\\
 v(rhb)+v(rhaj)+v(rhk)-2v(rh)&\le v(N).
\end{split}
\tag{PXC111b}
\end{equation}
The row \(rhaj\) belongs only to the second support and cuts the descent of
the first; \(rhak\) belongs only to the first and cuts the descent of the
second.  Hence the PXC108 two-cycle is exactly the circuit--covector incidence
graph of a minimal union of two indecomposable exact-cone facets.  All three
rational anchors \(A=17/4,5,21/4\) have the same \(835\) tight catalogue
facets, of normal rank \(53\), so their minimal exact face has dimension ten.
The conic PXC105 family has dimension two inside that face; the failure
interval is a selector-cell interior, not a new facet.

The same statement is genuinely dimension-free for (PXC106).  Its two block
partitions are

\[
 \{b\}\mid J\mid(\{a\}\cup K),\qquad
 \{b\}\mid(\{a\}\cup J)\mid K.
\]
Applying (PXC110) to each proves that the all-\(n\) cross-cover obstruction
is a two-facet dependency cycle for every nonempty \(J,K\).  This is stronger
than the finite PXC104 catalogue lookup, but the partition-star facets
themselves are an instance of the established semi-balanced theory; the new
candidate contribution is their staged-selector coupling.

The unrestricted proposed correspondence is false.  In (PXC88), the unique
positive cover has rows \(0134,0125,16\), top coefficients
\((1/3,2/3)\), and indispensable later blockers \(0126,0136\).  Form the
seven-by-six matrix whose columns are the five displayed coalition incidence
vectors and \(-{\bf1}_N\).  Its minor on player rows \(0,\ldots,5\) has
determinant \(-1\).  Hence the columns are independent: no nonempty subset of
the natural cover--blocker support has even a constant incidence dependence,
let alone an indecomposable semi-balanced one.  PXC88 is therefore an exact
counterexample to the assertion that every inclusion-minimal PXC108
obstruction projects to one exact-cone facet or a union of facets supported
on its obstruction rows.  It is the smallest certified unequal-top
counterexample to this projection claim in the present record; no
six-player minimality theorem is asserted.

The strongest correct replacement is consequently three-level.  Every
fixed combinatorial type has the rational polyhedral projection (PXC109).
Every equal-top partition cover has the canonical facet lift (PXC110).
Cycles made from such covers are facet-dependency cycles, as in
(PXC104)--(PXC106); general PXC108 obstructions remain circuit--covector
decorations of selector cells and need not contain an exact-cone facet.
This also explains why oriented-matroid programming is the right local
language while directed-cycle terminology alone does not prove a new
matroid circuit or exact-cone facet.

The preceding description cannot be made independent of the retained
complete certificate by simply forgetting \({\cal C}_x\).  A six-player
counterexample already occurs by adding one common-tight row to (PXC98).
With players \((r,h,a,b,j,k)=(0,\ldots,5)\), total \(17\), and lower rows

\begin{equation}
 rha\ge11,\quad rhb\ge11,\quad rj\ge6,\quad
 rhaj\ge13,\quad rhjk\ge11,                              \tag{PXC112a}
\end{equation}

let \(v\) be the lower envelope of the resulting compact nonnegative
polytope.  The old PXC98 selectors remain feasible after the extra row is
added.  Since the new core is a subset of the old one, the two opposite
lexicographic optimality inequalities sandwich them, and hence

\[
 x=(9/2,9/2,2,2,2,2),\qquad z=(4,4,3,3,2,1).
\]
The six zero-coordinate witnesses displayed by the verifier prove singleton
normalization, and lower-envelope attainment proves exactness.  The complete
proper-row \(x\)-tight families are exactly

\[
 {\cal C}_1=\{rha,rhb\},\qquad
 {\cal C}_2=\{rhb,rhaj\},\qquad
 {\cal C}_3=\{rha,rhb,rhaj\}.                            \tag{PXC112b}
\]

Indeed the independent verifier recomputes Lmin for all eight subsets of the
full \(x\)-tight menu: precisely the three families in (PXC112b) return
\(x\).  The full proper \(z\)-tight menu is

\[
 \{rh,rha,rhb,rj,rhaj,rhjk\},
\]
and its normalized-cover polytope still has exactly the two extreme covers

\[
 {\cal R}_0=\{rh\},\quad\lambda=1/2, \eta=0;\qquad
 {\cal R}_1=\{rha,rhb,rhjk\},\quad
 \lambda=(1/4,1/4,1/4), \eta=1/4.                        \tag{PXC112c}
\]

The exact sparse selectors form the matrix

\begin{equation}
\begin{array}{c|cc}
 &{\cal R}_0&{\cal R}_1\\ \hline
{\cal C}_1&(4,4,3,3,3/2,3/2)&(4,4,3,3,3/2,3/2)\\
{\cal C}_2&(4,4,5/2,3,5/2,1)&z\\
{\cal C}_3&z&z.
\end{array}                                                \tag{PXC112d}
\end{equation}

Every entry retains Lmin \(x\) and singleton normalization.  For
\({\cal C}_1\), the common forbidden direction
\(d=(0,0,0,0,-1/2,1/2)\) cuts both \(rj\) and \(rhaj\) while vanishing on the
cover union.  For \({\cal C}_2\) and the pair cover,
\(d=(0,0,-1/2,0,1/2,0)\) cuts \(rha\); the positive cover contains that row
and, together with \(rhaj\), pins \(z\).  The third family pins both.  Thus
the same exact game and selector pair has, according to the complete
certificate, a universal common-blocker obstruction, a mixed faithful/failing
fiber, or no failure at all.  This disproves a game-only, certificate-blind
PXC108 graph, including both universal- and primitive-generator readings.

There is a sharp canonical replacement.  Let
\(\mathfrak C_x(v,x)\) be the finite set of complete proper-row families.
For \({\cal C}\in\mathfrak C_x(v,x)\), retain the two intrinsic tangent
cones

\begin{equation}
\begin{split}
 T_x({\cal C})&=\{d:{\bf1}d=0, d(S)\ge0\ (S\in{\cal C}),
                         \ d_i\ge0\ (x_i=0)\},\\
 T_z({\cal C})&=\{d:{\bf1}d=0, d(S)\ge0\
       \ (S\in{\cal C},\ x(S)=z(S)),\ d_i\ge0\ (z_i=0)\}.
\end{split}
                                                               \tag{PXC112e}
\end{equation}

Declare \({\cal C}\sim{\cal C}'\) when both cones in (PXC112e) agree.
Then every cover lift has the same Lmin-\(x\) and Lmax-\(z\) fidelity
verdicts, the same PXC92b descent sets, and the same PXC108 graph for
equivalent certificates.  This is immediate by intersecting equal tangent
cones with the same active cover-row halfspaces and applying the
necessary-and-sufficient staged tangent test.  If \(z\) is not preserved, the
eventual altered Lmax allocation is a global polytope datum and need not be
determined by these local cones; no such stronger claim is made.
Nor is tangent-cone equality asserted to be the coarsest possible
graph-preserving equivalence; it is the canonical intrinsic quotient proved
here to preserve every staged test simultaneously.
Thus the representation-independent object is not one graph per game but the
finite fiber

\[
 \bigl\{\Gamma(v,x,z,[{\cal C}]):
          [{\cal C}]\in\mathfrak C_x(v,x)/{\sim}\bigr\}.     \tag{PXC112f}
\]

This fiber itself varies on a canonical rational polyhedral stratification.
In the extended \((v,x,z)\)-space, intersect the exact/core/efficiency cone
with the central hyperplanes of

\[
 x_i,\ z_i,\ x_i-x_j,\ z_i-z_j,\ x(S)-v(S),\ z(S)-v(S),
 \ x(S)-z(S).
\]
The relatively open faces form a finite rational polyhedral fan.  Selector
validity is constant on each face by the staged tangent criterion, so discard
the faces on which \(x,z\) are not the claimed selectors.  On every remaining
face, the payoff strata, zero sets, active menus, complete tangent-cone
classes, extreme-cover circuits, and every PXC108 edge are constant.  Hence
(PXC112f) is a finite \emph{fan-supported fibered selector stratification}.
The selector-valid cells need not themselves be closed under taking faces,
so no subfan claim is made.  Projection to game space
recovers the finite polyhedral projection (PXC109), but the PXC112 game has
three different points in its discrete certificate fiber.  Therefore that
projection is not a single-valued obstruction fan unless the fiber is
retained.  This is consistent with the standard degeneracy warning in
multiparametric LP: without a lexicographic representation convention,
critical-region descriptions can overlap and fail to meet facet-to-facet
\citep{JonesKerriganMaciejowski2007}.

On an important generic subarm the cyclic fiber has a much stronger
rank structure.  Order the \(u=n-2\) residual players strictly by
their \(z\)-payoffs, assume no common \({\cal C}_x\) row is active at \(z\),
and let \(A_{\cal R}\) contain the outsider columns of a positive-ambient
extreme cover.  Put \(K_{\cal R}=\ker A_{\cal R}^{\mathsf T}\).  Its later
descent set is exactly the lex-negative half

\[
 D(K)=\{d\in K\setminus\{0\}:d_p<0,\quad
        p=\min\{i:d_i\ne0\}\}.
\]
If \(p\) is the first coordinate not fixed by \(K^\perp\), then

\begin{equation}
 D(K)^*=K^\perp+\mathbb R_{\le0}e_p.                       \tag{PXC113a}
\end{equation}

For if \(d^0_p=-1\), adding an arbitrary vector of
\(K\cap\{d_p=0\}\) to \(d^0\) remains lex-negative.  Every element of the
polar must therefore vanish on that subspace; its restriction to \(K\) is a
nonpositive multiple of the \(p\)-coordinate.  This proves (PXC113a), and
the converse is immediate.

After rescaling their positive ambient loads, let \(A,B\) be two positive
balanced cover matrices,
\(A\lambda={\bf1}\), \(B\mu={\bf1}\), with all displayed weights positive.
If there is no edge \(B\to A\), every column \(a_j\) lies in the polar in
(PXC113a), so

\[
 a_j=B\beta_j+c_je_p,\qquad c_j\le0.
\]
Multiplying by the positive \(\lambda_j\)'s and summing gives
\({\bf1}\in\operatorname{col}B\) plus
\((\sum_j\lambda_jc_j)e_p\).  Since
\(e_p\notin\operatorname{col}B\), the latter coefficient is zero; positivity
and \(c_j\le0\) force every \(c_j=0\).  Hence

\begin{equation}
 A\to B\ \hbox{and}\ B\not\to A
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \operatorname{col}A\subsetneq\operatorname{col}B,
 \quad \operatorname{rank}A<\operatorname{rank}B.          \tag{PXC113b}
\end{equation}

Thus outsider rank is a strict potential on every asymmetric edge.  Every
directed cycle contains a reciprocal pair, and every shortest cyclic
PXC108 obstruction in this arm has length two.  This is a genuine
dimension-free forbidden-long-cycle theorem, not a finite census.

For partition covers the statement becomes completely combinatorial.  If
\(P,Q\) are partitions of the residual players and the cover rows are the
top pair joined to their blocks, then

\begin{equation}
 P\longrightarrow Q
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 Q\hbox{ splits a block of }P.                              \tag{PXC113c}
\end{equation}

Indeed a split block permits a transfer from its earliest separated member
to a later one; if \(P\) refines \(Q\), every \(Q\)-block indicator lies in
the span of the \(P\)-block indicators.  Consequently asymmetric edges are
strict partition refinements and reciprocal edges are exactly incomparable
partition pairs.  The PXC104--PXC106 cross-cover pattern is the primitive
incomparable-pair generator.  Their tied residual strata remain covered by
their direct proofs in (PXC105)--(PXC111), not by the strict-stratum
hypothesis of (PXC113).

The exact incidence audit enumerates all positive, independent,
rank-deficient \(0/1\) supports through five outsiders.  At \(u=5\) it
finds 315 failing supports, 89{,}610 directed edges, and 4{,}600 asymmetric
edges; every asymmetric edge goes \(2\to3\), \(2\to4\), or \(3\to4\) in
rank.  A separate exact-SMT encoding reconstructs the complete \(u=4\)
rank-deficient universe: 19 supports, 294 edges, 36 asymmetric edges, all
\(2\to3\).  The partition-lattice audit checks every ordered pair through
\(u=6\).
These computations audit the hypotheses and signs of (PXC113); the proof is
the polar-and-positivity argument above.

The rank potential is a shadow of a stronger order which removes all of
those restrictions.  Return to an arbitrary fixed instance, complete
\({\cal C}_x\), payoff strata (ties allowed), active zero-coordinate normals,
and qualifying extreme covers as in (PXC107)--(PXC108).  Then, for any two
covers,

\begin{equation}
 {\cal R}'\not\longrightarrow{\cal R}
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 {\cal D}_{{\cal R}'}\subseteq{\cal D}_{\cal R}.             \tag{PXC114a}
\end{equation}

Indeed take \(d\in{\cal D}_{{\cal R}'}\).  The missing edge says
\(d(S)\ge0\) for every \(S\in{\cal R}\).  Dotting \(d\) with the normalized
identity for \({\cal R}\) gives
\[
 \sum_{S\in{\cal R}}\lambda_Sd(S)
 =\eta d(N)+\sum_{i\in\operatorname{Top}(z)}\alpha_i d_i=0.
\]
Here efficiency gives \(d(N)=0\), and every top coordinate was fixed at the
earlier Lmax stage.  Since every support weight is strictly positive, all
the nonnegative row derivatives are zero.  Thus \(d\) satisfies the
\({\cal R}\)-row tangent conditions.  The retained common-\({\cal C}_x\)
inequalities, active nonnegativity normals, and staged descent conditions are
the same for both covers, proving (PXC114a).

If \({\cal R}\to{\cal R}'\), its witnessing direction cuts a row of
\({\cal R}'\) and therefore cannot belong to
\({\cal D}_{{\cal R}'}\).  Consequently

\begin{equation}
 {\cal R}\longrightarrow{\cal R}',\qquad
 {\cal R}'\not\longrightarrow{\cal R}
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 {\cal D}_{{\cal R}'}\subsetneq{\cal D}_{\cal R}.            \tag{PXC114b}
\end{equation}

Set inclusion is therefore a strict potential on every asymmetric edge,
in every dimension.  A directed cycle made only of asymmetric edges would
give an impossible strict inclusion cycle.  Hence every directed cycle in
\(\Gamma(v,x,z,[{\cal C}_x])\) contains a reciprocal edge, and whenever the
graph has any cycle it has a two-cycle.  Combining this with (PXC107) gives
the dimension-free primitive-mechanism decomposition:

\begin{equation}
\begin{split}
\text{universal extreme-cover failure}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
&\bigcap_{\cal R}{\cal D}_{\cal R}\ne\varnothing,\\
&\text{or, if that intersection is empty, }
 {\cal R}\rightleftarrows{\cal R}'\text{ for some pair.}
\end{split}
                                                               \tag{PXC114c}
\end{equation}

In the first branch, full-game Lmax optimality supplies the outside-union
blocker of (PXC103); in the second, the two covers cross-block one another.
Thus PXC101 and PXC104--PXC106 are canonical prototypes of the two primitive
mechanisms.  This is a mechanism-level generation theorem, not the stronger
and presently unsupported claim that every coalition-incidence support has
one of those displayed games as an oriented-matroid minor.  It holds on each
tangent-cone class of the PXC112 certificate fiber and is therefore
certificate-independent after the stated quotient.

Accordingly, the literal assertion that every inclusion-minimal sparse
failure is a circuit--covector \emph{cycle} is false: the PXC101 common
branch has no cover cycle.  The exact corrected statement is (PXC114c), and
its cyclic branch has a deletion-minimal reciprocal pair.  Because the proof
is dimension-free, it subsumes the requested \(n\le6\) graph-mechanism
classification; it does not enumerate all labelled coalition-incidence
realizations within either mechanism.

The finite active-\({\cal C}_x\) search through all 576 four-outsider trace
families of size at most three found no asymmetric cycle before (PXC114) was
recognized.  That floating run is retained only as discovery provenance:
(PXC114) is the conventional dimension-free proof, while exact finite audits
serve as sign and implementation controls.

None of (PXC112)--(PXC114) identifies the cover graph with the directed
cocircuit graph of a standard oriented-matroid program.  The cover columns
are realizable circuits, but PXC108 edges compare different cover circuits
through staged tangent covectors.  Directed pivot cycles in oriented-matroid
programming instead diagnose non-Euclidean programs; conflating the two
notions would contradict their distinct vertex and edge definitions
\citep{BjornerEtAl1999}.  Oriented-matroid complementarity similarly studies
complementary solutions, sufficiency, and criss-cross pivots inside one
program, not edges between distinct positive cover circuits
\citep{FukudaTerlaky1992}.  The new candidate statement is the fan-supported
fibered
descent-set potential for this selector-specific circuit--covector coupling.

There is a sharp obstruction to strengthening this mechanism theorem into a
literal forbidden-player-minor theorem.  For a game \(v\), write
\[
 (v\setminus i)(S)=v(S),\qquad
 (v/i)(S)=v(S\cup\{i\})-v(\{i\})
\]
for restriction and contraction, zero-normalizing the latter by subtracting
its singleton values.  If \(i,j\) are amalgamated to a player \(*\), put
\begin{equation}
 v^{ij}(S)=
 \begin{cases}
 v(S),& *\notin S,\\
 v((S\setminus\{*\})\cup\{i,j\}),& *\in S.
 \end{cases}                                                \tag{PXC115a}
\end{equation}
This is the standard quotient-game convention for a coalition structure:
the worth of a coalition of blocks is the original worth of their union
\citep{ZouEtAl2023}.  Pairwise mergers are known to alter cores in general;
core preservation requires additional structure even in the positive
matching-game result of \citet{Hezarkhani2016}.

For a fixed sparse lower-envelope presentation, call \(i,j\)
\emph{defining-row parallel} when

\[
 i\in S\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad j\in S
 \qquad\hbox{for every displayed defining row }S.
\]

The literal parallel-element removal audited below is restriction by either
player under this condition together with \(x_i=x_j\) and \(z_i=z_j\).
Thus this is an explicitly presentation-relative sparse-row schema, not an
unstated claim that the game has an intrinsic matroid representation.
Selector-zero reduction likewise means restriction by \(i\) under the sole
additional hypothesis \(z_i=0\); relabeling is transport by a permutation.

Relabeling is equivariant for the game, both selectors, complete
\({\cal C}_x\) families, normalized covers, and their lift verdicts.  None of
the other audited literal schemas has the corresponding universal
preservation property.

First take the \(n=6\) PXC102 game and delete \(a\).  In the restricted game
\(w\) on \(\{r,h,b,j,k\}\),


\begin{equation}
 w(N')=w(N'\setminus\{j\})=11,\qquad w(rj)=6,\qquad w(r)=0.
                                                               \tag{PXC115b}
\end{equation}

Every core point of \(w\) has \(y_j=0\), hence \(y_r\ge6\).  Thus the
restriction is not exact: its stated singleton value \(w(r)=0\) is not the
minimum of \(y_r\) over its own core.

The same obstruction defeats literal parallel-element removal.  In the
\(n=7\) PXC102 game, the two bottom players have equal coordinates in both
selectors and identical incidence in every defining row.  Deleting either
one again gives
\[
 w(N')=w(N'\setminus\{j\})=11,\qquad w(rj)=6,\qquad w(r)=0,
\]
so its restricted core has \(y_r\ge6\).  Incidence parallelism and selector
equality therefore do not make ordinary removal exact.

Next take the \(n=7\) PXC102 game.  Its two bottom players have equal
coordinates in both selectors and identical incidence in every defining
row.  Contracting either one, followed by zero normalization, and
amalgamating the pair in (PXC115a) both give the same exact
singleton-normalized six-player lower-envelope game


\begin{equation}
 T=18,\qquad rha,rhb\ge11,\qquad rj\ge6,\qquad rhjk\ge12. \tag{PXC115c}
\end{equation}

Indeed projection by \(y_*=y_{o_1}+y_{o_2}\) proves the amalgamation claim.
For contraction, if a coalition contains the remaining bottom player then
the same projection applies; if it omits that player, put the contracted
coordinate equal to zero in an attaining allocation.  The converse lower
bound follows from nonnegativity.  Thus every coalition minimum agrees with
(PXC115c).  Its selectors are


\begin{equation}
 x'=(13/3,13/3,7/3,7/3,7/3,7/3),\qquad
 z'=(4,4,3,3,2,2).                                         \tag{PXC115d}
\end{equation}

The unique complete proper-row certificate is again \(\{rha,rhb\}\), and
the two extreme covers are \(\{rh\}\) and \(\{rha,rhb,rhjk\}\).  Both lifts
are faithful: the first ceiling fixes \(r=h=4\), the two certificate rows
fix \(a=b=3\), and efficiency equalizes the remaining tied pair at \(2\).
Consequently even an exact, normalized, divergent, two-top output of a
parallel contraction need not retain sparse-fidelity failure.

A zero selector coordinate is not a substitute for nullity.  The following
six allocations, in player order \((r,h,a,b,j,k)\), define an exact
singleton-normalized total-\(18\) game by their coordinatewise lower envelope:


\begin{equation}
\begin{array}{rrrrrr}
0&13&1&3&0&1\\
12&0&0&1&1&4\\
12&5&0&0&0&1\\
4&10&1&0&0&3\\
11&3&0&1&0&3\\
3&11&1&3&0&0.
\end{array}                                                  \tag{PXC116a}
\end{equation}

Every displayed allocation lies in the resulting core and one attains each
coalition minimum, proving exactness; each coordinate is zero in a displayed
allocation.  Exact staged optimization gives


\begin{equation}
 x=(25/4,25/4,1/2,9/4,1/2,9/4),\qquad
 z=(6,6,0,2,1,3).                                           \tag{PXC116b}
\end{equation}

The only proper \(x\)-tight rows are \(rhj\) and \(rhbk\), and both are
indispensable.  The proper \(z\)-tight menu is


\begin{equation}
 rh, a, rha, rhj, rhaj, rhak, rhbk, rhabk.            \tag{PXC116c}
\end{equation}

Solving the normalized cover equations gives exactly five extreme supports,


\begin{equation}
 \{rh\},\quad \{rhj,rhabk\},\quad \{rhaj,rhbk\},\quad
 \{a,rhj,rhbk\},\quad \{rha,rhj,rhbk\}.                   \tag{PXC116d}
\end{equation}

Their respective weights are \(1/2\), \((1/2)^2\), \((1/2)^2\),
\((1/2)^3\), and \((1/4)^3\).  Every lift has the
same lower selector \(x\) and upper selector


\begin{equation}
 z'=(6,6,0,5/2,1,5/2)\ne z.                                \tag{PXC116e}
\end{equation}

The common direction \(2(z'-z)=(0,0,0,1,0,-1)\) vanishes on
the complete certificate and the union of all five covers; the sole negative
tight row is \(rhak\), which has coefficient zero in every cover.  Hence
(PXC116) is a common-direction obstruction of the PXC103 branch.  It is not
relabeling-equivalent to PXC101, since the number of qualifying extreme
covers is five rather than two.  By the five-player theorem (PXC100), both
are dimension-minimal failures.  Therefore the claim that every
deletion-minimal common obstruction is literally the PXC101 incidence type
is false in the smallest possible dimension.

The same sparse failure also settles the exactness column for contraction.
Contracting \(r\) in (PXC116), then subtracting the contracted singleton
baselines, gives the five-player characteristic vector
\[
\begin{split}
 &(0,0,0,-1,0,-2,0,-3,0,1,0,0,0,-1,0,-2,\\
 &\hspace{8em}0,1,0,1,0,1,0,0,0,1,0,1,0,1,0,1).
\end{split}
\]
Its singleton \(h\) has stated worth zero, whereas every point in its core
has \(y_h\ge1\): the contracted rows \(hj\ge1\) and \(hk\ge1\), together
with efficiency one and nonnegativity, first force \(a=b=k=0\) and then
force \(h\ge1\).  The core point \((1,0,0,0,0)\) attains one.  Hence a
zero-normalized contraction can leave the exact cone even when its source is
an exact singleton-normalized sparse failure.  This is distinct from
(PXC115c), where contraction remains exact but destroys the selector-fidelity
verdict.

Deleting the zero-\(z\) player \(a\) from (PXC116) also leaves the exact-game
cone.  In that restriction, the old row \(rhbk\) forces \(y_j=0\); the rows
\(rhj\ge13\) and \(hbjk\ge6\), together with efficiency \(17\), give
\(y_h\ge2\), but the restricted characteristic function has \(w(h)=0\).
Thus deletion based only on \(z_i=0\) is invalid even inside the target
failure class.

Finally, even a genuine null player preserves less than the required
quantified object.  It makes the full core a product with \(\{0\}\) and hence
preserves both selectors, but duplicated tight rows change the extreme-cover
polytope.  Adjoin a null player \(e\) to PXC104 and add the old grand row to
\({\cal C}_x\), forcing \(y_e=0\).  The enlarged game has the faithful extreme
covers


\begin{equation}
\begin{split}
 &\{rhb,rhj,rhak,rhaje,rhke\},\\
 &\{rhb,rhaj,rhk,rhje,rhake\}.
\end{split}                                                  \tag{PXC115e}
\end{equation}

with weights \((1/4,1/8,1/8,1/8,1/8)\), ambient \(1/4\), and top
coefficients \((1/2,1/2)\).  After duplicate rows are projected, either
support together with \({\cal C}_x\) recovers every defining row of PXC104,
so its lift is the full core times \(\{0\}\).  The original PXC104 game has
cover failure, whereas its null extension does not.  Null equivalence is
therefore not cover-extremality equivalence.

The conclusion is a no-go theorem about the proposed proof strategy, not a
finite forbidden-minor theorem in the opposite direction.  Literal
restriction, normalized contraction, parallel amalgamation, selector-zero
deletion, and even bidirectional null equivalence do not furnish a universal
failure-preserving reduction.  Conditional reductions remain possible, but
their admissibility must be characterized without assuming the desired
failure verdict.  The strongest presently proved dimension-free replacement
is the PXC112 tangent-cone quotient together with the PXC114 common/reciprocal
descent-set decomposition.  The PXC118 construction below shows that the
reciprocal branch also has another dimension-minimal exact incidence type.

This yields a precise dimension-free impossibility statement for the
proposed natural-minor program.  Call an operation schema \emph{universally
safe} if, whenever it is applicable to an exact singleton-normalized sparse
failure tuple \((v,x,z,{\cal C}_x)\), it preserves exactness, singleton
normalization, the mapped selector and complete-certificate data, the
qualifying extreme-cover quantifier, and universal cover failure.  Among the
literal schemata audited above,
\begin{equation}
\begin{array}{c|c|c|c}
\text{schema}&\text{exactness}&\text{singleton normalization}
             &\text{full sparse-failure tuple}\\ \hline
\text{relabeling}&\checkmark&\checkmark&\checkmark\\
\text{restriction}&\times&\checkmark&\times\\
\text{zero-normalized contraction}&\times&\checkmark&\times\\
\text{parallel-element deletion}&\times&\checkmark&\times\\
\text{\(z_i=0\) deletion}&\times&\checkmark&\times\\
\text{amalgamation}&\checkmark&
        \Longleftrightarrow v(\{i,j\})=0&\times .
\end{array}                                                  \tag{PXC117a}
\end{equation}
The check in the last row is exactness, not selector fidelity.  Indeed an
exact game's quotient by any player partition is exact: for every union of
blocks, aggregate an original core allocation attaining that union.  The
aggregate lies in the quotient core and attains the quotient worth.  Its
new singleton at the amalgamated block is \(v(\{i,j\})\), proving the stated
normalization criterion.  PXC115c shows that even the normalized,
defining-row-parallel, selector-equal case can destroy the final verdict.
Restriction preserves the displayed singleton worths, and normalized
contraction has zero singleton worths by definition, but PXC115b and the
parallel-removal witness show that neither assertion implies exactness.
The PXC116 deletion supplies the same conclusion under the additional
hypothesis \(z_i=0\).  Thus the table records the exact universal guarantees;
once a schema can leave the object class, no downstream selector,
certificate, or cover guarantee exists.  More explicitly, among the listed
schemata only relabeling universally preserves complete-\({\cal C}_x\)
status, the qualifying extreme-cover family, and selector failure.  For
amalgamation, a certificate row does not even have a quotient image unless
it is a union of player blocks; block compatibility still does not rescue
the selector verdict, by (PXC115c).  The surviving relabeling row is
equivariant at every stage and for every cover equation.

Let the safe quasi-order be generated by these universally safe audited
schemata, and denote it by \(\preceq_{\rm safe}\).  By (PXC117a), it is
ground-set relabeling only.  The PXC101 chamber contains a non-null sparse
failure for every \(n\ge6\), and relabeling cannot change cardinality.  Hence
\begin{equation}
\begin{split}
 &\{\,P_k(4,3,2,1): k\ge2\,\}\\
 &\hspace{2em}\text{is an infinite \(\preceq_{\rm safe}\)-antichain}.
\end{split}                                                  \tag{PXC117b}
\end{equation}
Non-nullity here is conventional rather than inferred from a selector:
PXC102 exhibits a strictly positive marginal coalition for every player.
Consequently no finite forbidden-minor list can characterize sparse fidelity
under the listed universally safe natural operations.  The same conclusion
holds in the reciprocal branch using the all-dimensional PXC106 family, but
the common PXC101 antichain already suffices.  Infinite antichains and
infinite excluded-minor lists are standard phenomena outside
well-quasi-ordered classes \citep{Whittle2005}; the content here is the
selector-specific proof that the proposed cooperative-game operations do not
yield a richer safe order in the first place.

This is not a claim about every conditional minor relation one might invent.
An operation admitted only after recomputing exactness and the desired
failure verdict is circular as an obstruction theory.  A stronger relation
would need a local, verdict-independent admissibility criterion; no such
criterion is presently proved.  Modulo that open possibility, the strongest
viable dimension-free replacement is therefore
\begin{equation}
\begin{split}
 &\text{PXC112 staged-tangent certificate quotient}\\
 &\qquad+\ \text{PXC114 common/reciprocal descent-set decomposition}.
\end{split}                                                  \tag{PXC117c}
\end{equation}

The reciprocal prototype is not literally canonical either.  Return to the
six-player PXC104 game and adjoin the single lower-envelope row
\begin{equation}
                    y(ajk)\ge 7.                              \tag{PXC118a}
\end{equation}
Call the resulting game \(v^+\).  It is exact by compact lower-envelope
attainment and singleton-normalized.  For example, zero-coordinate core
witnesses for \(r,h,a,b,j,k\), respectively, are
\[
 (0,13,3,3,2,2),\ (13,0,3,3,2,2),\
 (0,16,0,0,2,5),\ (0,16,0,0,2,5),
 (0,13,5,3,0,2),\ (0,13,7,3,0,0).
\]
Both old selectors satisfy (PXC118a), in fact at equality.  Restricting a
feasible polytope while retaining an old lexicographic optimum cannot improve
past that optimum.  Hence the selectors remain
\begin{equation}
 x=\left((41/6)^2,(7/3)^4\right),\qquad
 z=(6,6,5,4,1,1).                                           \tag{PXC118b}
\end{equation}

The proper \(x\)-tight menu is now \(\{rha,rhb,ajk\}\).
The two old rooted rows still give the floor proof preceding (PXC104c), while
\(ajk\) is redundant for that proof.  Conversely, exact optimization after
omitting either rooted row gives, up to exchanging \(a,b\),
\((16/3,16/3,16/3,7/3,7/3,7/3)\), and \(ajk\) alone leaves the uniform
allocation \((23/6)^6\).  Thus all proper-row subsets are exhausted and the
complete certificates are exactly
\begin{equation}
             \{rha,rhb\},\qquad \{rha,rhb,ajk\}.              \tag{PXC118c}
\end{equation}

The proper \(z\)-tight menu is
\[
 \{rh,rhb,rhj,rhaj,rhk,rhak,ajk,rhajk\}.
\]
The last row is forced at value \(19\) by the disjoint tight lower bounds
\(rh\ge12\) and \(ajk\ge7\), and is attained at \(z\).  Write the eight
cover weights in the displayed order as
\((A,B,J,P,K,Q,C,D)\).  Outsider balance and top normalization give
\begin{equation}
\begin{gathered}
 B=\eta,\qquad J=Q,\qquad K=P,\qquad
 C+D=\eta-P-Q,\\
 A+2P+2Q+D=\tfrac12,\qquad
 A,C,D,P,Q\ge0.                                             \tag{PXC118d}
\end{gathered}
\end{equation}
Increasing \(\eta\) with \(B,C\) adds the balanced grand-vector recession
direction, so no vertex has \(C>0\).  On the resulting simplex the four and
only four extreme covers are
\begin{equation}
 \{rh\}:\tfrac12,\quad
 \{rhb,rhajk\}:\left(\tfrac12\right)^2,\quad
 \{rhb,rhj,rhak\}:\left(\tfrac14\right)^3,\quad
 \{rhb,rhaj,rhk\}:\left(\tfrac14\right)^3.                 \tag{PXC118e}
\end{equation}

For either certificate in (PXC118c), the four retained-row upper selectors
are, in the same order,
\begin{equation}
 (6,6,4,4,3/2,3/2),\quad
 (6,6,4,4,3/2,3/2),\quad
 (6,6,4,4,1,2),\quad
 (6,6,4,4,2,1).                                             \tag{PXC118f}
\end{equation}
Thus every extreme cover fails for every complete certificate.  There is no
common staged direction: after fixing \(r,h\), equality on the union of the
four supports has zero kernel.  The last two covers nevertheless retain the
PXC104 reciprocal pair.  Their directions
\[
 (0,0,-1,0,0,1),\qquad (0,0,-1,0,1,0)
\]
are cut respectively by \(rhaj\) and \(rhak\), each a positive row of the
opposite cover.  Adding the appropriate cross blocker repairs the selector
back to \(z\), providing a fail-open control.

Consequently literal canonicality in the reciprocal branch is false.  The
PXC118 game has four qualifying extreme covers, whereas PXC104 has three;
this count is invariant under player relabeling.  Both are dimension-minimal
by the five-player theorem (PXC100).  Together with (PXC116), this exhausts
the two proposed literal-primitive claims negatively: each PXC114 mechanism
has more than one smallest exact incidence realization.  It does not replace
the mechanism-level PXC114 decomposition by a finite incidence catalogue.

The primary and independently formulated exact audits are:
\begin{verbatim}
python3 verifiers/divergence/verify_pxc115_minor_operation_no_go.py \
  --output verifiers/divergence/pxc115_minor_operation_no_go.json
/Users/maxf/miniforge3/bin/python3 \
  verifiers/divergence/verify_pxc115_minor_operation_no_go_independent.py \
  --output \
  verifiers/divergence/pxc115_minor_operation_no_go_independent.json
python3 verifiers/divergence/verify_pxc118_second_reciprocal_primitive.py \
  --output verifiers/divergence/pxc118_second_reciprocal_primitive.json
/Users/maxf/miniforge3/bin/python3 \
  verifiers/divergence/verify_pxc118_second_reciprocal_primitive_independent.py \
  --output \
  verifiers/divergence/pxc118_second_reciprocal_primitive_independent.json
\end{verbatim}
The primary audit exhausts all complete certificates and all extreme covers
in (PXC115)--(PXC116), while the PXC118 pair exhausts all eight subsets of
the proper \(x\)-tight menu, both complete classes, all four extreme covers,
and all eight certificate--cover lifts.  The independent audits use
standalone rational active sets; the PXC115 audit additionally uses exact Z3
checks for lower bounds and attainment of every mapped coalition.  They
import no project game, simplex, selector, or cover implementation.

The attempt to price only the two failure mechanisms of (PXC114) has a
necessary correction.  For a fixed complete certificate there are three,
not two, mutually exclusive branches: some qualifying extreme cover is
faithful; every cover fails with a common staged descent; or every cover
fails and a reciprocal pair exists.  The last two are (PXC114), while the
first is its complement.  This matters quantitatively.  The
actual-top-fixed seven-player spectral control has post-top spectral deficit
(T/24), twelve qualifying extreme covers, and every one is faithful.  Thus
an overlap price supported only on outside-union or cross-cover blockers is
identically zero on a branch with positive demand.  Exact reconstruction of
that game and the optimized-top control, full cover exhaustion, and staged
tangent replay are recorded in the mechanism-price audit below.  This is a
falsification of the blocker-only architecture, not a finite proof of the
overlap bound.

There is already a dimension-free labelled cell, containing the faithful
actual-top control, on which the missing price has a closed form.  Let
\[
 N=\{u,v\}\mathbin{\dot\cup}G\mathbin{\dot\cup}\{c\}
      \mathbin{\dot\cup}Q,\qquad |G|=g\ge1,\quad |Q|=s-1\ge1,
 \qquad n=s+g+2\ge6.
\]
Suppose the labelled selector profiles are
\[
 x=(C,D,B^g,A^s),\qquad
 z=(m,m,\ell^g,p,q^{s-1}),
\]
where the two first coordinates belong to \(u,v\), the next \(g\) to
\(G\), the next to \(c\), and the last \(s-1\) to \(Q\).  Assume the
displayed labels really select the coordinatewise minima:
\[
 C,D\ge m,\qquad \ell\ge B,\qquad p\ge A\ge q\ge0,
 \qquad B\ge A,\qquad m\ge\ell,
\]
and suppose that, for some \(q_0\in Q\), the two rows
\(\{u,v,c\}\) and \(\{c,q_0\}\) are tight at both selections.  Their
equalities are
\[
 C+D+A=2m+p,\qquad 2A=p+q.                    \tag{PXC119a}
\]
Put
\[
 \delta=A-q,\qquad
 \mathsf D_{s,g}=n+1+\frac{2(s-1)}g.
\]
Then
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{\quad
 O-\left(1-\frac{s}{\mathsf D_{s,g}}\right)T
 =\frac{s}{\mathsf D_{s,g}}
   \left[2(m-\ell)+(g+2)(B-A)+nq\right]\ge0.
 \quad}                                                \tag{PXC119b}
\end{equation}
Moreover
\begin{equation}
 1-\frac{s}{\mathsf D_{s,g}}
 \ge \frac{n}{\lfloor n^2/4\rfloor},\qquad n\ge6.     \tag{PXC119c}
\end{equation}
Thus the hybrid-overlap inequality holds throughout this labelled
two-top ladder cell in every dimension.

Here is the conventional proof.  The second equality in (PXC119a) gives
\(p=A+\delta\).  Subtracting the two efficiency equations after using both
common rows gives
\[
 \ell=B+\frac{s-1}{g}\delta,\qquad
 O=T-s\delta.
\]
The first efficiency equation and \(C+D=2m+\delta\) then give the exact
price identity
\[
 T-\mathsf D_{s,g}\delta
 =2(m-\ell)+(g+2)(B-A)+nq\ge0,
\]
which is (PXC119b).  No overlap term is being renamed as payment: the right
side consists only of two payoff-stratum gaps and the nonnegative bottom
level, while (PXC119a) comes from the two explicitly common tight rows.

For completeness, put \(F=\lfloor n^2/4\rfloor\) and
\(g=n-s-2\).  After clearing the positive denominator,
(PXC119c) is equivalent to
\[
 (F-n)[g(n-1)+2(n-3)]-Fg(n-g-2)\ge0.                 \tag{PXC119d}
\]
If \(n=2r\), the left side is
\[
 r\{rg^2-(3r-2)g+4r^2-14r+12\}.
\]
This convex integer quadratic has its minimum at \(g=1\), where its
braced value is \(4r^2-16r+14\ge0\) for \(r\ge3\).  If \(n=2r+1\), the
quadratic has vertex strictly between \(1\) and \(3/2\), so again its
integer minimum is at \(g=1\), where it is
\(4r^3-10r^2+4\ge0\) for \(r\ge3\).  This proves (PXC119c).

The actual-top-fixed control has \((s,g)=(3,2)\) and
\(\mathsf D_{s,g}=10\), so (PXC119b) gives the exact branch floor \(7T/10\),
strictly above \(7T/12\).  The branch minimizer has \(q=0\), \(B=A\), and
\(m=\ell\), making every displayed price zero.  Deleting only the order
\(m\ge\ell\) admits an exact affine fail-open point with overlap \(4T/7\),
so the top-order price is load-bearing.

The exact audits are:
\begin{verbatim}
python3 verifiers/divergence/verify_pxc119_faithful_ladder_price.py \
  --output verifiers/divergence/pxc119_faithful_ladder_price.json
python3 \
  verifiers/divergence/verify_pxc119_faithful_ladder_price_independent.py \
  --output \
  verifiers/divergence/pxc119_faithful_ladder_price_independent.json
(
  cd verifiers/divergence/search_archive
  python3 audit_pxc119_spectral_deficit_mechanisms.py \
    --case optimized_label_free_spectral_failure \
    --case actual_top_fixed_failure \
    --output pxc119_spectral_deficit_mechanisms.json
)
\end{verbatim}
The first verifier checks the symbolic slack formula, the parity numerator
through (n=256), the named faithful control, its equality branch point,
and the fail-open point.  The second uses a separate formal-row-span
encoding and independent rational spanning samples.  These computations
audit the algebra; the displayed elimination and parity argument are the
dimension-free proof.  The theorem is a whole labelled profile cell, not a
proof for every faithful cover and not yet a common- or reciprocal-branch
price theorem.

The controls point to a sharper open architecture.  With \(B_H\) the
actual-top spectral bound and
\(D_H=(nT/\lfloor n^2/4\rfloor-B_H)_+\), ask whether
\[
 D_H>0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \text{for the fixed complete }{\cal C}_x,
 \text{ some qualifying extreme top cover is faithful}.       \tag{PXC120?}
\]
If true, its contrapositive makes the incremental common and reciprocal
prices zero: universal cover failure for that certificate would already
imply that \(B_H\) meets
the hybrid target.  This is not a theorem here.  The exact diagnostic finds
faithful qualifying covers for both fixed labelled controls with positive
post-top demand: the actual-top-fixed control has demand \(T/24\), while the
top-and-bottom-fixed control has demand \(T/42\) and a basic cover on rows
\((6,49,120)\) with weights \(1/2\).  In the opposite direction, the common
PXC101/PXC116 and reciprocal PXC104/PXC118 controls all have \(D_H=0\), and
none of the 374 archived six-player pairs has positive \(D_H\).  These are
finite falsification checks only; the next construction gives the omitted
positive-demand universal-failure test and refutes the implication.

The zero-demand evidence for two canonical obstruction families is in fact
parametric.  In the PXC101 notation, let \(k\ge2\), \(n=k+4\), and put
\[
 u=\frac{q+J+(k-1)K}{k+1},\qquad
 p=\frac{T-(k+2)u}{2}.
\]
The chamber inequalities give
\[
 2(k+1)(p-M)=(q-J)+(k-1)(q-K)>0,
\]
so the actual-top spectral expression is
\[
 B_H=2M+2u+\min\{u,J\}+(k-1)K.                    \tag{PXC121a}
\]
For \(2\le k\le5\), put \(c_0=(k+2)/(2(k+1))\).  Direct elimination gives
\begin{equation}
\begin{split}
2(k+1)(B_H-c_0T)
={}&2k(M-q)-(k-2)J+(k-1)(k+4)K\\
 &+2(k+1)\min\{u,J\}.
\end{split}
\tag{PXC121b}
\end{equation}
If the minimum is \(J\), the right side is positive.  If it is \(u\), it
reduces to
\[
 2k(M-q)+2q+(4-k)J+(k-1)(k+6)K>0;
\]
for \(k=5\), use \(2q-J>0\).  Thus \(B_H\ge c_0T\), and
\(c_0\ge n/\lfloor n^2/4\rfloor\).  For \(k\ge6\), the chamber gives
\(J<(k+2)q/(2k+1)\), while
\[
 9B_H-4T=10M-8q-4J+18u+5(k-1)K+9\min\{u,J\}>0.   \tag{PXC121c}
\]
Indeed its first four terms exceed
\([18/(k+1)-6/(2k+1)]q>0\).  Since \(n\ge10\) then implies
\(n/\lfloor n^2/4\rfloor\le2/5<4/9\), the entire PXC101 common
chamber has \(D_H=0\).

The PXC106 reciprocal family is also closed throughout its full chamber.  In
its notation \(m=p+q\ge2\), \(4<A<6\), and
\[
 B_H=12+m+2\frac{A+m}{m+1},\qquad T=16+A+m.
\]
Clearing denominators gives
\[
 \frac{B_H}{T}>\frac{m+14}{m+22}\ge\frac23
 \ge\frac{m+4}{\lfloor(m+4)^2/4\rfloor}.            \tag{PXC121d}
\]
The numerator of the first strict inequality is
\((6-A)m^2+(88-13A)m+40+30A>0\).  Thus the complete PXC106
reciprocal family also has \(D_H=0\).  These are conventional spectral
closures of two whole obstruction chambers, not the unrestricted PXC120
implication.

That implication is false.  More generally, one affine common-obstruction
family contains both the old PXC116 control and a positive-demand falsifier.
Let \(t\ge1\), \(n=t+5\), and
\[
 N=\{r,h,a,b,j\}\mathbin{\dot\cup}K,\qquad |K|=t.
\]
Choose rational parameters satisfying
\[
 0<A,\quad 3A<B,\quad
 B+\frac{A}{t+1}<K_0<B+\frac{2A}{t},\quad K_0<M,
 \qquad C=A+(t+1)B-tK_0.                            \tag{PXC122a}
\]
Then \(2A<B\) and \(B-A<C<K_0<M\); the relative order of \(B\) and
\(C\) is deliberately unrestricted.  Put
\begin{equation}
\begin{split}
T&=2M+3A+(t+1)B,\\
x&=(M+A/2,M+A/2,A,B,A,B^t),\\
z&=(M,M,0,C,2A,K_0^t).
\end{split}
\tag{PXC122b}
\end{equation}
Define \(v\) as the lower envelope of the nonnegative total-\(T\) polytope
\[
 y(rh)\ge2M,\quad y(rhj)\ge2M+2A,\quad
 y(rhaK)\ge2M+tK_0,\quad
 y(rhbK)\ge2M+A+(t+1)B.                             \tag{PXC122c}
\]
This game is exact by construction and singleton-normalized: all mass on
\(h\) zeros \(r\), and all mass on \(r\) zeros every other coordinate.
The only proper \(x\)-tight rows are \(rhj,rhbK\).  Their complements give
\[
 y_a+y_j\le2A,\qquad y_a+y_b+y(K)\le A+(t+1)B.
\]
The Lmin floor is therefore \(A\), pinning \(a,j\); the next floor is
\(B\), pinning \(b,K\); efficiency then equalizes the tops.  Each row is
indispensable, so
\[
                 {\cal C}_x=\{rhj,rhbK\}.            \tag{PXC122d}
\]
Indeed, retaining only \(rhj\) leaves the first-floor average
\([A+(t+1)B]/(t+2)>A\) on \(a,b,K\).  Retaining only \(rhbK\) still pins
\(a,j\) at \(A\), but the remaining \(t+3\) coordinates have average
\([2M+A+(t+1)B]/(t+3)>B\).  Thus neither proper subset has Lmin \(x\).
At \(x\), the only active defining rows are precisely \(rhj,rhbK\);
the two-normal KKT cone contains no other proper zero--one objective.
Consequently they are the only proper \(x\)-tight rows and (PXC122d) is
the unique complete proper-row certificate.

For Lmax, \(rh\) pins \(r=h=M\).  The common rows and efficiency force
\(a=0,j=2A\) and \(b+y(K)=C+tK_0\).  The row \(rhaK\) gives
\(y(K)\ge tK_0\); since \(K_0>C\), the next ceiling pins all of \(K\) at
\(K_0\), then \(b=C\).  This proves (PXC122b).

At the six coordinate types the proper \(z\)-tight menu is exactly
\[
 \{rh,a,rha,rhj,rhaj,rhaK,rhbK,rhabK\}.             \tag{PXC122e}
\]
All active normals are constant on \(K\), so no tight zero--one objective
uses a proper nonempty subset of that block.  If \(w_0,\ldots,w_7\) are the
weights on the displayed rows, equal outsider loads and top normalization
give
\begin{equation}
\begin{gathered}
 w_5=0,\quad w_3=\eta-w_4,\quad w_6=\eta-w_7,\quad
 \eta=w_1+w_2+w_4+w_7,\\
 w_0=\frac12-w_1-2w_2-w_4-w_7.
\end{gathered}
\tag{PXC122f}
\end{equation}
Hence the cover polytope is a four-axis simplex with exactly five extreme
supports
\[
 \{rh\},\quad\{rhj,rhabK\},\quad\{rhaj,rhbK\},\quad
 \{a,rhj,rhbK\},\quad\{rha,rhj,rhbK\}.             \tag{PXC122g}
\]
The blocker \(rhaK\) has zero coefficient in every cover.  Every cover lift
with (PXC122d) has upper selector
\[
 z'=(M,M,0,L,2A,L^t),\qquad L=B+\frac{A}{t+1},       \tag{PXC122h}
\]
so all fail.  To see this without invoking the selector code, the positive
cover identity pins \(r=h=M\).  The two retained certificate rows and
efficiency then force
\[
 a=0,\qquad j=2A,\qquad b+y(K)=A+(t+1)B=(t+1)L.
\]
After these coordinates are fixed, every row in every one of the five cover
supports is either redundant or repeats one of these displayed equalities;
none distinguishes \(b\) from a member of \(K\).  The next ceiling therefore
equalizes those \(t+1\) coordinates at \(L\), proving (PXC122h).
Their common direction has \(d_b=t\delta\) and
\(d_k=-\delta\) for \(k\in K\), where \(\delta=K_0-L>0\); the sole
negative tight row is \(rhaK\).  Thus this is a whole dimension-free
PXC114-common family.

It also has an explicit price.  Put
\(\beta=\min\{B,C\}\), \(\gamma=\max\{B,C\}\).  Opposite sorting of the
residual minimum multiset \(\{B^t,\beta,A,0\}\) and maximum multiset
\(\{A,2A,\gamma,K_0^t\}\) gives
\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c|c}
 &B_H&O&P_C=O-B_H\\ \hline
t=1&2M+4A&2M+A+B+\beta&(B-A)+(\beta-2A)\\
t\ge2&2M+4A+(t-2)B+\beta&2M+A+tB+\beta&(B-A)+(B-2A).
\end{array}                                             \tag{PXC123a}
\]
The two summands are the first two canonical rank-paired stratum rectangles.
Concretely, \(rhj\) pins \(x_a=x_j=A\) and \(z_j=2A\), while active
nonnegativity pins \(z_a=0\); \(rhbK\) pins the next Lmin block
\(x_b=x_k=B\), and the omitted blocker \(rhaK\) separates
\(z_k=K_0\) from \(z_b=C\).  Opposite rank pairing therefore matches two
\(B\)-level minima against the earlier maximum endpoints \(A,2A\), losing
exactly the rectangles \(B-A\) and \(B-2A\).  When \(t=1\), only one
\(B\)-minimum remains and the second lower endpoint is
\(\beta=\min\{B,C\}\), giving \(B-A\) and \(\beta-2A\).
Thus \(P_C\) is explicit tight-row, active-normal, and staged-stratum
currency, not overlap left unexplained.

If \(t=1\) and \(D_H>0\), then
\[
 P_C-D_H=\frac{2M+3\beta-B-3A}{3}
          >\frac{4B-6A}{3}>0.                         \tag{PXC123b}
\]
For \(t=2\),
\[
 12B_H-7T=10M+27A+12\beta-21B>B+15A>0.               \tag{PXC123c}
\]
For \(t\ge3\), the target coefficient is at most \(1/2\), while
\[
 2B_H-T=2M+5A+(t-5)B+2\beta>(t-1)B+3A>0.             \tag{PXC123d}
\]
Consequently \(P_C\ge D_H\) for every \(t\ge1\), and the hybrid-overlap
inequality holds throughout this open rational common-obstruction family in
every dimension.  The PXC116 point
\[
 (t,A,B,K_0,M)=(1,1/2,9/4,3,6)
\]
lies in the \(C<B\) chamber.  The point
\[
 (t,A,B,K_0,M)=(1,1,5,29/5,6)                        \tag{PXC123e}
\]
has \(T=25\), \(B_H=16\), \(D_H=2/3>0\), overlap \(23\), and every
qualifying cover fails.  It therefore falsifies PXC120 while satisfying the
main bound by the explicit price \(4+3=7\).  The new theorem prices one
entire all-dimensional common family; it is not the universal common-branch
theorem.

The apparent restriction \(3A<B\) in (PXC122a) is not a restriction on the
positive-demand part of this mechanism.  Fix \(t\ge1\) and replace that
chamber by the maximal natural open chamber
\begin{equation}
 0<A<B,\qquad
 B+\frac{A}{t+1}<K_0<B+\frac{2A}{t},\qquad
 \max\{K_0,2A\}<M,\qquad
 C=A+(t+1)B-tK_0.                                  \tag{PXC125a}
\end{equation}
Then \(B-A<C<K_0<M\).  The lower-envelope construction (PXC122c), its
selectors (PXC122b), the unique complete certificate (PXC122d), the typed
tight menu, and all five cover vertices remain unchanged.  Indeed the Lmin
argument uses only \(A<B\) and \(M>B\).  After \(r,h,a,j\) are fixed in the
Lmax argument, \(K_0>C\) pins the \(K\) block before \(b\), irrespective of
where the already fixed level \(2A\) lies.  The active-normal and cover
calculations are incidence calculations, so they are also unchanged when
payoff strata coarsen.  Finally \(K_0>B+A/(t+1)\) makes the common exchange
nonzero, while the upper inequality in (PXC125a) is precisely the
comparison \(x(rhaK)>z(rhaK)\).  Thus (PXC125a) is the full natural affine
chamber for this strict common obstruction.

There is a sharper demand-localization theorem on that chamber.  Remove the
two top players and let \(R_H\) denote the residual part of the actual-top
spectral bound.  The two residual multisets are
\[
 \{A,A,B^{t+1}\},\qquad \{0,2A,C,K_0^t\}.
\]
Counting the Fr\'echet intersection at each threshold gives
\begin{equation}
R_H=\begin{cases}
2A+\min\{A,C\}+(\min\{B,2A,C\}-A)_+,&t=1,\\[2mm]
(t+1)A+\min\{A,C\}+(t-2)(B-A)\\
\qquad +(\min\{B,2A\}-A)_+ +(\min\{B,C\}-A)_+,&t\ge2.
\end{cases}                                             \tag{PXC125b}
\end{equation}
In particular \(B_H=2M+R_H\).  Put \(\beta=\min\{B,C\}\) and predeclare
the following price:
\begin{equation}
 P_C=\begin{cases}
 (B-A)+(\beta-2A)_+,&t=1,\\
 (B-A)+(B-2A)_+,&t\ge2.
 \end{cases}                                            \tag{PXC125c}
\end{equation}
This is not the complete canonical rectangle identity.  For \(t=1\), pair
the \(K\) coordinate with \(a\), and, when nonempty, pair \(b\) with \(j\).
For \(t\ge2\), pair two distinct \(K\) coordinates with \(a,j\).  Their high
endpoints are respectively \(B,\beta\) or \(B,B\), and their low endpoints
are \(A,2A\).  The pairs are disjoint, so (PXC125c) is a predeclared
subpacking and
\begin{equation}
                       P_C\le O-B_H.                 \tag{PXC125d}
\end{equation}

The spectral demand now localizes completely.  If \(t\ge3\), (PXC125b)
gives \(R_H\ge3A+(t-2)B\), and hence
\[
 2B_H-T\ge2M+3A+(t-5)B>0.
\]
Since \(n=t+5\ge8\), the target coefficient is at most \(1/2\).  If \(t=2\),
write
\[
 u=\min\{A,C\},\quad
 p=(\min\{B,2A\}-A)_+,\quad
 q=(\min\{B,C\}-A)_+,
 \qquad R_H=3A+u+p+q.
\]
When \(A\le B/2\), the comparison \(C>B-A\) gives
\(R_H>B+3A\).  When \(A>B/2\), \(p=B-A\) gives
\(R_H\ge B+2A\).  Using \(M>K_0>B\) in the two cases yields respectively
\begin{equation}
 12B_H-7T>B+15A>0,\qquad
 12B_H-7T>B+3A>0.                                  \tag{PXC125e}
\end{equation}
Thus seven players also need no incremental price.

It remains to inspect \(t=1\).  The elementary identity
\[
 \min\{A,C\}+(\min\{B,2A,C\}-A)_+
 =\min\{\beta,2A\}
\]
turns (PXC125b) into
\[
 3B_H-2T=2M-4B+3\min\{\beta,2A\}.
\]
If \(A\ge B/3\), this is positive.  For \(C\le B\), substitute
\(M>K_0=A+2B-C\) and split at \(C=2A\); the two lower bounds are
\(2A+C\) and \(8A-2C\).  For \(C\ge B\), use \(M>K_0>B\) and
\(\min\{B,2A\}\ge2B/3\).  Hence \(B_H\ge2T/3\) throughout that subchamber.

Consequently positive post-top demand forces
\begin{equation}
                 D_H>0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
                 t=1\quad\hbox{and}\quad B>3A.       \tag{PXC125f}
\end{equation}
But then \(C>B-A>2A\), so (PXC125c) reduces to the two PXC123 rectangles.
Whenever the demand is positive,
\begin{equation}
 P_C-D_H
 =\frac{2M+3\beta-B-3A}{3}
 >\frac{4B-6A}{3}>0.                                \tag{PXC125g}
\end{equation}
Equations (PXC125d)--(PXC125g) prove that the two-rung price covers every
positive-demand point in the maximal affine common-obstruction chamber.
This is a genuine mechanism-to-price handover: blocker comparison moves all
dimensions above six to the spectral branch, and the only remaining price
case has two explicitly identified rectangles.  It is still not a proof
that every PXC114 common obstruction contains this ladder incidence.

The incidence actually used by that price has a representation-free
formulation which avoids the invalid player-minor operations of (PXC115).
Fix the actual top block $H$, put $R=N\setminus H$, and write

\[
 a_i=\min\{x_i,z_i\},\qquad b_i=\max\{x_i,z_i\}\quad(i\in R).
\]

For two proper coalitions $C,Q$, partition $R$ into the four Boolean
incidence cells

\[
 V_{\epsilon\delta}=\{i\in R:
       \one_C(i)=\epsilon,\ \one_Q(i)=\delta\},
       \qquad \epsilon,\delta\in\{0,1\}.
\]

Choose distinct players
$u_{11}\in V_{11},u_{01}\in V_{01},u_{10}\in V_{10},u_{00}\in V_{00}$
and define the Boolean-square price

\begin{equation}
 P_{\square}(C,Q;u)
 =(a_{u_{11}}-b_{u_{01}})_+
  +(a_{u_{10}}-b_{u_{00}})_+.
                                                               \tag{PXC126a}
\end{equation}

Then, without any game-theoretic hypothesis,

\begin{equation}
                  P_{\square}(C,Q;u)\le O-B_H.       \tag{PXC126b}
\end{equation}

Indeed at every threshold $s$, an active summand in (PXC126a) uses one
player high in both allocations and one different player low in both.
The two pairs are vertex-disjoint.  Their active number is therefore at
most both the high--high count and the low--low count on $R$, hence at
most their minimum.  Integrating in $s$ and using the layer identity (LC)
proves (PXC126b).  This is a predeclared two-rectangle subpacking, not the
complete carrier identity (CS).

In the positive-demand case of (PXC125), where (PXC125f) forces $t=1$,
take $C=rhbK$, $Q=rhaK$, and choose

\[
 u_{11}=k\in K,\qquad u_{01}=a,\qquad
 u_{10}=b,\qquad u_{00}=j.
\]

The two lengths in (PXC126a) are exactly
$B-A$ and $(\min\{B,C\}-2A)_+$.  Thus the positive-demand PXC125 price is
the Boolean square cut out by one retained Lmin row and the forced-zero Lmax
blocker.  When $t\ge2$, this same choice remains a valid subpacking but is
smaller than the two-distinct-$K$ price in (PXC125c); that branch already has
$D_H=0$, so no incremental price is required.
No restriction, contraction, amalgamation, or literal incidence minor is
being asserted.

This gives a falsifiable next statement rather than a new conclusion about
all common obstructions.  In a positive-demand PXC114 common branch, maximize
$P_{\square}$ over retained common $x$-tight rows $C$, outside-union
blockers $Q$ occurring in basic staged Lmax certificates, and the four
incidence-cell choices above.  The proposed square-extraction implication is

\begin{equation}
 D_H>0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \max P_{\square}\ge D_H.                            \tag{PXC126?}
\end{equation}

Its exact negation is a common obstruction with positive demand and
$P_{\square}<D_H$ for every such row, blocker, and cell choice.  Such a
witness would not refute the hybrid-overlap inequality: it would send the
proof to the mixed singleton-witness packet (MCP)--(MWP).  Accordingly
(PXC126?) is an extraction target, not a theorem claimed here.

The exact primary and independent audits are:
\begin{verbatim}
python3 verify_pxc121_obstruction_spectral_closure.py \
  --output pxc121_obstruction_spectral_closure.json
python3 verify_pxc121_obstruction_spectral_closure_independent.py \
  --output pxc121_obstruction_spectral_closure_independent.json
python3 verify_pxc122_positive_common_price.py \
  --output pxc122_positive_common_price.json
python3 verify_pxc122_positive_common_price_independent.py \
  --output pxc122_positive_common_price_independent.json
python3 verify_pxc123_dimension_free_common_price.py \
  --output pxc123_dimension_free_common_price.json
python3 verify_pxc123_dimension_free_common_price_independent.py \
  --output pxc123_dimension_free_common_price_independent.json
python3 verify_pxc125_common_demand_localization.py \
  --output pxc125_common_demand_localization.json
python3 verify_pxc125_common_demand_localization_independent.py \
  --output pxc125_common_demand_localization_independent.json
python3 verify_pxc126_boolean_square_price.py \
  --output pxc126_boolean_square_price.json
python3 verify_pxc126_boolean_square_price_independent.py \
  --output pxc126_boolean_square_price_independent.json
\end{verbatim}
The PXC122 independent audit reconstructs all coalition minima and both
selectors by standalone exact active-set enumeration.  The PXC123 primary
audit exhausts complete certificates, tight menus, covers, and reduced
selectors through \(n=9\); its independent companion separately checks the
typed incidence algebra and exact price identities through \(t=64\).  The
PXC125 primary audit adds exact-game replays outside the old \(3A<B\)
chamber and checks 240 rational layer profiles.  Its standalone companion
integrates the threshold counts directly on 1,536 rational profiles through
\(t=64\), and includes a fail-open point with positive seven-player demand
after the blocker comparison is removed.  The displayed elimination is the
all-dimensional proof.  The PXC126 primary audit checks the Boolean-square
formula on 96 rational profiles and the PXC125 specialization at four exact
chamber points.  Its standalone companion exhausts 240,000 four-cell endpoint
assignments and reproduces the threshold integral independently; both retain
a control on which reusing the same endpoints twice breaks the bound.  The
threshold-counting argument is the dimension-free proof of (PXC126b), while
(PXC126?) remains open.  No novelty claim is made before specialist review.

The certificate-dependence falsifier, rank-potential audit, and their
independent replays are:
\begin{verbatim}
python3 verify_pxc112_certificate_dependence.py \
  --output pxc112_certificate_dependence.json
python3 verify_pxc112_certificate_dependence_independent.py \
  --output pxc112_certificate_dependence_independent.json
